Task-aligned guidance: (i) phone-level probes use shallow/mid SSL layers with k$\approx$200--500 or noise-aware local units; (ii) SLU/ASR favors text/segment-synchronous units and shared vocabularies; (iii) sentence-level modeling under tight budgets prefers moderately coarse segmentation with larger k, interleaved speech--text training with sufficient model capacity, and ultra-low-rate codebooks or multi-token combos for precision \cite{visser2025spokenlanguagemodelingdurationpenalizeds,gong2025xytokenizermitigatingsemanticacousticcon,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}.

\subsection{Rate and sequence management} \label{subsec:Rate and sequence management}
A hierarchical overview is provided in \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_0}: the left panel maps the strategy space balancing frame rates and decoder strength (highlighting the 12.5 Hz sweet spot, depth-trimmed hybrids, and ultra-low 6.25 Hz regimes with text-aware diffusion); the middle panel consolidates sequence compression and alignment mechanisms (projector pooling, CTC blank removal, acoustic BPE/unigram, alignment-aware connectors, and block-causal streaming); and the right panel codifies a standardized reporting protocol (rates and bitrate; pre/post-compression statistics; codec specifics; latency; and paired accuracy--perceptual metrics with language/BPE notes).

Reflecting the left panel, cadence, bitrate, and emission schedules are co-designed to meet wall-clock constraints without sacrificing semantics or perceptual quality \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}. Large-scale interleaved pretraining converges on 12.5 Hz as an accuracy--efficiency sweet spot; going lower risks content loss unless countered by stronger decoders \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Hybrids at a fixed 12.5 Hz vary acoustic depth per frame (12.5--112.5 tps) and, with latent diffusion, retain low WER and flat DNSMOS as depth decreases, outperforming a 100 tps SoundStream baseline at 50 tps on MUSHRA \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Ultra-low-rate TaDiCodec (6.25 Hz; few diffusion steps) remains intelligible across languages under text-aware decoders \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

Echoing the middle panel, temporal compression without changing discretization uses projector pooling for continuous features ($\approx$4--5$\times$ with Whisper) and CTC blank removal for label-synchronous streams, shrinking sequences to $\approx$14.55%, stabilizing decoder-only training, and integrating with label-synchronous beam search \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Token-string compression via acoustic BPE/unigram improves throughput and intelligibility but should be reported alongside bitrate/quality \cite{guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview}. Alignment-aware connectors that map n frames to m text-aligned tokens reduce latency and prioritize semantics, and cross-modal harmonization (matched embeddings and coupled masking) balances audio/video rates \cite{guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Streaming stacks benefit from 12.5 Hz semantic inputs with block-causal schedules; continuous conditioning can rely on projector pooling or CTC compression to bound time-to-first-token \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. Two robust low-bitrate strategies dominate: moderate frame rates ($\approx$12.5 Hz) with reduced per-frame depth and strong decoders, or ultra-low rates ($\approx$6.25 Hz) with text-aware diffusion/flow \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

Aligned with the right panel, for fair comparison, report frame/token rates; bitrate; tokens/s pre/post compression (BPE/unigram, CTC, projector pooling) with ratios; codec specifics (RVQ depth/code sizes, semantic conditioning); streaming/duplex latency; and pair WER/CER with perceptual metrics, noting language-specific frame-rate choices and acoustic-BPE settings and their impact on semantic fidelity \cite{li2024effectivenessacousticbpedecoderonlytts,ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_0}
\subsection{Disentanglement and mixed-token designs} \label{subsec:Disentanglement and mixed-token designs}
Disentanglement routes low-entropy content to the LM while prosody, timbre, affect, and non-verbal cues are handled by orthogonal controls, aligning with multi-stream capability frameworks \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview}. RVQ codecs naturally factorize: SpeechTokenizer distills RVQ-1 to linguistics (high MI with text; competitive ASR) and reserves RVQ-2:8 for prosody/timbre, enabling style editing by swapping residual levels \cite{zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar}. Two-branch designs such as EMOVA combine discrete semantics with a compact prototype style codebook predicted by an LLM (gender $\times$ emotion $\times$ pitch), improving controllability and outperforming entangled HuBERT units in vision--language and ASR \cite{chen2025emovaempoweringlanguagemodelsseehearspea}. NAST's global--local split uses a single global residual vector (speaker) plus local discrete units (content), reducing speaker leakage \cite{messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage}.

Hybrid interfaces condition acoustics on semantics: DiffSoundStream applies FiLM to disentangle content and residual detail, with WER/DNSMOS dropping without semantic conditioning at low rates; end-to-end S2ST combines HuBERT-like units (content) with EnCodec streams (timbre/prosody) and short prompts for identity; ultra-low-rate pipelines shift identity and long-range prosody into prompts/text-aware decoders, keeping discrete tokens generation-friendly; AV stacks use coupled masking to bias codes toward shared emotional content \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}.

Training signals enforce invariance and stability: speaker-perturbed swapped-prediction with F0/formant scaling yields speaker-invariant tokens and lower unit edit distance under noise/reverb/time-stretch; lexical bottlenecks via continuous embeddings improve ABX/position discriminability and generation; early-codebook semantic distillation increases LM predictability; factorized codecs allocate content, prosody, identity to disentangled subspaces at low bitrate; fixed windows typically match phoneme/syllable/word segmenters at the same duration \cite{chang2024dcspinspeakerinvariantspeechtokenizerspo,algayres2023generativespokenlanguagemodelbasedcontin,guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}. Editing/control interfaces mix discrete semantics with orthogonal style controls (prototype selection, residual-level swaps, short speaker prompts), and schedules align information to latency budgets: AR on semantics or early RVQ levels plus NAR refinement, with prompts/later channels preserving style; synchronized AV rates and coupled masking support parallel prosody/facial recovery without inflating content \cite{chen2025emovaempoweringlanguagemodelsseehearspea,zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Evaluate streams separately: content WER/CER and MI with text; speaker similarity/verification and leakage via speaker classification and ABX; and perceptual/distributional metrics under bitrate constraints \cite{zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview}.

\subsection{Continuous and codec-free interfaces} \label{subsec:Continuous and codec-free interfaces}
Codec-free interfaces pass spectrograms or continuous encoder embeddings to LLMs/decoders, avoiding quantization error and helping prosody/timbre retention at the cost of longer sequences that require projector compression, cross-attention gating, or label-synchronous selection \cite{guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview}. Thin adaptors between frozen encoders and LLMs (e.g., USM$\rightarrow$mT0 via a 2-layer Transformer; Whisper-large via a convolutional subsampler) validate a small modality gap for multilingual ASR, speech-conditioned reasoning, and empathetic dialogue \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. CTC blank removal yields label-synchronous continuous prompts that outperform convolutional downsampling, integrate with label-synchronous beam search, and markedly reduce attention cost for decoder-only ASR \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.

For generation, continuous latents replace RVQ stacks to retain high-frequency detail and support diffusion/flow training; SoundStream with a 24-d Gaussian latent at 50 Hz stabilizes diffusion via noise injection, range clipping, and unit-std normalization, decoding without discrete indices \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Scheduling remains crucial: CTC blank removal, shallow projectors/MLPs, cross-attention gating, wait--k, and similarity-aware thinning keep time-to-first-token low \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}. Bypassing quantization aids paralinguistics and robustness---Whisper features with a small adaptor suffice for affect understanding and instruction following; alignment-aware connectors that emit vectors directly in the LLM space improve semantic retention over discrete units \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech}. Pragmatically, use continuous inputs for perception/reasoning and predict discrete indices or mels for synthesis to bound decoding latency; continuous lexical/syllabic embeddings offer compact semantics without codebooks \cite{wang2024c3llmconditionalmultimodalcontentgenerat,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.

\subsection{Representative methods and empirical findings} \label{subsec:Representative methods and empirical findings}
Representative families include RVQ codecs (EnCodec/SoundStream/DAC), semantic units from SSL/supervised encoders (HuBERT/Whisper, syllabic), and hybrid/model-aligned tokenizers (ALMTokenizer, RepCodec, HASRD, XY Tokenizer, LM-SPT, universal tokens) \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. RVQ codecs with strong decoders (e.g., DAC+Vocos) deliver high PESQ/STOI/MCD, low WER, and high NISQA; AR-first/NAR-refine schedules achieve $\approx$3--5% WER with reduced latency \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}. DM-Codec loads semantics into early residual channels and improves LibriSpeech WER/WIL and ViSQOL/MOS at 0.75--1.5 kbps; token LMs on DM-Codec indices are competitive for zero-shot TTS \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}. DiffSoundStream conditions latent diffusion on semantic/acoustic tokens and renders via continuous-latent SoundStream, improving WER/DNSMOS over semantic-conditioned SoundStream and surpassing a 100 tps baseline at 50 tps MUSHRA \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. TaDiCodec pushes to 6.25 Hz with BSQ and text-aware diffusion/flow, matching or surpassing higher-bitrate baselines across languages (e.g., SeedTTS test-en WER 2.73, SIM 0.69, UTMOS 3.73) \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

Discrete semantic units remain competitive at sub-kbps budgets: adding ASR objectives during tokenizer training improves invariance/compressibility/linguistic structure, while very large k-means codebooks risk under-utilization and weak compressibility \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. SyllableLM at 6.25 Hz (8192 units) reaches LibriSpeech WER 7.0/CER 3.2 with 90--300M LMs and strong sWUGGY/sBLIMP/StoryCloze \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}. Interleaved speech--text pretraining corroborates 12.5 Hz as a robust operating point for SLM conditioning and streaming \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Large-scale spoken dialogue (dGSLM on J-CHAT) uses HuBERT+k=1000 tokens with HiFi-GAN and XVector prompts at low bitrate \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Continuous lexical embeddings (tGSLM) match or surpass discrete GSLM diversity with $\approx$5$\times$ memory savings and strong zero-shot ABX/sWUGGY/sBLIMP/NED via multi-step heads \cite{algayres2023generativespokenlanguagemodelbasedcontin}. Tokenized TTS inside SLMs supports barge-in/duplex via interruption markers \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Hybrid/model-aligned tokenizers reduce redundancy and align schedules: AR semantic paths plus NAR EnCodec refinement preserve style with short prompts and yield competitive ASR-BLEU on English$\leftrightarrow$Spanish S2ST; FiLM-conditioned hybrids halve tokens/s at equal or better quality by leaning on stronger decoders \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Families such as ALMTokenizer, RepCodec, HASRD, XY Tokenizer, and LM-SPT emphasize early-channel semantic supervision, later-channel timbre/prosody, and text-synchronized tokens for editing/parallel generation, aided by PAST-style prompt-aware encoders \cite{guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview}. Multimodal discrete pretraining (audio+face) improves unimodal reconstruction and downstream emotion without burdening content channels \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Efficiency regularities: masked-parallel generation with oracle semantics reveals decoder quality more than codec choice; AR+NAR schedules consistently reduce WER/latency with high-fidelity codecs; 12.5 Hz semantics lighten context and support block-causal streaming; syllabic and 6.25 Hz streams need stronger decoders but preserve intelligibility/similarity with text-aware diffusion/flow. IndicSUPERB and BabySLM provide cross-lingual and developmental probes to contextualize capacity \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,lavechin2023babyslmlanguageacquisitionfriendlybenchm,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. The practical envelope emerges: kbps codecs (DAC/DM-Codec) lead fidelity; mid/low-rate 12.5 Hz semantics and hybrids deliver competitive WER/MOS with streaming; at ultra-low rate, BSQ tokens plus text-aware diffusion/flow close the reconstruction--generation gap.

\subsection{Comparative evaluation axes and open issues} \label{subsec:Comparative evaluation axes and open issues}
Capacity-controlled comparisons should expose reconstruction quality, semantic fidelity, bitrate/sequence length, downstream SLM performance, robustness, cross-language transfer, codebook health, and generation scheduling. DASB documents a performance gap between discrete tokens and continuous SSL features across tasks, motivating harmonized reporting; STAB provides fast intrinsic diagnostics correlating with downstream behavior \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. A practical triage combines IndicSUPERB (12 Indic languages; ASR, speaker verification/identification, LID, QbE, KWS), STAB metrics, and fastabx ABX discriminability \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.

Recent advances span LM-SPT (ASR-aligned semantic tokens at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz), PAST (phonetic-aware, streamable), SpeechRAG (direct audio retrieval into a frozen LLM), and SLM bridges that align frozen speech/text foundations with $\approx$1% parameters, improving ASR/AST and zero-shot instruction following \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Transparent reporting should cover: interface bitrate/rate/channels/utilization (tokens/s, kbps, codebook count/size, perplexity, per-channel information balance, predicted channels) \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat}; sequence length and token-string compression (acoustic BPE often 2--2.4$\times$) with language-specific frame rates \cite{li2024effectivenessacousticbpedecoderonlytts,shen2024acousticbpespeechgenerationdiscretetoken,gong2025xytokenizermitigatingsemanticacousticcon,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}; semantic fidelity and abstraction via resynthesis WER/CER, LM perplexity, n-gram predictability, ABX, PNMI/CNMI, and segmentation/vocabulary ablations \cite{chang2024dcspinspeakerinvariantspeechtokenizerspo,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}; reconstruction/perception via PESQ/STOI/ViSQOL/MCD/FAD and human CMOS/MOS/MUSHRA plus SALMon-style attribute consistency, intelligibility, and similarity \cite{ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom,ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.

Track codebook health (utilization, entropy/perplexity, dead-codes/collapse, temporal drift), RVQ slice--full consistency, and correlate with IndicSUPERB outcomes; quantify robustness (noise/channel/reverb), redundancy (unit edit distance/context fragmentation), and compressibility (Huffman/BPE/de-dup) with STAB, including TER-based prosody sensitivity \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. Downstream comparisons should match bit/token rate, decoder backbones, compute, and training data; emphasize cross-language transfer and retrieval diagnostics for homophones and long-range context \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Compute/latency reporting---parameters, training FLOPs, inference RTF, auxiliary-branch/scheduling latency---should accompany intrinsic triage (STAB) and DASB-style rankings under constrained budgets \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.

Open issues:
- Discrete--continuous gaps at matched bitrate persist on DASB; continuous interfaces remain stronger on several tasks \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark}.
- No single best segmentation--vocabulary pair across sWUGGY/sBLIMP/ProsAudit/StoryCloze; mixture-of-granularities and adaptive tokenization are promising \cite{kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}.
- Prosody/non-speech coverage is fragile at low rates; add TER, rhythm/duration, spontaneous/multilingual tests and document de-dup/smoothing effects; very coarse units risk rhythm loss and vocoder dependence \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}.
- Channelization vs LM difficulty: more channels aid reconstruction but hurt predictability; standards for channel-aware evaluation/scheduling are needed \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat}.
- Stability across languages/speakers/conditions is challenging; deeper RVQ layers show lower slice--full consistency, complicating streaming/editing \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Large-vocabulary/multilingual tokenization lacks stable recipes; extend STAB's utilization/entropy normalization to broader, multilingual settings \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
- Retrieval over speech with homophone robustness and long-window modeling is underexplored \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Extreme-compression validity requires standardized human MOS/CMOS and a reconstruction--generation gap metric (e.g., $-$16.5% English WER gap in TaDiCodec) \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

A concise reporting template should state dataset scope (e.g., IndicSUPERB's 1,684-hour Kathbath across 12 languages), task portfolio, tokenizer diagnostics and correlations (STAB), LM-aware choices (e.g., LAST/PAST/LM-SPT/dMel), interface descriptors (rate/bitrate/channels/k and usage), reconstruction/perception metrics (objective + human + SALMon), semantics/LM alignment (resynthesis WER/CER, PNMI/CNMI, LM perplexity), prosody/paralinguistics (TER, rhythm/duration, de-dup/smoothing), robustness/redundancy (STAB invariance/robustness/compressibility), downstream transfer (matched ASR/TTS/S2ST, retrieval diagnostics), and compute/latency (parameters, FLOPs, RTF), using fastabx and suite-level scaffolds for controlled comparisons \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.

















\section{Speech Encoders and Representations} \label{sec:Speech Encoders and Representations}

\subsection{Encoder families and layer-wise specialization} \label{subsec:Encoder families and layer-wise specialization}
Speech encoders expose two LM-facing interfaces: (i) continuous conditioning that projects frame- or segment-level embeddings, and (ii) unitization that discretizes layer-wise features into tokens. SSL families (HuBERT, wav2vec 2.0/XLS-R, WavLM, data2vec) and supervised encoders (Whisper) remain standard backbones; prompt-aware front ends (PAST) and prompt-composition frameworks (SpeechComposer/SpeechGen) specialize features to tasks, while hybrids mix continuous spectra with discrete units to trade context length against LM perplexity/NLL \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask,wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge}. Depth specialization is consistent: lower layers are acoustic, middle layers phonetic/lexical, and upper layers task-tuned; mid-layer k-means units ($\approx$100--1k) from HuBERT/wav2vec 2.0 work well for textless modeling and spoken LMs, with SpeechComposer favoring HuBERT-Base L6 \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}. Under coarse, syllabic cadences, data2vec2 with teacher-layer distillation improves reconstruction WER and segment-level invariance over HuBERT; robust preprocessing (LID, diarization, denoising) expands transfer to spontaneous multilingual dialogue \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe,nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.

For continuous interfaces, Whisper is a strong frozen front end: thin subsamplers or small projectors suffice for multilingual ASR, affect understanding, and empathetic dialogue; freezing Whisper-large-v3 and stage-wise tuning (encoder$\rightarrow$encoder+projector) stabilize alignment \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. For discrete interfaces, inserting pre-VQ pooling into Whisper yields stable $\approx$12.5 Hz tokens suitable for streamable SLMs \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Token invariance benefits from LM-aligned and phonetic-aware recipes (LAST/PAST) or training-free dMel, and from enrollment-conditioned selective normalization (SHuBERT), which improves SUPERB and multi-talker ASR with a frozen encoder \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,entry2025selectivehubertselfsupervisedpretraining}.

Encoder--bitrate co-design is pivotal: mid HuBERT layers (e.g., L6--L9) are strong mid-rate tokenizers; under aggressive compression, data2vec2 plus layer-wise distillation stabilizes syllabic/segmental tokens; Whisper-based discretizers operate robustly at $\approx$12.5 Hz \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask,baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. For selection/validation, combine fastabx for phonetic separability, IndicSUPERB for multilingual coverage, contamination-robust ASR testing, and SALMon for acoustic robustness; LM-SPT and PAST offer LM-aligned or phonetic-aware tokens at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Prefer mid-to-upper layers for semantic tokens (HuBERT L6--L9; wav2vec 2.0 mid), selected with LM-aware validation (LAST) and corroborated by SALMon; use SHuBERT/PAST under noise \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025selectivehubertselfsupervisedpretraining,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
  \item Under low rates, favor data2vec2 plus layer-wise distillation to pooled teacher means \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}.
  \item For multilingual/noisy inputs, condition on continuous Whisper features via shallow subsamplers; freeze then stage-wise tune; hybrids mixing continuous+discrete features control context and NLL \cite{verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy}.
  \item Discretizing Whisper: average pooling + VQ at $\approx$12.5 Hz yields streamable tokens \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.
  \item For spontaneous multilingual dialogue, combine language-specific SSL encoders with LID/diarization/denoising before clustering \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Fusion and representation selection} \label{subsec:Fusion and representation selection}
Fusion spans layers within and across encoders/modalities; selection decides which features to discretize or project. LM-aware tokenization (LAST) maps speech features toward a text-LM space, improving spoken LM and S2T; moderate segments and larger K boost zero-shot SLU while reducing data/runtime \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}. Middle layers consistently supply semantics: HASRD screens layers (e.g., L8) with a lightweight ASR head, learns convex fusions for reconstruction, and projects out semantics before residual acoustic quantization; transfers from E-Branchformer highlight phonetic emphasis near L8 and linguistic content near deeper layers, with k-means$\approx$500 balancing granularity and predictability \cite{hussein2025hasrdhierarchicalacousticsemanticreprese,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}. Probes show a final-layer dip for syntax in wav2vec 2.0/HuBERT, motivating mid-layer conditioning; informed selection via an MLP over time--layer features improves ASR/SID/ER/SE/TTS and interpretability \cite{shen2023wavesyntaxprobingspokenlanguagemodels,mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel}. Text-aligned TASTE queries last-layer keys and shallow values with text tokens to produce subword-synchronous features \cite{tseng2025tastetextalignedspeechtokenizationembedd}.

For continuous conditioning, aggregating/averaging layers improves coverage of content and paralinguistics; DESTA learns Whisper-layer weights and summarizes with Qformer/CNN adapters to trade fidelity for efficiency, and DM-Codec averages teacher layers (BERT/HuBERT) to supervise RVQ, outperforming single-layer targets \cite{lu2024destaenhancingspeechlanguagemodelsthroug,ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}. Heterogeneous fusion pairs supervised semantic robustness with SSL acoustic invariance using concatenation+projectors or gated cross-attention; discrete--continuous fusion aligns rates and sums embeddings in a shared LM space for stable parallel generation \cite{mei2025shnumultilingualconversationalspeechreco,lin2025dkusystemmultispeakerautomaticspeechreco,tan2025drvoiceparallelspeechtextvoiceconversati}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Tokenizers: select mid layers (e.g., HuBERT L8, k$\approx$500); for mixed tokens, learn convex layer fusions and project out semantics before residual quantization \cite{hussein2025hasrdhierarchicalacousticsemanticreprese,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
  \item Direct conditioning: aggregate/average layers then compress with Qformer queries or CNN subsampling to $\approx$60--1{,}500 states/utterance \cite{lu2024destaenhancingspeechlanguagemodelsthroug}.
  \item Robustness/targeting: fuse heterogeneous encoders via concatenation+projectors or gated cross-attention to balance semantic vs speaker cues \cite{mei2025shnumultilingualconversationalspeechreco,lin2025dkusystemmultispeakerautomaticspeechreco}.
  \item Text-aligned SLU/ASR: use attention-based, text-keyed fusion (TASTE) \cite{tseng2025tastetextalignedspeechtokenizationembedd}.
  \item Task-adaptive discrete units: adopt informed layer-selection modules that weight per-layer tokens \cite{mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Robustness, streaming, and deployment constraints} \label{subsec:Robustness, streaming, and deployment constraints}
Real-world SLMs need low latency and stability to noise, reverberation, speaker variation, and emotion across ASR/SID/LID/KWS/QbE. Noise-aware tokenization (NAST), invariance-promoting training, and broad evaluation (SALMon, IndicSUPERB) are central \cite{messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Streaming front ends trim look-ahead while preserving quality: BESTOW replaces bidirectional encoders with short-right-context or cache-aware FastConformers; CIF-based emission produces boundary-aware, label-synchronous prompts for low-latency ST/ASR \cite{chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem,deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc}. Tokenizers become streamable via block-causal attention and chunked schedules ($\approx$2 s), with training-free chunk-wise expansion keeping accuracy; duplex agents maintain continuous conditioning with compact encoders and noise injection for robustness \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,chang2024dcspinspeakerinvariantspeechtokenizerspo,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Domain shifts are pronounced: ABX gaps show wav2vec 2.0/XLS-R room/mic sensitivity and speaking-style drift; NAST and speaker-invariant tokenizers reduce unit edit distance under time-stretch/pitch/reverb/DNS noise \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,chang2024dcspinspeakerinvariantspeechtokenizerspo}. SHuBERT's dual-path Barlow Twins with enrollment conditioning improves multi-talker ASR and SI-SDRi; denoising objectives aid syllabic segmentation and duplex operation \cite{entry2025selectivehubertselfsupervisedpretraining,cho2025sylbersyllabicembeddingrepresentationspe,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. Latency reflects encoder context, token/frame rate, projector/LM attention, and scheduling; adjustable contexts, CIF prompts, encoder distillation (GenDistiller), and LM-aligned low-rate tokenizers (LM-SPT/PAST) reduce delay while preserving intelligibility \cite{chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem,deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc,gao2024gendistillerdistillingpretrainedlanguage,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Match causalization/context to budget: cache-aware FastConformer [70,13]$\rightarrow$[70,0] or bidirectional with 13-frame look-ahead \cite{chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem}.
  \item Align emission to scheduling: block-causal tokenization ($\approx$2 s chunks) or CIF; validate with STAB \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc}.
  \item Harden invariance with LM-aligned/noise-aware tokenizers, MUSAN/DNS augmentation, time-stretch/pitch-shift, and dual-path Barlow Twins; monitor ABX and Unit Edit Distance \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.
  \item Favor streamable tokenization/segmentation (LAST or dMel) for unified real-time ASR/TTS on shared LMs \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.
  \item Optimize for edge: distill encoders and operate at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz with RVQ or compact units to cut memory/latency \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Diagnosing representation quality} \label{subsec:Diagnosing representation quality}
Diagnostics should capture phonetic discriminability/invariance, leakage across linguistic/paralinguistic factors, and higher-level competence. As summarized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_1}, the suite is organized hierarchically: (1) phonetic discriminability/invariance via fast ABX with MFCC baselines and PanPhon correlations, complemented by layer-wise profiles and snippet/window sweeps to expose leakage across factors; (2) decoder-free token stability and linguistic alignment using TER/MTER under controlled WORLD prosody edits, segment-restricted TER for emphasis, PNMI, and streamable phonetic-supervised tokenizers (PAST) to select rate--latency--fidelity operating points; and (3) beyond-semantic competence and robustness through BoSS affect/dialect probes and SALMon consistency/alignment tasks, extensible to broader multilingual ASR/SID/LID/KWS/QbE coverage. A practical instantiation couples fast ABX, decoder-free token stability, and beyond-semantic tests; layer-wise curves guide layer/rate choices, and PAST defines operating points for rate--latency--fidelity trade-offs \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio}. Fast ABX on LibriSpeech (dev-clean/other) reports phoneme vs speaker errors against MFCC baselines and correlates with PanPhon articulatory distances; TER/MTER under controlled WORLD perturbations decouple prosody sensitivity from timbre, PNMI measures phoneme--token alignment, and segment-restricted TER captures word-level emphasis \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. BoSS adds affect/dialect probes; IndicSUPERB and SALMon broaden multilingual and robustness coverage across ASR/SID/LID/KWS/QbE \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Report within-/across-speaker and within-/any-context ABX with MFCC baselines; include layer-wise profiles and window sweeps \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio}.
  \item Use TER/MTER and PNMI at matched bitrates to assess token stability, prosody sensitivity, and linguistic alignment \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}.
  \item Add BoSS affect/dialect probes for empathetic-agent readiness \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.
\end{itemize}
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_1}
\subsection{Discretization vs continuous conditioning} \label{subsec:Discretization vs continuous conditioning}
Discrete tokens match decoder-only objectives, expose explicit rate/latency control, and enable retrieval/masked modeling; continuous interfaces preserve suprasegmentals, avoid codec loss, and align well to frozen LLMs via thin adapters or query-formers, often improving intelligibility and robustness \cite{yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul,wang2024comparativestudydiscretespeechtokenssema}. Continuous conditioning retains pitch/energy/rate/identity with minimal trainable parameters; bridging frozen Whisper to frozen LLMs via thin projectors and progressive alignment minimizes forgetting, and continuous lexical embeddings (tGSLM) replace hard clusters with a bottleneck; SSL families like data2vec/emotion2vec favor larger k if discretized, tilting toward continuous or high-cardinality discretization \cite{li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,algayres2023generativespokenlanguagemodelbasedcontin,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}.

Discrete interfaces provide tunable rates and unified sequence modeling: HuBERT-based 1k vocabularies for S2ST, segment-level k-means to manage context budget, and NAST-style augmentation-invariant units for robustness \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage}. Discrete targets can supervise SSL pretraining while serving continuous features at inference (SHuBERT), and discrete indices can outperform regression targets in cross-modal masked modeling (VQ-MAE-AV); ultra-low-rate generation is viable with text-aware BSQ at 6.25 Hz (TaDiCodec) \cite{entry2025selectivehubertselfsupervisedpretraining,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Codebook size should reflect encoder family: smaller k for HuBERT/ContentVec, larger k for data2vec/emotion2vec \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. Hybrids are pragmatic for duplex agents: keep lexical progression discrete for predictability/control and inject continuous tokens for style, discourse, and affect to reduce omissions/repetitions and preserve MOS \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,wang2024comparativestudydiscretespeechtokenssema}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Prefer continuous conditioning for high-fidelity comprehension and prosody/affect retention; align with thin adapters and progressive multi-task training without eroding text abilities \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.
  \item Prefer discrete tokens for explicit rate control, retrieval, and masked modeling; learned codebooks (RepCodec) and training-free dMel improve fidelity/streamability over vanilla k-means \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.
  \item Use hybrids to combine discrete controllability with continuous fidelity, mitigating discrete inconsistency \cite{guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete}.
  \item For ultra-low-rate generation, pair BSQ at 6.25 Hz with text-aware diffusion/flow \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.
\end{itemize}

Evaluation should be modality-aware: for speech UIs report SLU metrics (ASR/SID/LID/KWS/QbE) and alignment-aware diagnostics; for text UIs retain NLU-style tests. Use STAB to rate tokenizers, SALMon for robustness, IndicSUPERB for multilingual coverage, and include retrieval metrics with SpeechRAG-style robustness to ASR errors \cite{tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}.

\subsection{Alignment challenges to text LLMs} \label{subsec:Alignment challenges to text LLMs}
Speech--text gaps span information content (tone/prosody/duration), geometry (embedding distributions), timescale (Hz frames vs subwords), and structure (weaker syntax in speech encoders). Probes show speech encoders lag BERT in syntax and can be confounded by lexical artifacts, motivating alignment that reduces non-linguistic variance and clarifies syntactic signals, ideally in the LLM space to handle heterogeneous lengths/pauses \cite{shen2023wavesyntaxprobingspokenlanguagemodels,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}. With frozen foundations, uniform subsampling and thin transformers trained by next-token prediction bridge encoders to LLMs; deeper projectors yield diminishing returns, and staged pipelines (modal alignment$\rightarrow$conversational tuning) or alignment-aware connectors that pre-distill text-token embeddings mitigate forgetting; optimal-transport regularization improves geometric alignment \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,shu2023llasmlargelanguagespeechmodel,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}. Tokenizer-side alignment also helps: LM-SPT avoids frame-synchronous SSL targets at low rates by matching a frozen Whisper's features between original audio and semantic-only reconstructions, and DM-Codec distills text/speech teachers into RVQ latents via cosine alignment without strict time sync \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}.

To avoid catastrophic forgetting, favor staged freezing and targeted adaptation: train small projectors with frozen backbones, then freeze connectors and tune the LLM; apply Partial-LoRA to speech-only tokens to add emotion without harming instruction following \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}. Paralinguistic grounding ties acoustic cues to text-native reasoning using time-stamped emotion metadata and by maximizing mutual information between speech-unit and LLM latents, supporting affect/identity retention \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Reconcile rate/geometry by freezing pretrained speech/text models and learning a thin projector; operate at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.
  \item Enforce tokenizer-side semantic alignment (LM-SPT) for LM-salient low-rate tokens; distill early into RVQ latents (DM-Codec) \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}.
  \item Ground paralinguistics with explicit metadata and implicit QA generation to boost empathetic reasoning \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}.
  \item Use conservative adaptation (frozen backbones, small adapters, staged connector/LLM tuning) to add speech competence while preserving text decoding \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech}.
\end{itemize}
Diagnostics should pair syntax-aware probes that control lexical confounds with rate-aware WER, prosody/tone coverage, and alignment metrics computed in the LLM space to verify closure of tone/prosody/duration/syntax gaps without sacrificing text-native behavior.

















\section{Token to Speech Decoding and Vocoders} \label{sec:Token to Speech Decoding and Vocoders}

\subsection{Decoding targets and pathways} \label{subsec:Decoding targets and pathways}
Practical decoding targets coalesce into four routes: (i) codec-native inversion that predicts codec indices/latents and invokes a pretrained decoder, (ii) unit-to-waveform vocoding from discrete semantic/mixed tokens, (iii) token-to-mel prediction with a neural vocoder tail, and (iv) hybrid token-to-latent paths that feed a codec decoder. A discrete, LLM-ready token interface---vetted by STAB for downstream suitability---supports controllable streaming and policy-aware scheduling, as in StreamUni \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. As illustrated in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_2}, these four routes consolidate into three families: codec-aligned decoding (subsuming hybrid token-conditioned latents) to minimize conversion loss and maintain codec-rate latency; unit vocoder token-to-waveform paths that preserve modularity and enable promptable style control and full-duplex behavior; and token-to-mel pipelines that map tokens to mel spectrograms, leveraging flow/diffusion and mature neural vocoders for chunkable, streaming-friendly synthesis. This organization complements the STAB-vetted, LLM-ready token interface just noted, which underpins controllable streaming and policy-aware scheduling (e.g., StreamUni).

Codec-native inversion removes the spectrogram stage, aligns latency with the codec frame rate, and integrates cleanly with multi-stream RVQ; S2ST systems directly decode generated EnCodec tokens for real-time synthesis \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}. Hybrid token-to-latent variants replace indices with token-conditioned continuous latents---e.g., WaveNet-based diffusion feeding SoundStream---combining discrete predictability with continuous fidelity at 24 kHz \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Unit vocoders retain tokenizer modularity and promptable control; a listen-while-speaking SLM uses a GAN-based token-to-waveform vocoder with an acoustic prompt to steer timbre and decouple identity from content for duplex interaction \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. Token-to-mel pipelines map tokens to time--frequency targets via conditional flow/diffusion and render with HiFi-GAN/BigVGAN/Vocos, enabling chunkable, streaming-friendly S2S and ultra--low-bitrate setups \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

Consistent with the grouping in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_2}, route selection hinges on the upstream token interface, latency/RTF, and style control: codec-native inversion minimizes conversion loss and removes a separate vocoder \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; unit vocoders maximize modularity and promptable identity control \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}; token-to-mel leverages robust conditional flow/diffusion and mature vocoders for streaming \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}; hybrid latent diffusion inherits codec fidelity while easing rate constraints via continuous latents \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_2}
\subsection{Objective families and decoder backbones} \label{subsec:Objective families and decoder backbones}
Decoder backbones largely fall into: GAN/vocoder pipelines optimized for low-latency rendering, diffusion/flow decoders resilient to aggressive compression and token sparsity, and autoregressive (AR) or semi-AR token decoders that stabilize pacing and alignment; effectiveness depends on token semantics, bitrate, and streaming constraints \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,shen2024acousticbpespeechgenerationdiscretetoken,zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

GAN/vocoder pipelines. HiFi-GAN remains a strong default for unit-to-waveform and mel inversion due to favorable RTF and perceptual quality; conditioning on x-vectors yields identity-controllable reconstructions from discrete units \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Cascaded S2ST stacks commonly predict mels (e.g., FastSpeech2 with PortaSpeech prior/post-net) using mel L1, duration/pitch/energy L2, VAE KL, and flow NLL objectives before HiFi-GAN synthesis \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Codec-native variants blend adversarial/feature-matching with reconstruction losses and can adopt learned stochastic decoders at low bitrates \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

Diffusion/flow decoders. Conditional flow-matching and diffusion map discrete tokens to acoustic targets with stability under low token rates; large-scale interleaved training applies CFM over tokens with HiFi-GAN as terminal vocoder \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. At ultra-low bitrates, diffusion-only decoding improves intelligibility and perception versus GAN baselines while simplifying optimization \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. WaveNet-based diffusion with v-parameterization and cosine SNR, integrated into a SoundStream decoder, boosts low-rate quality; moment-matching distillation reduces sampling to $\approx$4 steps with minor DNSMOS impact and stable WER \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

Autoregressive and semi-AR decoders. AR decoders provide tight duration/prosody control, whereas semi-AR/CTC schedules trade some control for parallelism and lower latency. Phonetic-grounded causal modeling (PAST), training-free streamable dMel, unified hierarchical RVQ tokens, and standardized STAB evaluation compose cleanly with GAN or diffusion/flow renderers for stable timing and chunked generation at low rates \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

Loss design and guidance. Combining adversarial and feature-matching terms for periodicity/timbre with reconstruction/spectral losses for envelopes/prosody works well in codec-native and GAN pipelines \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}, while mel-stage decoders leverage mel L1, duration/pitch/energy L2, VAE KL, and flow NLL before HiFi-GAN \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Diffusion/flow avoids adversarial instability and is robust at low token rates \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. At kbps RVQ rates, codec-native or GAN decoders deliver low-latency, identity-preserving synthesis; for semantic/mixed tokens under aggressive compression or streaming, token-conditioned diffusion/flow---with step-distilled samplers and a HiFi-GAN tail when targeting mel---yields higher perceptual quality \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

\subsection{Hybrid continuous--discrete approaches} \label{subsec:Hybrid continuous--discrete approaches}
Conditioning discrete content tokens with continuous vectors for prosody, timbre, and global style consistently improves naturalness and identity retention at low rates. Flow-SLM pairs discrete linguistic tokens with continuous acoustic embeddings \cite{chou2025flowslmjointlearninglinguisticacousticin}; GOAT-TTS keeps content discrete while conditioning on continuous style prompts to reduce buzzy artifacts \cite{song2025goatttsexpressiverealisticspeechgenerati}; DiffSoundStream conditions latent diffusion on semantic and acoustic tokens but decodes via continuous SoundStream latents to recover microstructure under compression \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

A local--global design pattern is effective: fuse time-synchronous discrete content/prosody with a slowly varying or utterance-level continuous style. VAE-GSLM concatenates discrete semantics, learned paralinguistic latents, and an utterance embedding for DDPM/DDIM decoding, then applies HiFi-GAN, improving MOS over token-only baselines \cite{chen2025variationalframeworkimprovingnaturalness}. UniCodec merges content and prosodic token streams and modulates them with a global continuous token via FiLM-style scale/bias, decoupling utterance-level style from segmental content \cite{jiang2025universalspeechtokenlearninglowbitratene}. Instruction-following SLMs expose continuous LLM hidden states to the unit generator for synchronized text-and-unit emission with stable prosody \cite{fang2025llamaomniseamlessspeechinteractionlargel}, and cascaded S2ST augments discrete streams with a global prosody embedding and scalar trajectories to reduce artifacts in cross-lingual synthesis \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Continuous-first modeling in tGSLM learns on continuous lexical/acoustic tokens but currently relies on discrete HuBERT units for synthesis, motivating direct diffusion/vocoder decoders to simplify deployment \cite{algayres2023generativespokenlanguagemodelbasedcontin}.

Design guidance: adopt local discrete semantics plus learned continuous paralinguistic latents; modulate globally via FiLM/scale--bias; prefer moderate-step diffusion/flow with a robust HiFi-GAN tail for high naturalness under compression \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,chen2025variationalframeworkimprovingnaturalness,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Where explicit control is required, combine global style with discrete content and word-level pause/duration/F0 signals to preserve rhythm and emphasis \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.

\subsection{Conditioning, controllability, and style preservation} \label{subsec:Conditioning, controllability, and style preservation}
Controllability separates semantic prediction from acoustic realization so identity, emotion, and prosody are steerable under latency. Affect alignment remains limited (e.g., 52.59% for a strong omni model), underscoring the need for stronger prosody/emotion controls \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.

Prompt conditioning and identity control. Short acoustic prompts ($\approx$3--5 s) seed timbre/global style while leaving content to the LM. In end-to-end S2ST, brief EnCodec-token prompts preserve speaker style across translation, with better WavLM-style similarity after translation-data upsampling \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}. At ultra-low token rates, text-aware diffusion/flow decoders gain from short prompts, improving WER, similarity, and UTMOS without added latency \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Unified models combine an <enroll-speech> prompt with x-vectors for zero-shot identity in TTS and conversion \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.

Speaker/style embeddings and role-aware rendering. Fixed-dimensional embeddings injected into decoders or vocoders stabilize identity; multi-speaker dialogue stacks condition a unit vocoder on role-specific embeddings and render stereo with channel swaps for turn-level analysis \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Cross-lingual style transfer uses a VAE-derived global prosody embedding with SALN to control style without parallel same-text data \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.

Disentangled channels and localized prosody edits. Mixed-token designs expose semantic and acoustic channels so residual vectors carry prosody/timbre while early channels encode content \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Prompt-conditioned acoustic-unit generation preserves identity while the LM focuses on content; residual-channel scheduling enables local prosody manipulation and aligns with multi-stream RVQ \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}. When streams are deduplicated or pushed to lower rates, explicit duration and prosody tokens stabilize rhythm/intonation \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.

Global--local prosody controls and latency. Combining a global prosody embedding with local, text-synchronous edits---pause insertion, duration modification, word-level F0 transfer with speaker normalization---yields better ratings on emphasis, intonation, rhythm, and emotion, directly targeting BoSS affect criteria \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Short prompts amortize identity setup and pair well with parallel or semi-AR unit generation; prompt-guided diffusion/flow at low token rates caps latency while maintaining intelligibility and similarity \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Reporting should include intelligibility (WER/CER), quality (UTMOS/MOS), and identity/style similarity (e.g., WavLM cosine); role-separated stereo renderings with fixed embeddings aid dialogue auditing \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Chunked generation that alternates silent reasoning with speech helps reasoning without extra latency, and synthetic speech--text pairs improve paralinguistic instruction following without degrading core LLM skills \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked,lu2025desta2developinginstructionfollowingspee}.

Ecosystem supports conditioning and evaluation at scale: IndicSUPERB offers 1,684 h across 12 languages for six SLU tasks with large gains from language-specific tuning; fastabx provides efficient ABX analysis of learned representations; LM-SPT and PAST learn low-rate, semantically aligned and phonetic-aware units; SpeechRAG aligns speech--text embeddings for ASR-free retrieval and robust QA under noisy transcripts \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}.

\begin{itemize}
  \item Use short, homogeneous prompts to seed identity/style in textless S2ST over neural-codec tokens and in text-aware diffusion/flow or prompt-composition models; continuous speech tokenizers (Cont-SPT) further improve naturalness and continuity \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl}.
  \item Inject speaker/style embeddings in decoders/vocoders to preserve identity independently of content; in unit-vocoder stacks, enroll with an x-vector plus an <enroll-speech> prompt for controllable TTS/VC \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.
  \item Combine global and local controls: apply a VAE-derived global embedding via SALN and add word-level pause, duration, and pitch controls to strengthen emphasis, intonation, and rhythm \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.
  \item Prefer tokenizations that separate content from acoustics (e.g., LM-SPT semantics with dMel or codec residuals) and localize edits to acoustic/RVQ channels; stabilize low-rate decoding by conditioning codec latents on semantic tokens (DiffSoundStream), mitigating discrete inconsistency, and adding explicit duration/prosody tokens as in ProsodyLM \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces}.
  \item Evaluate controllability with identity- and affect-sensitive metrics aligned to task goals; BoSS benchmarks highlight persistent affect gaps that should guide conditioning design \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.
  \item For multi-party dialogue, condition the vocoder on fixed per-role embeddings and render stereo with channel swaps at turn boundaries for controlled, turn-level analyses \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.
\end{itemize}

















\section{Language Modeling over Speech Tokens} \label{sec:Language Modeling over Speech Tokens}
Language modeling over speech tokens treats acoustics as sequence elements---semantic units, codec codewords, or short continuous prompts---so a single causal decoder can reason over text and speech while trading off latency, fidelity, and sequencing. To systematize this landscape, we present a hierarchical taxonomy that structures the field into four pillars (see Figure~\ref{fig:tree_figure_Langu}): (1) modeling objectives and architectures---covering AR, NAR, and hybrid decoding with interleaved training and token-conditioned diffusion/flow, accompanied by actionable recipes and diagnostics; (2) modality transfer and LLM adaptation---including continued shared-token pretraining versus cold-start projector bridges, alignment strategies, and PEFT curricula with supporting toolkits; (3) interleaved and duplex modeling exemplars---spanning token-level and linguistic--acoustic interleaving, inference-time budget policies, duplex scheduling with robustness features, and blueprint patterns; and (4) empirical regularities and scaling---highlighting token-rate knees around $\approx 12.5$ Hz, capacity/sequence compression effects, task asymmetries, schedule--temperature coupling, token-space data scaling, evaluation suites, and effective configurations. This organization makes explicit the core trade-offs (latency--fidelity--sequencing), guidance on where to place AR vs. NAR computation, how to preserve text priors while adding speech skills, and how interleaving policies and token rates shape compute--quality efficiency, aligning with prior findings on modality transfer, scheduling for mixed streams, and scaling regularities \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.
\input{figs/tree_figure_Langu}


\subsection{Modeling objectives and architectures} \label{subsec:Modeling objectives and architectures}
Decoder-only SLMs realize four regimes under a next-token objective: (i) autoregressive (AR) decoders over discrete codec/semantic tokens or continuous prompts; (ii) non-autoregressive (NAR) decoders that parallelize residual acoustics via masked infilling or multi-codebook heads; (iii) AR+NAR hybrids that place AR on low-entropy channels and NAR on high-rate residuals; and (iv) interleaved dual-token designs that train a single decoder on mixed text--speech streams with a shared vocabulary and schedule-aware loss masking \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Token-conditioned diffusion/flow decoders complement these objectives at low token rates to recover prosody and timbre without reintroducing AR latency \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

AR backbones span textless unit LMs that quantize speech (e.g., HuBERT+k-means) and generate long-form audio via a unit LM plus unit vocoder \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}, duplex decoders that predict next audio tokens while ingesting a streaming listening stream with middle-fusion conditioning \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}, and decoder-only ASR that feeds CTC-compressed prompts and a sentinel (e.g., <aud>) into a causal decoder trained with joint CTC+CE \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. NAR and hybrid schedules reduce wall-clock delay by decoding RVQ-1 autoregressively and predicting higher RVQ levels in parallel, preserving intelligibility while cutting exposure bias; end-to-end S2ST and retrieval-augmented speech generation benefit most when codecs prioritize intelligibility over raw reconstruction \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}. Interleaved training with unified text--speech vocabularies enables a single decoder to cover text-only, speech-only, and mixed contexts with modality sentinels and loss masking, while token-to-waveform fidelity is restored via conditional flow/diffusion or vocoder tails, especially for semantic-only or ultra--low-rate tokens \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}.

Operational recipes emphasize chunked unspoken reasoning to improve quality without added latency (STITCH), lightweight paralinguistic infusion from synthetic speech--text pairs (DeSTA2), and low-budget training via careful initialization, synthetic interleaving, and preference optimization (Slamming) \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked,lu2025desta2developinginstructionfollowingspee,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}. Diagnostics and tokenization advances---IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU, LM-aware tokenization (LM-SPT/LAST), STAB stability probing, and fastabx separability---align interfaces with LM behavior \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. Actionable patterns: use AR decoder-only LMs for unit-based textless generation \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}, inject streaming listening embeddings with middle fusion for duplex agents \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}, feed CTC prompts for decoder-only ASR \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}, place AR on RVQ-1 and NAR on residuals for low-latency codecs \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}, and attach token-conditioned diffusion/flow decoders at low rates \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.

\subsection{Modality transfer and LLM adaptation} \label{subsec:Modality transfer and LLM adaptation}
A practical path to speech-capable models freezes a strong text LLM and adds tiny speech bridges (projectors, adapters, or LoRA), reaching competitive ASR/AST, QA, and mixed-modal dialog with $\approx$1% extra parameters and modest compute \cite{peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,cuervo2025textspeechlanguagemodelsimprovedcrossmod}. Three axes matter: initialization (continued interleaved pretraining vs. frozen LLM cold-start), interface (continuous projector vs. shared token vocabulary), and alignment curricula/losses (stage-wise freezing, PEFT, geometry- and behavior-preserving regularization).

Continued pretraining in a shared token space extends a text LLM to speech without projectors: GLM-4-Voice expands the vocabulary with speech tokens and scales interleaved next-token pretraining, preserving text competence via breadth of mixed-modality data \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, and SpeechComposer adapts a text-only OPT in the same decoder and vocabulary \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}. For speech-only S2ST, warm starts help optimization but final quality depends more on multilingual bidirectionality and parameter sharing than on text-LM initialization \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.

Cold-start bridges map SSL features into a frozen LLM's embedding space with minimal trainable parameters: a USM-to-LLM adaptor enables zero-shot instruction following and SLU \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}, production stacks project Whisper-large-v3 into Qwen-class LLMs via linear/MLP projectors with constrained LoRA and Partial-LoRA on speech spans to mitigate forgetting \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}, and the same projector approach supports decoder-only ASR and duplex listening embeddings with continued joint training of the speech generator and SSL encoder \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Alignment preserves language priors while adding speech skills: KL geometry/policy distillation and KL-regularized RL (e.g., Dr.GRPO) improve speech outputs without collapsing text abilities \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}; MI-based training retains beyond-semantic cues (prosody, identity) while respecting text geometry \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}; synchronized listening embeddings strengthen duplex alignment \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. Parameter-efficient adaptation and curricula---LoRA on a mostly frozen LLM (typical ranks/$\alpha$: 16--64/16--32), prompt/prefix-tuning for classification-like tasks and few-shot ICL on a fixed 150M GSLM with Random Label Mapping, language-aware prompting to reduce code mixing, progressive unfreezing (encoder $\rightarrow$ encoder+projector $\rightarrow$ projector+LLM with LoRA), and pseudo-prompt LM training for decoder-only ASR---control budgets and limit forgetting \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.

Blueprints and diagnostics---LAST for LM-aware tokenization, VoxEval for spoken QA/knowledge under varied audio, STAB for tokenizer stability, IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU, and fastabx for ABX probing---accelerate development \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. Efficient cross-modal transfer aligns speech and text at matched abstraction levels, freezes strong backbones with tiny adapters, scales on large synthetic interleaved token corpora, and uses modality-specific MoE to curb forgetting while preserving native speech latency \cite{cuervo2025textspeechlanguagemodelsimprovedcrossmod,shao2025deeptalktowardsseamlesssmartspeechintera,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste}.

\subsection{Interleaved and duplex modeling exemplars} \label{subsec:Interleaved and duplex modeling exemplars}
Interleaved, duplex-capable SLMs unify perception, reasoning, and speech/text synthesis by consuming and producing mixed token streams in a single causal decoder, typically initialized from text LMs and frozen audio foundations with lightweight adapters or shared dual-token vocabularies for zero-shot instruction following, long-form generation, contextual biasing, and simultaneous listening--speaking at favorable compute \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,park2025longformspeechgenerationspokenlanguagemo,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste}. As summarized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_3}, we organize this design space into three branches: token-level interleaving (shared vocabularies, adapter-bridged embeddings, synthetic span interleaving, and budget-cycled decoding), linguistic--acoustic planning interleaving (plan-before-speak phases, fixed text:speech ratios, and transcribe$\rightarrow$generate staging), and duplex scheduling controls (simultaneous listen--speak with IRQ tokens, middle-fusion conditioning, and turn-level text guidance). Together, these threads illustrate how recent systems unify perception, planning, and speech synthesis for low-latency, robust spoken interaction. Operationally, these branches instantiate token-level interleaving under a unified next-token objective, linguistic--acoustic interleaving that separates semantic planning from acoustic realization, and duplex scheduling that synchronizes user and assistant channels for overlaps, backchannels, and barge-in.

Token-level interleaving concatenates modality-tagged spans so one decoder covers ASR, TTS, S2ST, and mixed continuation: LLaSM inserts audio patches via boundary tokens \cite{shu2023llasmlargelanguagespeechmodel}, SpeechComposer uses a shared vocabulary \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}, and large-scale token-space corpora replace span-corrupted text with speech tokens sampled by a text-to-token model (e.g., Poisson spans, $\approx$0.3 corruption) to enable 100B--600B-token interleaved pretraining without waveform synthesis \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Instruction-following bridges prepend adapted speech embeddings to text before a frozen LLM \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}, while inference-time policies reduce latency by cycling text and speech token budgets and by staging transcribe$\rightarrow$generate to anchor acoustics to a fresh semantic plan (e.g., 13 text then 26 speech tokens) \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends}.

Linguistic--acoustic interleaving enforces plan-before-speak schedules using explicit phase tags (chain-of-modality prompting) and streaming schedulers that first output partial transcripts, then complete translations per chunk; fixed text:speech ratios provide interpretable latency--quality knobs and transfer across backbones \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends}. Duplex scheduling injects a streaming listening stream while emitting next audio tokens; IRQ tokens pause generation on interruption, and middle-fusion conditioning yields near-ceiling interactivity F1 with strong intelligibility, while turn-level planning that emits brief textual guidance before each speech turn and balances text:speech losses improves semantic quality by over 30% yet preserves full-duplex timing \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}.

Exemplar blueprints include semantic-first AR planning with low-latency acoustic realization (DualSpeechLM-style), token-LMs augmented by a flow/diffusion decoder (FlowSLM), vector-token predictability with Poisson-span interleaving near $\approx$0.3 corruption (VecTokSpeech), and listening-embedding integration with interruption tokens and chunk-wise alternation (Ichigo-style) \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. When vocabularies cannot be shared, projector-first interleaving delivers mixed contexts without LM expansion \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}; with shared vocabularies, synthetic interleaved corpora efficiently scale mixed-sequence competence \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Span markers boost cross-modal continuation, plan-before-speak policies lower initial audio latency, streaming CoT improves S2ST responsiveness, and duplex robustness rises with blockwise listening fusion, IRQ tokens, and turn-level guidance plus conservative decoding temperatures \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_3}
\subsection{Empirical regularities and scaling} \label{subsec:Empirical regularities and scaling}
Compute--quality depends on token cadence, segmentation granularity, decoder alignment, and capacity splits between the speech front end and language backbone. Interleaved pretraining scales smoothly in token space and exhibits a rate knee near $\approx$12.5 Hz, below which further compression hurts performance \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

Token rate and segmentation: coarser cadences expand headroom at fixed FLOPs---SyllableLM operates at 5.0--8.33 Hz with ~30$\times$ less LM training compute than AudioLM and 4--4.5$\times$ faster inference at equal LM size while maintaining competitive semantics \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}; ultra--low-rate codecs remain generation-friendly when paired with text-aware decoders, with 10--32 diffusion steps forming an efficiency sweet spot and a favorable reconstruction--generation gap for SLMs \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Backbone capacity and sequence compression: larger decoders consistently improve recognition in Encoder--Adaptor--LLM pipelines (e.g., Qwen2.5-7B > Qwen2-1.5B; Gemma3-12B > Qwen2.5-7B with identical front ends), while projector pooling/splicing (4:1--5:1, 20$\rightarrow$40 ms) halves sequence length with negligible loss \cite{li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. Task asymmetry: scaling disproportionately benefits generative targets---TTS/VC improves faster than ASR as decoder size grows \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}, and discrete TTS dWER drops markedly from ~389M to ~1B parameters---yet cross-domain generalization remains uneven \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}.

Scheduling and sampling: planning-aware interleaving favors conservative decoding (e.g., TurnGuide performs best at temperatures 0.8--1.0), whereas speech-only chunking peaks at higher temperatures, revealing a tight schedule--temperature coupling \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. In-context learning in SLMs often needs meta-training or prompt-tuning; scale alone is insufficient \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Data scale and token-space construction: interleaved corpora built directly in token space yield monotonic gains from 0$\rightarrow$100B$\rightarrow$200B$\rightarrow$600B tokens and support the $\approx$12.5 Hz operating point for both training and streaming \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.
