Capacity planning follows: pick cadence near the knee using prosody-sensitivity and STAB diagnostics ($\approx$12.5 Hz for semantic/mixed tokens), or, at 5--8.33 Hz, budget stronger text-aware decoders plus explicit silence/syllable modeling \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. In Encoder--Adaptor--LLM systems, prioritize robust speech backbones with lightweight adapters and reduce sequence length via projector pooling or frame splicing (4--5$\times$ to 25/12.5/6.25 Hz); prefer LM-aligned tokenizers (LM-SPT) and adapt by language to avoid semantic dilution at low rates \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}. Allocate compute to model size over extra tokens; interleaved SLMs scale more efficiently than textless SLMs and show slower linguistic scaling than text LMs, making ASR gains hinge on front-end quality and tokenization choices \cite{cuervo2024scalingpropertiesspeechlanguagemodels,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Tune decoding temperatures to the interleaving policy \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}, use diffusion/flow decoders with 10--32 steps for ultra--low-rate synthesis while tracking the reconstruction--generation gap \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}, scale interleaved corpora in token space for monotonic returns \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, and include prompt-tuning or meta-training to elicit ICL-like behaviors \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Comprehensive evaluation should cover multilingual SLU (IndicSUPERB), robustness/knowledge under varied audio (VoxEval), and tokenizer diagnostics (STAB, fastabx) \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.

Effective configurations pair strong text LMs with rate-aware tokenization near $\approx$12.5 Hz, carefully scheduled speech--text interleaving, and compute concentrated in model capacity, trained on large synthetic mixed-token corpora ($\approx$600B of 1T total tokens), delivering steeper quality--compute gains, competitive spoken QA, and strong semantics at reduced cost \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,cuervo2024scalingpropertiesspeechlanguagemodels}.

















\section{Training Paradigms and Modality Alignment} \label{sec:Training Paradigms and Modality Alignment}
Curricula that endow LMs with speech competence co-optimize five phases---interface pretraining, modality alignment, supervised and instruction consolidation, duplex/streaming specialization, and post-alignment via preferences/RL---under three constraints that govern transfer: interface invariance, compression without semantic erosion, and forgetting control with standardized evaluation. Two pathways dominate: connector-first bridges with frozen foundations and thin adaptors versus continued mixed-modality pretraining with shared vocabularies; both are shaped by AR/NAR decompositions and tokenizer choices that balance latency, bitrate, and drift \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. The subsections consolidate multi-stage pipelines and alignment blueprints, scalable synthetic supervision, interface consistency with compression, and diagnostics for retention and robustness.

\subsection{Multi-stage pipelines} \label{subsec:Multi-stage pipelines}
A practical scaffold freezes strong speech encoders and a text LLM, inserts a $\approx$1% adaptor, and trains under a single next-token objective; warm starts from a text LM are paired with LM-aware tokenization and scaled mixed data, then enriched with speech captioning, joint speech--text unit modeling, and paralinguistic/contextual supervision to deliver ASR/AST, dialog, and zero-shot instruction following within bitrate/latency budgets while preserving text priors \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,lu2024destaenhancingspeechlanguagemodelsthroug,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. Canonical infrastructure---IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU, LM-aware tokenization that reuses a single text LM (LAST), and phonetic-separability probes (fastabx)---standardizes data and diagnostics \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. As summarized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_4}, the overall design is organized as a concise two-level map: Stage 0 aggregates interface and tokenization choices (LM-aware and phonetic tokenizers, ultra-low-rate codecs, audiovisual masked pretraining, and diagnostic tools); Stages 1--2 address modality alignment and consolidation via connector-first adapters, interleaved shared-vocabulary pretraining, caption-based alignment, and paralinguistic grounding; and Stages 3--4 emphasize specialization and optimization for streaming/duplex use, ASR-free retrieval via SpeechRAG, preference learning with structural constraints, contextual biasing, and stability/reporting practices, with representative works cited at each leaf.

Key building blocks include semantically aligned or phonetic tokenizers (LM-SPT at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz; PAST with supervised phonetics and a causal streaming variant) \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer}; ASR-free SpeechRAG for retrieval under high WER \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}; connector-first SLM with $\approx$1% (156M) adaptor over frozen foundations \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}; hybrid AR/NAR S2S that generates low-entropy streams autoregressively and residuals in parallel (MSLM-S2ST) \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; audiovisual masked pretraining with VQ codebooks and contrastive alignment (VQ-MAE-AV) \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}; collapsed diffusion codecs that operate at ultra-low rates and optionally continue decoder-only training (TaDiCodec) \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}; and training-free dMel discretization for robust RichTTS/RichASR \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.

Stage 0 (interface and rate control) fixes tokenizer/codec, cadence, bitrate/codebooks, and inversion targets using STAB-style metrics, while mitigating discrete inconsistency for stable conditioning; ultra-low-rate diffusion codecs and VQ-MAE-AV represent efficiency and coverage extremes \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Stage 1 (alignment) follows connector-first bridges---LM-aware tokens (LAST, LM-SPT) or Whisper$\rightarrow$LLM projectors with progressive unfreezing only as needed \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}---or continued pretraining with shared-vocabulary interleaving at $\approx$12.5 Hz that scales more efficiently than textless SLMs while preserving knowledge \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,nachmani2024spokenquestionansweringspeechcontinuatio}; affect-first alignment can precede paralinguistic grounding (BLSP-Emo) \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}. Stage 2 consolidates with supervised/instruction data---real and synthetic pairs, mined speech, language-specific tuning---and can add auxiliary SER heads for affect; textless SLMs benefit from prompt-tuning warmups with few-shot demonstrations, and decoder-only ASR gains from interleaved LM-only updates plus joint CTC--decoder training \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,noroozi2024instructiondatagenerationunsupervisedada,lu2025desta2developinginstructionfollowingspee,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Stage 3 specializes for duplex/streaming via IRQ prediction, barge-in handling, and streaming curricula that interleave partial transcripts/plans before speech emission, and integrates SpeechRAG for listen-and-retrieve \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}. Stage 4 applies preference learning/RL with structural constraints (e.g., KL/DPO proximity, structural tokens, optimal transport) and second-pass rescoring with contextual biasing, few-shot prompts, and checkpoint averaging, achieving sizable WER gains \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext,sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu}.

Forgetting is curbed by progressive unfreezing, freezing connectors during downstream NTP, LoRA restricted to speech spans, retaining LM-only mini-batches for ASR, and amortizing gaps via continued pretraining; reports should include tokenizer geometry/rates, per-stage optimizers/policies, mixed-data composition (~1T tokens), streaming hyperparameters, and verifiable-reward setups under standardized benchmarks and STAB diagnostics \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_4}
\subsection{Alignment mechanisms and blueprints} \label{subsec:Alignment mechanisms and blueprints}
Alignment spans four layers: LM-aware tokenization that projects acoustics into text-LM space (LAST) \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}; tokenizer-boundary semantic grounding via reconstruction-driven distillation against ASR encoders to yield low-rate units (LM-SPT) \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}; encoder--tokenizer designs that produce decoupled, stable units for joint speech--text modeling \cite{entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste}; and tokenizer$\rightarrow$LM$\rightarrow$decoder coupling with multi-token prediction and paralinguistic conditioning \cite{fan2025whatmakesgoodspeechtokenizerllmcentricge,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}.

Geometry/timing into the LLM space leverage thin projectors with temporal pooling trained jointly with Whisper and LoRA-adapted LLMs under NTP \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}; KL-based behavior alignment and auxiliary SER heads ground affect (BLSP-Emo) \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}. Projector-free paths extend the LM vocabulary with $\approx$12.5 Hz speech tokens and train on interleaved sequences \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Decoder-only ASR stabilizes with prompt-length--aware routing to LM-only objectives and joint CTC--decoder training, with shallow fusion at inference \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.

Semantic grounding couples codec and language priors: DM-Codec distills BERT/HuBERT into RVQ token features via cosine and [CLS] guidance alongside reconstruction/adversarial/commitment losses, removing teachers at inference \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}; text-aware diffusion decoders expose and narrow reconstruction--generation gaps at ultra-low rates (TaDiCodec) \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Shared-vocabulary interleaving at 12.5 Hz maintains consistent grounding/modeling/inversion targets \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, while AV settings benefit from contrastive correspondence with coupled masking and cross-attention fusion (CAF--CAF) \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}.

Stable anchoring favors single-objective alignment of encoder$\rightarrow$projector$\rightarrow$LLM with temporal pooling for latency control and pseudo-prompt LM objectives to reduce train--test mismatch in decoder-only ASR \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Schedule-aware post-alignment down-weights <think> vs <transcribe> losses and uses structural-validity rewards to prevent reward hacking; beyond semantics, BoSS maximizes relevance via neural estimators and MI-preserving constraints so affect/identity/style persist \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.

A compact recipe blends projector-first bridges with KL behavior alignment and affect grounding, shared-vocabulary interleaving, tokenizer-side distillation with text-aware decoding, multimodal fusion choices, and decoder-only ASR routing/CTC settings; diagnostics pair IndicSUPERB, VoxEval, and fastabx with contamination audits \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}.

\subsection{Synthetic and programmatic supervision at scale} \label{subsec:Synthetic and programmatic supervision at scale}
Scaling with modest compute relies on frozen backbones plus $\approx$1% adaptors, LM-aware tokenization so a single TextLM processes both modalities, and staged objectives that align linguistic content with paralinguistics---enabling duplex listen-and-speak, zero-shot instruction following, and affect grounding \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,chen2025goatslmspokenlanguagemodelparalinguistic}. Three recurring ingredients: token-space interleaving that converts corpora or text to mixed [speech/text] sequences without waveforms; auditable instruction sets via TTS-rendered dialogues and LLM-generated Q/A; and AI-feedback preferences with verifiable rewards.

Token-space pairing scales efficiently: a text-to-token generator reaches $\approx$25k tokens/s on a single H800, enabling ~600B mixed tokens at 12.5 Hz for unified NTP and streaming-ready interfaces \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Streaming CoT is programmatically built by sampling prefixes from speech/transcript/translation triplets with speech-CoT prompts, and transfers across backbones \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Open blends (LLaSO, Omni VoiceTextBlender) synthesize millions of instruction pairs for joint SFT and seed duplex chat skills; compute-aware regimens (Slamming) combine dense TTS pretraining with short, verifiable DPO to surpass uncurated real-audio mixes on single GPUs \cite{peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem,fang2025llamaomniseamlessspeechinteractionlargel,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}, with bridging-style conversions noting TTS-only distribution shifts \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.

Paralinguistic/contextual supervision conditions targets on transcripts plus emotion labels (BLSP-Emo) \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}; EI-CPM prompts GPT-4o with word-level transcripts (WhisperX) and categorical/dimensional emotions to produce filtered CPQAs, expanded by context-free PQA* \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. These pipelines broaden coverage over affect, dialect, age, identity, and non-speech vocalizations with TELEVAL/IndicSUPERB assessments \cite{chen2025goatslmspokenlanguagemodelparalinguistic,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo}. Duplex/IRQ supervision scales with synthetic wake words, interruption timing, and MUSAN noise for precise IRQ labels \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. Preference optimization in Encoder--Adaptor--LLM ASR uses verifiable two-hypothesis rewards with structural validity/correctness/error-type/detail matching and improvement-over-prior, where RF1+RF2+RF5 combinations perform best; large-scale token interleaving seeds such post-alignment \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

Standardized SLU (IndicSUPERB), acoustic-awareness evaluation (SALMon), tokenizer diagnostics (STAB), ASR-free RAG, and continuous tokenization (Cont-SPT) accelerate iteration, while known limits include partial linguistic coverage, uneven task gains, ABX tooling friction now reduced by fastabx, TTS-only shifts, safety brittleness without adversarial tests, and budget sensitivity/forgetting mitigated by compute-aware blends and rehearsal/DPO \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}. Low-budget SSL pretraining (e.g., HuBERT on 8 GPUs) lowers barriers but demands contamination controls \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}. Open, auditable recipes combine 12.5 Hz token-space interleaving, streaming CoT triplets, paralinguistic QA curation with strict filters, duplex IRQ supervision, verifiable post-alignment, and instruction blends that reach sub-3-day training on 4 GPUs with ~226 ms streaming latency \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem,fang2025llamaomniseamlessspeechinteractionlargel,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,lu2025desta2developinginstructionfollowingspee}.

\subsection{Modality alignment, sequence compression, and interface consistency} \label{subsec:Modality alignment, sequence compression, and interface consistency}
A stable speech--LM contract uses invariant interfaces and alignment-aware compression: text-aligned discrete semantic tokens (LM-SPT) or a fixed-geometry continuous token space (continuous tokens/flow matching) predicted by the LM, with learned semantic reduction rather than rigid pooling; reconstruction matches a frozen ASR encoder at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz to preserve lexical anchors and paralinguistics for low-latency streaming and improved S2T/TTS \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul}. Interfaces are enforced either by adaptor-first bridges with uniform 4$\times$ subsampling into the LLM embedding space \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}, shared-vocabulary SLMs with a unified discrete inventory at 12.5 Hz \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, or ultra-low-rate BSQ codecs at 6.25 Hz consumed by AR/masked decoders \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}; RepCodec offers information-rich semantic tokens from HuBERT/data2vec for seamless integration \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}.

Compression should not erode semantics: fixing 12.5 Hz during interleaved pretraining standardizes targets and shortens sequences, while 4$\times$ subsampling reduces prefilling but motivates alignment-preserving compression; at 6.25 Hz, text-aware decoding closes reconstruction--generation gaps and preserves intelligibility/identity when tokenizer and decoder are co-designed, with OT-based regularization validated by STAB/IndicSUPERB \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Lexical anchoring holds under shared 12.5 Hz vocabularies and conservative continuous bridges; ultra-low-rate BSQ with text-aware decoding sustains cross-lingual WER \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Continuous tokenizers (Cont-SPT) often capture finer semantics/robustness than discrete tokens; decoupled tokenization with multi-token prediction improves information density and speed, while XY-Tokenizer tightens text alignment and acoustic fidelity with speaker-aware generation \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,gong2025xytokenizermitigatingsemanticacousticcon,fan2025whatmakesgoodspeechtokenizerllmcentricge,wang2024comparativestudydiscretespeechtokenssema}. A pragmatic blueprint fixes one interface end-to-end (discrete at a fixed Hz or continuous projection), compresses on the perception path, avoids shifting downstream contracts, audits side-effects (rates, downsampling, alignment-guided vs random), and reports vocabulary/rate consistency across pretraining and fine-tuning \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,wang2024c3llmconditionalmultimodalcontentgenerat}.

\subsection{Diagnostics, ablations, and reporting} \label{subsec:Diagnostics, ablations, and reporting}
A rate- and schedule-aware protocol should standardize interfaces (unit tokenizers; SSR-style segmentation/compression aligned to text tokens), fix compute budgets, and quantify alignment, retention, robustness, and forgetting. Evaluation spans zero-shot cross-modal transfer, spoken QA/knowledge robustness under varied audio (VoxEval), and scaling behavior of interleaved SLMs that favor model size over token count while leveraging synthetic data and low-budget SSL pretraining \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste}. Core SLU metrics (ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, KWS) should be paired with acoustic slices (noise, emotion, speaker, room), speech--text alignment probes, and tokenizer consistency analyses \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Recognition scoring uses IndicSUPERB and STAB with consistent toolchains, fixed decoding, and checkpoint averaging \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}; duplex/TTS fidelity is measured by Whisper-v3 WER on LibriTTS-testsetB without interruptions and turn-taking F1 within [0,1.0 s] windows \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Tokenizer robustness should connect STAB axes---speaker/context/language invariance, pitch/speed/noise robustness, compressibility, vocabulary utilization---to downstream trends with multi-seed CIs \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. For AV emotion, ablations on fusion (self vs cross-attention), loss mixtures, SER heads (attention pooling, mean, Query2Emo), modality contributions, token sizes, and encoder depth consistently favor cross-attention fusion and Query2Emo with both VQ pretraining and encoder fine-tuning \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Textless ICL diagnostics verify demonstration use (guessing-rate jumps), track attention progression, and standardize utterance lengths; controlled ablations vary language, task, and representation (FBANK vs SSL encoders), with fastabx isolating phonetic separability on IndicSUPERB \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Mixed speech--text generation ablates task-set composition, text-augmented prompting, unseen-speaker conversion, and randomized loss sampling \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}; for S2ST, upsample translation data for style, prefer bidirectional training for ASR-BLEU/style, and warm-start from OPT for speed \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.

Encoder--Adaptor--LLM baselines freeze pretrained encoders/LLMs, train tiny (~1%) adapters, optionally feed dMel tokens, and ablate augmentation scale and LLM size against Whisper Turbo/large-v3 and multimodal zero-shot baselines under meeteval/CER protocols; post-alignment decoding with mixed beam+sampling sets a strong baseline, and verifiable rewards (RF1+RF2+RF5) yield measurable WER/CER gains \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}. Retention and cross-modal gains are tracked with experience replay, model merging, and discounted LoRA to limit catastrophic forgetting; report text-only retention ($\le$1--2% absolute EM/F1 or recall@k drop), include direct speech-retrieval baselines where aligned retrievers rival text-only retrieval and beat cascades at high WER, and audit duplex responsiveness alongside base TTS quality \cite{hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. Affect grounding benefits from staged curricula and in-domain continuation \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}.

Transparent reporting should include public code/configs/models, full data provenance/statistics, explicit task coverage with standardized benchmarks (IndicSUPERB, STAB, SALMon), compute/training budgets, and exact evaluation setups (tokenizers, contextual-bias prompts/lists, few-shot exemplars, decoding policy) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu}. Real-time duplex UIs should report schedules, frame/token rates, bitrates, vocabulary/codebooks, interleaving policies, beam/sampling settings, WER/CER tools, and baselines; AV/duplex specifics include barge-in window, F1 protocol, streaming encoder and fusion strategy, agent bitrate, SER heads, encoder depth, tokenization (discrete vs continuous), and SSL pretraining/fine-tuning status \cite{yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}. Linking STAB/ABX intrinsic metrics to downstream outcomes clarifies how tokenizer choices (e.g., PAST) and chunked reasoning policies (STITCH) shape latency--accuracy profiles \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}.

















\section{System Architectures and Generation Strategies} \label{sec:System Architectures and Generation Strategies}

\subsection{Cascaded vs end-to-end vs interleaved} \label{subsec:Cascaded vs end-to-end vs interleaved}
As synthesized in the tree-structured schematic in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_5}, three design regimes for spoken dialogue systems are contrasted: cascaded ASR$\rightarrow$LLM$\rightarrow$TTS pipelines that prioritize controllability yet risk error compounding and paralinguistic loss; unified end-to-end SLMs that reduce hand-offs, support duplex interaction, and rely on thin projector bridges with targeted semantic/temporal/affect alignment to stabilize behavior; and interleaved/parallel decoding that structures when text and speech tokens appear to bound latency while preserving style via AR-on-semantics with NAR residual refinement. The figure also foregrounds evaluation dimensions---multilingual SLU on IndicSUPERB, unsupervised ABX discriminability, and syntax probing---that we use to balance semantic competence against paralinguistic fidelity across regimes.

Cascaded ASR$\rightarrow$LLM$\rightarrow$TTS systems remain attractive for controllability, debuggability, and component swapability, yet they accumulate errors and lose prosody, speaker, and affect cues when collapsing speech to text; they perform competitively on semantic SLU but lag on paralinguistics and limit coverage for unwritten languages \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}. End-to-end SLMs unify perception, reasoning, and synthesis to cut hand-offs and time-to-first-audio, preserve identity and affect, and enable duplex listening-while-speaking without text-mediated error propagation; thin projector bridges that connect frozen speech encoders to frozen text LLMs deliver strong ASR/AST and zero-shot control with $\approx$1% trainable parameters and support contextual biasing \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}. Alignment strategy is pivotal: progressive connector-first alignment with temporal pooling and LoRA minimizes drift, decoder-only ASR stabilizes with joint CTC--decoder training and LM-only routing, and behavior-first affect alignment improves empathetic dialogue without harming instruction following \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}. Interleaved and parallel regimes recover controllability and bound latency by structuring when modalities appear within a single decoder: large-scale shared-vocabulary interleaving supports mixed continuations, plan-first text interleaves stabilize duplex semantics, and AR-on-semantics with NAR residual refinement preserves speaker style at low latency; fixed text:speech cycles and block-wise chunking further reduce first-audio delay \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask,zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends}. Holistic comparison should pair multilingual SLU on IndicSUPERB with ABX discriminability and syntax probing, noting mid-network syntax peaks and scale benefits \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,shen2023wavesyntaxprobingspokenlanguagemodels}. Guidance: cascades fit domains with near-perfect transcripts and strict TTS control; unified SLMs are preferred when latency and paralinguistics matter, strengthened by lightweight bridges to curb long-sequence drift; interleaved/parallel variants offer a middle path combining plan-first text, AR-semantic emission, and NAR refinement for low latency and expressive speech \cite{wang2024whydospeechlanguagemodelsfailgeneratesem,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,cuervo2024scalingpropertiesspeechlanguagemodels,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_5}
\subsection{Generative regimes and efficient backbones} \label{subsec:Generative regimes and efficient backbones}
Generation choices span three axes: RVQ-aware hierarchy, acoustic backbone (token-level AR, hybrid discrete/continuous, flow/ODE/diffusion), and parallelism/factorization (multi-token, grouped decoding, codebook reduction) \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,wang2024c3llmconditionalmultimodalcontentgenerat}. Grouped decoding and RQ-style factorization shorten horizons by emitting blocks of residual indices under shared attention.

- Hierarchical RVQ with channel-aware scheduling. Coarse-to-fine schedules devote AR capacity to the first codebook and predict residual channels non-autoregressively, matching or exceeding cascades in S2ST while preserving speaker style; phoneme-synchronous stops stabilize timing under greedy or low-temperature sampling, and intelligibility depends more on quantization/codebook design than the downstream decoder \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,song2024ellavstableneuralcodeclanguagemodelingal,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
- Masked infilling and parallel masked generation. Iterative masked decoders avoid long AR chains and hierarchical masking overheads; MaskSR performs full-band masked infilling with nine residual codebooks and parallel multi-token prediction, improving speed and speaker similarity, with quality governed primarily by the decoder backbone \cite{li2024masksrmaskedlanguagemodelfullbandspeechr,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
- Few-step flow/ODE and diffusion decoders. Conditional flow-matching or diffusion after a semantic/hybrid token LM reconstructs micro-prosody in 10--32 steps and pairs well with AR or masked LMs, enabling ultra-low-rate token interfaces and explicit quality--latency trade-offs \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
- Multi-token prediction and long-horizon efficiency. Predicting multiple tokens per step (or per masked iteration) reduces decoding steps with minor quality loss; residual heads can share KV caches with the AR base stream and use factorized classifiers to amortize attention \cite{li2024masksrmaskedlanguagemodelfullbandspeechr}.
- Runtime optimizations. FastAdaSP merges adjacent-similar KV states on the fly, compounding gains with grouped decoding and few-step diffusion; constant/linear or single-layer aggressive schedules suit different task sparsities \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.

Design guidance: use AR-on-first/NAR-on-rest RVQ with phoneme-synchronous stopping when balancing intelligibility and latency; prefer masked infilling with codebook summation when step count and speaker similarity dominate; attach few-step flow/diffusion under low token rates; combine multi-token/grouped residual decoding with KV-sequence merging; diagnose bottlenecks via quantization/codebook utilization in AR+NAR and decoder quality in masked-parallel settings \cite{li2024masksrmaskedlanguagemodelfullbandspeechr,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

\subsection{Sequencing and scheduling taxonomy} \label{subsec:Sequencing and scheduling taxonomy}
Sequencing policies orchestrate semantic planning, recognition, and acoustic realization under latency, duplex, and compute constraints. Three families---token-level interleaving, parallel/phase-decoupled generation with reconciliation, and chunked generation---offer tunable control of time-to-first-audio, stability, and fidelity while countering discrete-codec inconsistencies \cite{liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete}. Robust evaluation should couple large-scale streaming corpora with IndicSUPERB SLU, fastabx ABX discriminability, and STAB tokenizer diagnostics; LM-aware tokenization (LAST), streamable phonetic-acoustic units (PAST), and LM-SPT consistently reduce frame rates and strengthen SLMs, and SpeechRAG provides ASR-free retrieval robustness under high WER \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Lightweight bridges enable zero-shot instruction following, contextual biasing, and dialogue with tight budgets \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.

- Token-level interleaving. Alternate LM-aligned semantic tokens with phonetic/acoustic tokens in a single causal stream; fixed-ratio and plan-first variants bound latency and stabilize semantics. Examples include GLM-4-Voice (e.g., 13 text:26 speech with $\approx$0.8 s blocks), SpeechComposer's text-then-speech composition, and block-causal interleaving compatible with 12.5 Hz/2 s streaming tokenizers \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}.
- Parallel or phase-decoupled with reconciliation. Generate with an explicit text plan, then reconcile text--acoustic consistency using SALMon-style checks and stable tokenizers; shared-vocabulary interleaving scales efficiently and supports dual-resolution speech (e.g., DrVoice) \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,tan2025drvoiceparallelspeechtextvoiceconversati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask,zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends}.
- Chunked generation with windowed compute and async I/O. Fixed or overlapping windows control memory and latency for single-GPU budgets; 2 s block-causal decoding matches streaming tokenizers, and simultaneous translation uses 320/640 ms chunks with wait-k and context truncation \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
- Duplex guardrails and stop policies. Combine input/output-side defenses against audio jailbreaks with activation patching and refusal triggers; IRQ-gated interruption exposes an explicit stop token trained for $\approx$0.5 s mean delay and evaluated within [0, 1.0 s] \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.
- Intra-decoder compression schedules. Progressively discretize/downsample hidden states (e.g., dMel intensity bins or SSL k-means) and apply layer-wise merge schedules guided by transfer entropy; validate with ABX and IndicSUPERB \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.

Key knobs include read--write geometry (silent reasoning vs spoken emission), chunk size/cadence, delay control (e.g., wait-k), IRQ/stop, and intra-decoder merging. STITCH leverages long audio chunks to interleave silent reasoning with speech; choose chunk sizes aligned with tokenizer segmentation and latency budgets ($\approx$0.8 s for rapid dialogue, $\approx$2 s for stable inversion, 320/640 ms for simultaneous translation), favor LM-aware tokenization (LAST) with stable units, and apply FastAdaSP merging under any schedule \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. IndicSUPERB evidences language-specific tuning benefits for ASR/LID and speaker-focused gains from SSL pretraining; full-duplex training supports multi-turn production use \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem}.

Application templates: low-latency assistants combine SpeechRAG with adapter-based SLMs for zero-shot instruction following and multilingual SLU, using fixed-ratio interleaving with short speech blocks and IRQ-gated stops (optionally 5:5 turn-level guidance), deployable via ESPnet-SpeechLM \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. For streaming S2ST, pair wait-k with language-pair--tuned chunks; direct speech-token emission avoids text$\rightarrow$MT$\rightarrow$TTS delays and attains strong BLEU/BLASER 2.0 on CVSS-C, and 2 s text-then-speech blocks add stability; stress-test tokenizers with STAB and adapt per language family \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. Where strict text fidelity is required, plan-first schedules that predict text before speech anchor acoustic realization; compute-constrained deployments should overlay FastAdaSP-style merging \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.

\subsection{Textless S2S and two-branch designs} \label{subsec:Textless S2S and two-branch designs}
Two-branch and textless S2S architectures separate a semantic path for linguistic planning from an acoustic path for prosody, timbre, and style, enabling speech-in/speech-out without text intermediates while preserving paralinguistics and supporting unwritten or low-resource languages. The BoSS taxonomy argues for keeping speech-unit embeddings inside the SLM to jointly model affect and prosody with semantics \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. A unified S2ST LM that predicts HuBERT-based semantic units and EnCodec acoustic streams in a single decoder attains multilingual, bidirectional translation with speaker-style preservation by autoregressing the most informative stream and refining residuals non-autoregressively; syllable-synchronous semantic tokens further compress planning, and unit-vocoder stacks demonstrate textless dialogue continuation \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe,nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.

Streaming duplex variants couple a listening branch (SSL encoder + projector) and a speaking branch (decoder-only TTS + vocoder) with an explicit interruption head; middle-layer fusion preserves near-baseline intelligibility and enables barge-in, whereas early fusion degrades WER \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. When robustness is paramount, a lightweight textual intermediate can be introduced programmatically---e.g., a transcribe$\rightarrow$translate Chain-of-Thought that keeps end-to-end training and bounded-lag control \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Design choices: pace content with low-rate LM-aligned semantic tokens (LM-SPT or LAST at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz) to reduce context pressure while leaving identity and micro-prosody to the acoustic branch and decoder; favor single-decoder textless S2ST over HuBERT/EnCodec units for tight latency and joint translation--synthesis; interleave listening embeddings at middle layers and regulate emission with IRQ supervision for sub-second synchronization; when strict fidelity or cross-lingual robustness is required, fall back to a plan-first transcribe$\rightarrow$translate schedule without abandoning end-to-end learning \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste,ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces}.

















\section{Expressivity Paralinguistics and Style Control} \label{sec:Expressivity Paralinguistics and Style Control}

\subsection{Prosody, identity, and emotion modeling} \label{subsec:Prosody, identity, and emotion modeling}
Expressive SLMs such as ProsodyLM, GOAT-SLM, and SeamlessExpressiveLM aim to co-model lexical content with prosody, identity, and affect under bitrate/latency budgets, yet capability probes still surface gaps: BoSS finds weak emotion alignment and limited age-aware adaptation in omni/SLM stacks, and TTS comparisons show an expressivity--identity trade-off---Bark increases F0/rate variance but degrades speaker stability relative to VITS, with only modest gains from scaling \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}. We distill these themes in the hierarchical map shown in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_6}, which organizes the space along three top-level branches: (1) factorized architectures that preserve global style while controlling local, word-aligned prosody; (2) lightweight, streamable control surfaces and signal-aligned supervision---including adapters, interpretable heads, audio--visual fusion, and phonetic--acoustic tokenizers---that disentangle affect from identity; and (3) practical scaling with frozen foundations, interleaved multitask training, new resources/diagnostics, and rate-aware decoding that ties expressivity to lexical timing. The structure foregrounds the expressivity--identity trade-off, promotes disentangled semantic/acoustic pathways, and situates training/evaluation recipes with respect to BoSS-style capability gaps.

Consistent with branch (1) in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_6}, a pragmatic factorization separates global style from local prosody: inject an utterance-level embedding via adaptive normalization to preserve timbre/affect and add word-synchronous duration/F0 controls for emphasis, rhythm, and pitch; this improves human judgments, supports cross-lingual transfer without same-text pairs, and aligns with two-branch SLMs that keep a low-entropy semantic stream distinct from an acoustic realization stream \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. 

Aligned with branch (2), control surfaces should be lightweight and streamable. Short enrollment prompts provide global levers for identity/emotion/intonation at ultra-low token rates but risk identity drift as expressivity rises \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}. Label-efficient adapters and interpretable heads (e.g., BLSP-Emo, EMOVA, Dual-Adapter SLMs) improve affect control, while audio--visual fusion helps when acoustics alone are ambiguous \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,chen2025emovaempoweringlanguagemodelsseehearspea,wang2025dualinformationspeechlanguagemodelsemoti}. Signal-aligned supervision and light bridges between pretrained speech and text foundations are effective: time-stamped categorical/dimensional labels and phonetic-acoustic tokenizers (PAST) strengthen representations, and thin projectors/adapters add $\approx$1% parameters yet yield strong ASR/AST, contextual biasing, and zero-shot instruction following while preserving affect cues in multilingual settings \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. Identity remains fragile at low bitrates---BoSS highlights verification/diarization weaknesses---motivating disentangled channels, explicit identity heads, and decoder regularization when targeting high prosodic variance \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.

Reflecting branch (3), scaling recipes converge on initializing from strong speech/text foundations, freezing them, and training small adapters on interleaved speech--text multitask data (dialog, continuation, QA, ASR/AST, contextual biasing), prioritizing model size over token count and exploiting synthetic data \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}. New resources and diagnostics broaden training/evaluation: IndicSUPERB's Kathbath corpus across 12 languages and six SLU tasks, LM-SPT for LM-aligned low-rate semantic tokens, PAST for streamable phonetic--acoustic units, SpeechRAG for ASR-free QA in high-WER settings, and fastabx for ABX discriminability \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. Rate-aware decoding helps tether expressivity to words: multi-token prediction accelerates generation ($\approx$12$\times$), reduces WER, and enables word-aligned prosody controls \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,fan2025whatmakesgoodspeechtokenizerllmcentricge}.

Operationally---summarized by the hierarchy in \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_6} and elaborated below---the above principles yield the following actionable guidelines:
\begin{itemize}
\item Factorize style and prosody: inject a global embedding via adaptive normalization and add word-synchronous duration/F0 controls for emphasis/rhythm/pitch; this also supports cross-lingual transfer without parallel text \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.
\item Use short enrollment prompts for identity/affect and disentangle semantic vs acoustic streams so lexical tokens remain predictable while acoustic channels carry timbre/prosody; monitor identity drift as expressivity increases \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.
\item Add label-efficient emotion adapters and plug-in style heads---BLSP-Emo, EMOVA, Dual-Adapter SLMs---and validate coverage with MAD Speech; leverage audio--visual fusion when available \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,chen2025emovaempoweringlanguagemodelsseehearspea,wang2025dualinformationspeechlanguagemodelsemoti}.
\item Ground affect with time-aligned categorical/dimensional labels and phonetic-acoustic units to teach when prosody should change, improving empathetic QA and speech-in/speech-out reasoning \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
\item Evaluate along expressivity--identity axes with MOS/UTMOS, speaker similarity, SER, diarization/verification, and WER/dWER; BoSS-style testing exposes persistent gaps \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.
\end{itemize}
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_6}
\subsection{Tokenization for expressivity} \label{subsec:Tokenization for expressivity}
Expressivity-aware tokenization should expose controllable prosody, identity, and non-verbals while keeping the semantic path low-rate and predictable. Empirical probes reveal leakage: HuBERT-based units at 50 Hz retain duration/emphasis, and deduplicated content streams still carry tempo cues; coarse syllable tokens flatten prosody and lose identity after k-means, arguing for explicit style streams or conditioning and a capability blueprint with multiple inventories---lexical (V\_L), affective (V\_{AC}), contextual-dynamics (V\_{CD})---plus first-class non-verbal tokens \cite{peng2024empiricalstudyspeechlanguagemodelsprompt,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Text-aligned prosody tokens provide direct, low-rate handles: aggregating shallow ASR features at word spans preserves prosody and speaker traits for high-similarity span-level editing \cite{tseng2025tastetextalignedspeechtokenizationembedd}; prosody-sensitive discretization---training k-means on emotional speech with light temporal smoothing---improves responsiveness to emphasis while reducing speaker leakage, though energy cues may need explicit conditioning due to loudness normalization \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}.

Multi-stream and RVQ-aware designs disentangle content from style and lower next-token entropy on the semantic path. A unified tokenizer that concentrates linguistic content in RVQ-1 and paralinguistics in later RVQ layers achieves low WER with high speaker similarity; mixed local tokens plus a compact global style vector similarly preserve timbre; audio--visual discrete tokens add prosody--face coupling and protect emotion cues under masking \cite{zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Codec alignment matters: language-aware codecs improve identity and MOS-Q/S at matched bitrates relative to EnCodec-like baselines, whereas naive mixing of HuBERT with EnCodec increases style leakage, motivating losses that penalize cross-stream bleed or pairing discrete lexical backbones with compact global style vectors \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,peng2024empiricalstudyspeechlanguagemodelsprompt}. 

Low-rate constraints favor hybrid, multi-rate tokenization---keep a compact semantic/phonetic stream and allocate prosody/timbre to a slightly higher-rate stream---or adopt continuous tokenizers to preserve low/high-frequency cues, tuning frame rates per language \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,yang2025almtokenizerlowbitratesemanticrichaudioc,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. For editing and S2ST, content/style separation is especially useful: DualSpeechLM uses high-level USToken for understanding with acoustic tokens for generation, StreamUni segments policy/translation stages with speech-CoT, and STAB supplies tokenizer diagnostics predictive of downstream quality \cite{wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. LM-aware training (LAST) aligns discrete speech units with pretrained text LMs, enabling a single LM across speech and text, while PAST, LM-SPT, SpeechRAG, SLM bridging, and IndicSUPERB contribute streamable units, LM-aligned semantics, ASR-free retrieval-generation, and multilingual SLU coverage \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. 

\begin{itemize}
\item Prefer word-aligned prosody tokens (duration/F0/energy) for contrastive focus, emotion, stress, and cross-utterance consistency; train discretizers on emotional speech with light smoothing to boost prosody sensitivity and limit identity leakage; consider continuous tokenizers when discrete codes are too lossy \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext}.
\item Use multi-stream RVQ-aware interfaces: allocate RVQ-1 or local tokens to content and later layers or a compact global vector to style; encourage disentanglement with masked-channel training and validate via RVQ-1:8 resynthesis (WER, speaker-sim) and SALMon consistency \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
\item When adopting unified codecs, redistribute paralinguistic information to prevent leakage; language-aware codecs are promising defaults \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat}.
\item Do not rely on deduplication to purge prosody; explicitly audit residual tempo/duration coupling when mixing semantic and acoustic units \cite{peng2024empiricalstudyspeechlanguagemodelsprompt}.
\item Mitigate expressivity loss at low frame rates with hybrid multi-rate or continuous/context-aware tokenizers; if using syllable tokens, add an auxiliary prosody channel and consider diffusion decoding; tune frame rates per language \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,yang2025almtokenizerlowbitratesemanticrichaudioc,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.
\item Integrate non-verbals and, when possible, audio--visual streams; pair with explicit paralinguistic metadata or QA targets distilled from emotion annotations to strengthen empathetic, context-aware reasoning \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel}.
\item Report style-sensitive metrics alongside intelligibility: Sim-O, MOS-Q/S, MUSHRA; SALMon attribute checks; MAD Speech diversity; ABX-style closeness; prosody-aware diagnostics (TER under emphasis, span-edit success); and ALLM judges for affect, pace, emphasis, pitch, and non-verbals \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Control interfaces and prompts} \label{subsec:Control interfaces and prompts}
Four complementary interfaces govern paralinguistics---text instructions/attribute tags, acoustic prompting with strength scheduling, sequence-level prompt placement, and direct token/control-token manipulation---augmented by decoder-side knobs. Efficacy depends on locality, representation stability, and codec design: discrete codec inconsistencies map similar audio to multiple sequences, causing repetitions/omissions unless mitigated, and compression-first codecs (e.g., EnCodec) can harm semantic fidelity relative to semantic-aware designs like X-Codec \cite{liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio}.

Text instructions and routing provide low-latency, broadly applicable control: instruction-only empathy emerges from alignment, task/language/bitrate routing uses <trans>/<gen>, <src>/<tgt>, and stream-ID tokens <j>, and time-stamped SER metadata can improve affect-aware reasoning when gated against conversational context; a unified surface via <enroll-speech> plus x-vectors steers identity/style across tasks \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}. Acoustic prompts efficiently seed timbre and style, but homogeneity and placement matter: single-speaker, single-emotion prompts or segmented/gated conditioning outperform heterogeneous concatenation; prompt strength scales with the prompt-to-source length ratio, with $\approx$0.30 maximizing identity transfer in cross-lingual S2ST without harming translation, and prompt-guided decoding retains steerability at 6.25 Hz \cite{peng2024empiricalstudyspeechlanguagemodelsprompt,gong2024seamlessexpressivelmspeechlanguagemodele,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Placing the style prompt in a dedicated segment adjacent to the target with explicit boundaries from the textual plan reduces interference and stabilizes local controls \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.

Direct manipulation unlocks precise trade-offs and edits. Stream-ID tokens select residual codec streams to dial prosodic richness against latency/bandwidth, source-style prompts preserve timbre across S2ST, decoder temperature in flow-matching acts as a global expressivity knob, and role prompts can be composed with attribute tags and acoustic prompts for persona synthesis; when time-stamped SER exists, metadata can bias affect regionally if conflicts are mitigated \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. Fine-grained word-synchronous controls---pause insertion at punctuation, duration adjustments via alignment ratios, F0 transfer with normalization/interpolation---enable localized edits under strict budgets and complement global prompting \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. For deployment, IndicSUPERB facilitates multilingual SLU, and STITCH lets models interleave silent reasoning with speech to match latency while improving math reasoning \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}. Evaluations should couple control-compliance with semantic integrity---BLEU/BERTScore for S2ST under fixed prompt ratios/stream-IDs, instruction-only empathy with alignment-matched prompts, word-aligned metrics for pause/duration/F0 edits---and, under ultra-low rates, audits of reconstruction--generation gaps while sweeping prompt conditioning and decoder temperature \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.

\begin{itemize}
\item Text instructions and routing: standard empathy prompts; <trans>/<gen>, <src>/<tgt>, and <j> tokens for task/language/bitrate control \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.
\item Acoustic prompting: use single-speaker, single-emotion prompts or segmented/gated conditioning; set ratio $\approx$0.30 for strong identity transfer; employ prompt-guided decoding at ultra-low rates \cite{peng2024empiricalstudyspeechlanguagemodelsprompt,gong2024seamlessexpressivelmspeechlanguagemodele,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.
\item Placement/boundaries: dedicate a prompt segment adjacent to the target speech and separate content/style intents with explicit boundaries \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.
\item Local edits: pause, duration, and pitch controls aligned to words under tight budgets \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.
\item Decoder-side expressivity: increase flow-matching noise temperature to raise prosodic diversity \cite{yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul}.
\item Metadata prompting: inject time-stamped emotion labels when available and gate or down-weight on conflict \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Evaluation and watermarking} \label{subsec:Evaluation and watermarking}
Assessment should jointly probe instruction-following for style, paralinguistic fidelity, prosodic diversity, and social cue handling, while enabling provenance checks. Audio-aware LLMs scale open-ended judging: on StyleSet, Gemini-2.5-Pro correlates with humans on voice-style instruction-following and role-play and consistently ranks GPT-4o-audio strongest on style \cite{chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe}. Broader suites complement automatic and LLM-based judging: LLaSO-Eval scores emotion/gender/age/accent with closed-set metrics and uses GPT-4o judges with abstention auditing; TELEVAL decouples explicit semantics from paralinguistics and implicit semantics in dialogue to score empathy, dialect-consistent generation, and age-aware style; BoSS fuses GPT-4o style judgments with Emotion2Vec-based audio scoring to reveal dialect and affect controllability gaps \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. 

Prosodic diversity can be tracked distributionally by clustering pitch/voicing/energy and comparing synthesized versus training distributions with NDB/JSD; acoustic BPE increases diversity without extra labels \cite{li2024effectivenessacousticbpedecoderonlytts}. MAD Speech quantifies diversity across identity (voice, gender) and expressivity (emotion, accent, background noise), reporting e.g., SoundStorm > AudioLM on several facets, with Best-of-K reducing and higher temperature increasing diversity; SALMon adds attribute-consistency and text-match checks \cite{futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Behavioral probes capture spontaneity/domain sensitivity: Bark produces more fillers and longer pauses than VITS and scores higher on read but not conversational speech, indicating domain-specific trade-offs \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}. For provenance and edit detection, SSR-Speech embeds frame-synchronous watermarks ($\approx$50 Hz) that detect edited regions with 99.9% binary accuracy and minimal quality impact, aligning with unit/codec interfaces for post-hoc verification \cite{wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot}. 

A reporting checklist improves comparability: include SALMon-style acoustic factors (emotion, background noise, speaker identity, room acoustics); tokenizer/model diagnostics predictive of downstream performance (e.g., STAB) and multi-task benchmarks such as IndicSUPERB; and rigorous provenance (languages, hours, contributors, geographic coverage, conversational setup), following practices from Taiwanese Mandarin spoken LLMs \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem}. Aligning intrinsic and extrinsic evaluations---e.g., correlating STAB metrics with IndicSUPERB SLU outcomes across 12 languages---strengthens interpretability and cross-task comparisons, while ALLM judges, TELEVAL, LLaSO-Eval, BoSS, and StyleSet jointly triangulate style controllability versus stochastic diversity \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe,li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan,sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.

















\section{Real Time Streaming and Duplex Dialogue} \label{sec:Real Time Streaming and Duplex Dialogue}

\noindent Real-time agents must coordinate perception, planning, and synthesis across concurrent input/output channels under strict latency, bandwidth, and compute limits. A systems-first view treats latency as the primary constraint: decompose end-to-end delay into capture, buffering, perception/prefill, time-to-first-audio, and steady-state generation; then optimize three levers---(i) chunking and read--write schedules that cap look-ahead while preserving coherence, (ii) low-rate, streamable token interfaces that shrink conditioning length and bandwidth, and (iii) budgeted inference that cuts prefill and decoding cost without sacrificing intelligibility or style. These principles structure the subsequent subsections on latency-aware design, streaming policies, duplex turn-taking, and architectures.



\subsection{Latency-aware design} \label{subsec:Latency-aware design}

Interactive stacks benefit from streamable tokenizers and dual-stream decoders that avoid heavy compression or cascaded MT/TTS, thereby reducing first-packet delay and average latency across ASR, S2ST, and duplex chat \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}. In practice, three levers dominate.

Chunking, buffering, and read--write schedules. With causal tokenizers, short interleaved cycles deliver fast onset: GLM-4-Voice starts speaking after $\approx$10 speech tokens with a text-first plan; aligning assistant text insertion to the first speech chunk index $k_{start}$ and setting $\tau_{SC}=5$ at 12.5 Hz ($\approx$400 ms) balances semantic planning and responsiveness in duplex chat \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. For SimulST, fixed word-lag k with 320--640 ms chunks exposes latency--quality frontiers, while semantic truncation bounds context for long streams \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Low-rate tokenization and streamable interfaces. Lower cadence reduces both prefill and per-step cost. A 12.5 Hz discrete interface with block-causal attention in tokenizer/decoder is a robust default for interleaved training and streaming synthesis, sustaining $\approx$25k text-to-token throughput on H800 \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Ultra-low-rate codecs such as TaDiCodec (6.25 Hz, 0.0875 kbps) with few-step diffusion decoding achieve RTF 0.12--0.29, enabling compute- and bandwidth-constrained operation; training-free, chunk-wise expansion supports online streamability with short windows ($T_{chunk} \approx 1$ s, $T_{shift} \approx 0.4$ s) and compact primary codebooks to shorten LM-facing sequences \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}. Coarser, syllabic segmentation provides linear-time pacing compatible with 5--12.5 Hz streams \cite{cho2025sylbersyllabicembeddingrepresentationspe}.

Budgeted inference: prefill reduction and fast decoding. Prefill dominates long-context latency; capping it requires shorter conditioning and KV-efficient decoders. Step-size distillation reduces diffusion steps (e.g., 100$\rightarrow$4) while maintaining WER/DNSMOS under real-time budgets \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. LSLM meets duplex constraints with a lightweight discrete interface, a compact decoder ($\approx$106M), and a streaming encoder ($\approx$34M), using AR top-p sampling on live embeddings for rapid interruption handling \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. On recognition paths, CTC sparsity enables online prompting by emitting only salient encoder states \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Aligning tokenizer and decoder interfaces (e.g., 12.5 Hz, block-causal attention, chunked emission) simplifies end-to-end control \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

First-chunk policies and reporting. Deterministic read--write alternation with short text plans and small speech blocks minimizes time-to-first-audio; GLM-4-Voice's $\approx$10-token onset and $\tau_{SC}=5$ at 12.5 Hz are concrete targets \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. Report wall-clock effects and schedules jointly: fixed word-lag k and chunk sizes in SimulST \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}; token-to-speech thresholds and interleaving ratios in duplex chat \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends}; whether CTC sparsity is used \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}; and, for generation, token rate (12.5 vs 6.25 Hz), bitrate, decoder family, and diffusion steps, noting that few-step schedules and ultra-low-rate tokens markedly lower RTF and bandwidth.

Tooling and benchmarks. ESPnet-SpeechLM standardizes data/pretraining/inference \cite{tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo}. VoxEval probes spoken QA/reasoning under varied acoustics \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}; SALMon adds acoustic awareness and evaluation robustness \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}; Taiwanese Mandarin spoken LLMs demonstrate real-time, full-duplex multi-turn behavior \cite{yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem}. IndicSUPERB (1,684 h; 12 languages) highlights SLU gains with language-specific SSL; LM-SPT and PAST yield semantically aligned, streamable tokens; SpeechRAG enables ASR-free retrieval-generation under high WER \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}. For streaming TTS/S2ST, prefer $\approx$12.5 Hz discrete/semantic tokens with block-causal attention and interleave textual planning; continuous tokens (Cont-SPT) and diffusion-assisted decoding sustain quality at modest rates \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.



\subsection{Streaming policies for ASR/S2ST} \label{subsec:Streaming policies for ASR/S2ST}

Streaming policies decide when to read audio and write tokens, coupling encoder chunking, tokenizer cadence, and decoder scheduling. Four recurring strategies---wait-k, read--write alternation, incremental decoding with request-more-audio control, and duration-synchronous generation---integrate naturally with streamable encoders (Emformer, HuBERT/data2vec), fixed-rate discrete tokenizers (e.g., RepCodec, SpeechTokenizer), and unified SLM decoders \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar}.

Wait-k and prefix-to-prefix. BESTOW samples K$\sim$U(3,12) during training and sets task-specific K at inference (e.g., K=6 for ASR), combining look-ahead L=4 with pre-decision steps P=8 and supporting uni/bidirectional streaming to hit latency targets \cite{chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem}. SimulS2S-LLM derives a test-time wait-k from CIF prompt length, stabilizing prefix decoding via frame-synchronous CTC, incremental beam search, and shallow-fused n-gram LM \cite{deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc}. In shared tasks, EOS-to-request-more-audio realizes incremental reading with a latency multiplier for soft wait-k; StreamUni further gates generation by transcription growth and prunes served history via semantic truncation \cite{ouyang2025cmusiwslt2025simultaneousspeechtranslati,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Read--write alternation. In plan-before-speak assistants, emission after every R text tokens yields deterministic cycles and prompt audio onset; first-chunk latency decomposes as LLM(R)+TTS(W)+FlowMatching(W)+Vocoder(2W), exposing tunable trade-offs over R and W \cite{fang2025llamaomni2llmbasedrealtimespokenchatbota}. StreamUni complements these cycles with transcription-driven gates and semantic truncation and reports AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL using mWERSegmenter \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Incremental decoding with request-more-audio. Textless streaming ST couples an Emformer encoder (e.g., 20/4 or 32/6 segment/right-context) with an RNN-T decoder that streams semantic tokens at 25 Hz with blanks and beam search \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. EOS-driven request-more-audio loops and latency multipliers stabilize decoding under noise and long-tail inputs; frame-synchronous CTC with shallow fusion maintains bounded lag \cite{ouyang2025cmusiwslt2025simultaneousspeechtranslati,deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc}.

Duration-synchronous generation. SyncSpeech aligns emission to predicted durations with look-ahead q=1, enabling true online dual-stream decoding; unlike full-context NAR flow/diffusion, it supports continuous generation under read--write/wait-k \cite{sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}.

Integration and diagnostics. Emformer chunk geometries provide low-latency features; CIF prompts expose label-synchronous cues for test-time wait-k; RNN-T at 25 Hz yields fixed-rate streams; R and W standardize first-chunk auditing \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc,fang2025llamaomni2llmbasedrealtimespokenchatbota}. Report AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL with mWERSegmenter, the LLM/TTS/Flow/Vocoder first-chunk decomposition, and use incremental beam with frame-synchronous CTC and shallow fusion as a stability reference \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc,fang2025llamaomni2llmbasedrealtimespokenchatbota}.

Design guidelines and complementary advances. Under tight budgets, train with sampled K and small look-ahead (e.g., L=4), pick task-specific K at inference, pair with streaming encoders and incremental CTC beam search, and exploit accessible SSL and codec front-ends \cite{ouyang2025cmusiwslt2025simultaneousspeechtranslati,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,dhawan2024codecasrtrainingperformantautomaticspeec,sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}. Standardize first-chunk reporting via LLM/TTS/Flow/Vocoder, tune R,W jointly, and favor duration-synchronous emission for continuity \cite{fang2025llamaomni2llmbasedrealtimespokenchatbota,sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}. Evaluation should combine IndicSUPERB SLU, VoxEval, tokenizer assessment (STAB), and SALMon stress tests \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Robustness improves with dMel tokens for training-free resilience, mitigation of codec DRI to prevent repetition/omission, and alignment-guided sequencing to suppress infinite silences \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,song2024ellavstableneuralcodeclanguagemodelingal}.



\subsection{Duplex and turn-taking} \label{subsec:Duplex and turn-taking}

Full-duplex agents must listen while speaking, support barge-in, govern overlap, schedule backchannels, detect interruptions, and decide turn boundaries via learnable streaming policies realized by duplex S2S architectures that fuse user/agent audio, turn-level text planning, and streaming chain-of-thought \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Three paradigms dominate: token-level control in a unified decoder, direct modeling of turn events without external VAD, and distributionally grounded scheduling that matches human turn statistics.

Barge-in and interruption detection. Lightweight interruption heads/tokens enable sub-second reactions.
