A listening-while-speaking decoder with an IRQ token holds low probability during normal speech and spikes on true interruptions until sampled, achieving interactivity F1 of 95.50% (clean) and 85.15% (noise), with noise/speaker mismatch mitigated by noise-hardening and domain-balanced training \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. SALM-Duplex couples architectural support with barge-in augmentation---cutting agent speech and inserting $\approx$0.64 s silence---and ignores false alarms if the user stops within 0.1 s, reaching 83.0% success on UltraChat and 94.5% on an ``Impatient'' suite with lower latency than Moshi \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl}. End-of-turn gating offers a lightweight alternative, triggering responses via end-of-turn token probability plus a silence counter while allowing immediate user preemption \cite{yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem}.

Overlap handling and floor control. Learned overlap caps regulate co-speaking rather than eliminating it. SLIDE inserts short silences to bound predicted overlap ($\le$0.3 s when forecasts exceed 0.6 s), producing human-like IPU/pause/gap/overlap distributions and outperforming cascaded baselines \cite{lu2025slideintegratingspeechlanguagemodelllmsp}. VAD-based diagnostics show generated dialogues can match human distributions even before large-scale augmentation, indicating that streamable scheduling provides a strong prior \cite{fu2024investigatingeffectslargescalepseudoster}. For safety-critical or embodied agents, predictable half-duplex remains effective and well-rated \cite{fu2025personalizedsociallyassistiverobotsendsp}.

Backchanneling and non-verbal cues. Explicit backchannel policies tied to internal state improve grounding; schedule backchannel tokens under overlap caps and map conversational states to motor behaviors in embodied systems (e.g., listening$\rightarrow$nodding, speaking$\rightarrow$head motion, idle$\rightarrow$breathing) to stabilize floor exchanges \cite{lu2025slideintegratingspeechlanguagemodelllmsp,fu2025personalizedsociallyassistiverobotsendsp}.

Policy learning vs inference-time control. Next-token--level supervision internalizes turn-taking beyond thresholds. NTPP jointly predicts next-token pairs to model overlaps, backchannels, and interruptions without external VAD; an interruption suite with 400 synthetic scenarios evaluates ``speak-up'' and interruption responses, and channel swapping validates speaker independence \cite{wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual}. TurnGuide inserts brief plan-first text just before assistant speech, preserving duplex dynamics and improving coherence, with Candor correlations on IPU/pause/gap/overlap comparable or superior to token-synchronous baselines \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}.

Evaluation methodology and training signals. Use pyannote.audio VAD to compute time-normalized per-channel IPU/pause/gap/overlap counts/durations; closeness to human distributions diagnoses scheduling quality \cite{lu2025slideintegratingspeechlanguagemodelllmsp}. For interruptions, report barge-in success, end-to-end interruption latency, and interactivity F1 under clean/noisy regimes, apply standardized false-alarm rules, stress-test speaker independence via channel swapping, and avoid contaminated test sets \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual}. Style--role suites (e.g., StyleSet) cover sequential turn-taking but not overlap/barge-in; duplex-native evaluations and turn-level correlations on conversational corpora (e.g., Candor) fill the gap \cite{chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. Targeted data---barge-in augmentation (truncate speech, insert $\approx$0.64 s silence), synthetic interruption suites, inference-only bootstraps (end-of-turn thresholds, silence counters)---combines with integrated policies (overlap caps, IRQ heads, token-pair objectives) and direct duplex S2S fusion to yield natural alternation, timely backchannels, and reliable barge-in under noise with modest data \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta}. Operational reporting should include dataset provenance/scale (e.g., IndicSUPERB 1,684 h/12 languages), SLU coverage, tokenizer quality (STAB), and duplex performance under SALMon-style perturbations with contamination-checked holds \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem}.



\subsection{Architectures for streaming and duplex} \label{subsec:Architectures for streaming and duplex}

Deployable stacks share three strata: (i) streamable perception/tokenization that emits low-latency, label- or chunk-synchronous cues; (ii) duplex-aware decoders that interleave listening/speaking with shared caches and channel-aware control; and (iii) chunk-aware synthesis that renders partial acoustic tokens with bounded look-ahead. Representative systems include SALM-Duplex, NTPP, SyncSpeech, Ichigo-style unified decoders, and Stitch, which interleaves unspoken reasoning with spoken chunks to ``think while talking''; other paths adapt text LLMs (e.g., TWIST) or unify understanding/generation via dual speech tokens and Chain-of-Condition training (e.g., DualSpeechLM) \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta}. A schematic overview is provided in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_7}, which summarizes deployable streaming-duplex SLM stacks as three coordinated strata and makes explicit the dataflow across them: arrows indicate the progression from low-rate, streamable tokens into interleaved, duplex-aware decoding (e.g., middle fusion, shared KV, and turn planning) and onward to chunked synthesis (e.g., dual-stream TTS and fixed-size playback). By enforcing duration/span-synchronous schedules that keep first-audio low, the depicted pipeline supports ``think-while-talking'' and responsive barge-in under unified, rate-aware scheduling, mirroring the design space surveyed here.

Streamable encoders and tokenizers. Text-initialized decoders with block-causal streaming attention in the speech tokenizer and block-wise 2 s speech decoding support online S2S under a single vocabulary; training-free chunk-wise expansion further eases duplex deployment \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,chang2024dcspinspeakerinvariantspeechtokenizerspo}. For SimulST, streaming CoT over an LSLM conditions chunk-wise transcription, maintains a truncation queue, and generates translations under delay-controlled rules, achieving state-of-the-art SimulST/StreamST and transferring across base LSLMs \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Interleaved, duplex-aware decoders. A listening-while-speaking LM fuses a streaming vq-wav2vec encoder with a decoder-only TTS stack via middle fusion at every block, balancing speech quality and duplex responsiveness (WER $\approx$4--4.5%; interactivity F1 up to 98%) \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. SALM-Duplex trains a unified decoder over discrete speech tokens while conditioning on live user encodings for barge-in/overlap; NTPP adds channel embeddings, pair-wise causal masking across channels, and depth-aware RVQ masking so one KV cache serves both sides through overlaps and interruptions \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual}. Text-initialized stacks interleave short ``plan'' bursts with speech chunks under a single next-token objective to keep caches warm across modalities \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

Chunk-aware, low-look-ahead synthesis. SyncSpeech emits duration-synchronized updates in a dual-stream decoder, avoiding full-context NAR passes \cite{sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}. Stitch exploits playback slack by chunking language and acoustic decoders, sustaining simultaneous thinking-and-talking under fixed chunk sizes \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}. The same chunking idiom composes with block-causal tokenizers and 2 s decode windows to keep first-audio low and flow continuous \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

Turn-aware I/O interleaving. TurnGuide interleaves user/assistant channels at the chunk level ($\tau_{SC}=5$ at 12.5 Hz) with UID/AID markers, preserving per-channel timing and enabling barge-in/overlap; assistant turns align with pyannote VAD, Whisper timestamps, and punctuation inside a GLM-4-Voice backbone \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. Chunk-level alternation with token-level read--write ratios bounds first-audio while reusing a single KV cache.

Blueprint. Perception: select streamable encoders/tokenizers (block-causal tokenizer + 2 s decode windows or training-free chunk expansion). Core: use a single decoder with shared KV, middle fusion for listening, channel embeddings and pair-wise masks for overlap control, and brief text ``plan'' bursts interleaved with speech. Synthesis: adopt unit/flow/diffusion decoders with duration- or span-synchronous schedules to minimize look-ahead \cite{sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}. For SimulST/StreamST, layer streaming CoT and delay-controlled rules on the same backbone \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. These ingredients underpin SALM-Duplex, NTPP, SyncSpeech, Ichigo-style stacks, and Stitch-like thinking-and-talking, and have been exercised in real-time assistants with low WER, high interactivity F1, and responsive barge-in under unified, rate-aware scheduling \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_7}








\section{Datasets Benchmarks and Tasks} \label{sec:Datasets Benchmarks and Tasks}

This section consolidates datasets, benchmarks, and task formulations spanning ASR/SLU, S2TT/S2ST, TTS/resynthesis, paralinguistics, audio--visual speech, and duplex dialogue. We systematize corpora by modality, domain, and language coverage; outline common construction/split conventions and tokenization settings; map tasks to datasets/metrics; and compile benchmark suites with multilingual, robustness, streaming, and reproducibility considerations \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.

\subsection{Core corpora and data taxonomy} \label{subsec:Core corpora and data taxonomy}

Modality and domain. Resources cover time-aligned ASR/SLU speech--text; S2TT and S2ST (textless and text-conditioned); TTS/vocoder and unit/codec resynthesis; interleaved speech--text instructions for mixed-token training; paralinguistics/speaker; and synchronized audio--visual speech, mapping information types (phonetic, semantic, paralinguistic/prosody), temporal scales, and acoustic conditions to evaluation pathways such as ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, and KWS \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,wang2024c3llmconditionalmultimodalcontentgenerat,wang2024whydospeechlanguagemodelsfailgeneratesem}. Read/studio speech supports phonetic probing and high-fidelity resynthesis; conversational/in-the-wild captures overlaps/noise; expressive/emotional material targets prosody/affect; audio--visual corpora probe alignment and fusion robustness \cite{futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. ABX analyses quantify phonetic/speaker/room invariances with efficient tooling \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.

Language coverage. Corpora span English-only, multilingual, regional families (e.g., Indic), and low-resource settings. IndicSUPERB contributes 1,684 h across 12 languages with six SLU tasks and standardized splits, where language-specific SSL markedly outperforms FBANK, especially for LID and SV/SID \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. fastabx accelerates cross-lingual ABX probes \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.

Tokenization stacks. LM-aware tokens (LAST), LM-aligned semantic distillation (LM-SPT), phonetic--acoustic tokens (PAST), dMel, and continuous tokenizers (Cont-SPT) provide compact, robust, and streamable interfaces for joint speech--text modeling with improved fidelity/latency \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.

Representative corpora.
- English read/conversational: LibriSpeech/LibriLight/LibriHeavy, GigaSpeech; Hub5'00 for conversational evaluation; decoder-only ASR often pairs 5k/2k BPE with LibriSpeech/Fisher text for text-only LM updates \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.
- Multilingual ASR: Common Voice, VoxPopuli, AISHELL, MLS; FLEURS supports tokenizer assessment (STAB) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
- Production-style multilingual ASR: MLC-SLM 2025 (~1.5k h, 11 languages) with balanced dev; paired speaker sets (CN-Celeb1/2, VoxBlink/2) for embeddings \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}.
- S2TT/S2ST: CoVoST2, Europarl-ST, Fisher Es--En; CVSS-C/T for waveform-to-waveform S2ST and style preservation; Heroes/Mined Audiobook for dubbing/audiobook expressivity \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.
- TTS/resynthesis: LibriTTS, VCTK, LJSpeech, Hi-Fi TTS, CMU Arctic for unit/codec decoders, intelligibility (WER/dWER), speaker similarity, and naturalness \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste}.
- Interleaved speech--text: LibriLight, VoxPopuli, TED-LIUM, People's Speech, Spoken Wikipedia, MLS, plus large text and synthetic speech tokens for unified next-token training \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.
- Speaker-centric: VoxCeleb1/2, CN-Celeb1/2, VoxBlink/2; BoSS adds dialect and non-verbals for beyond-semantic evaluation \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.
- Emotion/prosody: IEMOCAP, RAVDESS, CREMA-D, DFEW, Aff-Wild2; ESD-zh and BoSS non-verbals; ProsodyLM targets word-level prosody \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
- Audio--visual: VoxCeleb2, GRID, LRS3; AV emotion (RAVDESS/CREMA-D/DFEW/Aff-Wild2); VQ-MAE-AV and BLSP-Emo exemplify multimodal pretraining and empathetic generation \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}.

ASR-adjacent stacks. Transcript-free SpeechRAG aligns speech--text embeddings for direct retrieval and audio-conditioned generation, mitigating ASR error propagation; TWIST and tokenizer progress (PAST, LM-SPT, LAST) improve recognition/generation and enable low-rate streaming \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}.

Construction and splits. Standard ASR uses LibriSpeech dev/test-clean; contamination in LibriSpeech/Common Voice motivates audited holds; 16 kHz is common for ASR/tokenizers, 24 kHz for TTS/codecs; token frame rate governs information retention and varies by language \cite{tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom,li2024effectivenessacousticbpedecoderonlytts}. Rate--distortion and unit discriminability should be profiled across codebook sizes/frame rates; LM-aware, token-aligned designs preserve semantics at low rates and scale with synthetic data \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,guo2025fastlongspeechenhancinglargespeechlangua}.

\subsection{Task--dataset--metric mapping} \label{subsec:Task--dataset--metric mapping}

Scope and defaults. We map task families to representative datasets, metrics, and protocol knobs, emphasizing instruction-style setups, rate-/latency-aware controls, and duplex diagnostics \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.

ASR and multilingual ASR. Datasets: LibriSpeech, Common Voice, VoxPopuli, AISHELL, Fisher, MLC-SLM 2025; Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2 frames ASR as QA-style sequence generation with multi-audio inputs \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}. Metrics: WER/CER/WCER; diarization-aware t-cpWER/t-cpCER and DER when segmentation is joint \cite{kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}. Knobs: contextual biasing via entity lists/few-shot and dynamic prompting ($\approx$9--20% WER gains), capped streaming segments, SpeechRAG for high-WER regimes, and per-language reporting \cite{sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}.

S2TT: offline, SimulST, StreamST. Datasets: CoVoST2, Europarl-ST, MuST-C, SimulST/StreamST shared tasks \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem}. Metrics: SacreBLEU/COMET and AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL via SimulEval \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Knobs: publish framing/chunking, read--write policy (e.g., wait-k), prefix alignment/gating, tokenization path/rate, codec family/semantic fidelity, and truncation/segment rules to avoid skips \cite{ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}.

Textless S2ST with style. Datasets: CVSS-C/T; SALMon probes acoustic/text consistency; curated dubbing/audiobook sets support expressivity \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Metrics: ASR-BLEU, SALMon ranking, ABX invariance, speaker similarity/EER, prosody measures, MAD Speech diversity, WER/dWER for intelligibility \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,wang2024whydospeechlanguagemodelsfailgeneratesem}. Knobs: disclose unit/codec, token rate/bitrate, and enrollment prompts \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.

TTS, zero-shot TTS, VC, resynthesis. Datasets: LibriTTS, VCTK, CMU Arctic, VCC, LJSpeech/HiFi-TTS; use uncontaminated LibriTTS-testsetB as relevant \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}. Metrics: WER/dWER, MOS/UTMOS/DNSMOS, FAD, speaker similarity, SALMon for attribute control, MAD diversity, unsupervised ABX \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge}. Knobs: $\approx$3 s enrollment for zero-shot; distinguish continuation vs cross-speaker; report decoder family/steps for latency \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.

Spoken classification and paralinguistics. Datasets: Speech Commands (KWS), IEMOCAP/RAVDESS/CREMA-D (ER), Voxforge/FLEURS (LID), MUStARD (dialogue acts) \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Metrics: accuracy or macro-F1 on clean, contamination-free splits \cite{tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}. Knobs: specify few-/zero-shot splits and prompt formats; MUSAN augmentation for interruption/command detection \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Enhancement and separation. Datasets: VoiceBank-DEMAND, LibriMix/Libri2Mix, LibriSpeech mixtures with DNS/MUSAN noise and reverberation \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage}. Metrics: PESQ, STOI, SI-SDRi, and downstream PER/WER transfer to SUPERB/IndicSUPERB heads \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Knobs: label/computation sweeps; supervised vs SSL (HuBERT) front-ends; discrete-token pipelines with universal vocoders and adversarial robustness \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel,djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains}.

Spoken language modeling and interleaved speech--text. Datasets: Spoken StoryCloze/Topic StoryCloze, spoken QA (WebQuestions, LLaMA Questions, TriviaQA), and synthetic interleavings \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Metrics: length-normalized likelihood, QA accuracy, WER for intermediate transcripts, LLM-graded outputs. Knobs: modality paths, token rates (e.g., 12.5 Hz), interleaving templates.

Spoken dialogue generation and duplex interaction. Datasets: J-CHAT (68,892 h, diarized, no transcripts) for textless dialog \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Metrics: MOS for naturalness/meaningfulness, dWER for unit resynthesis, barge-in success/latency, turn-taking stats (VAD/IPU/overlap), and chunked-reasoning latency \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}. Knobs: diarization-based prompts, unit vocoders + speaker embeddings, long-context prompts, IRQ tokens/end-of-turn thresholds, chunk schedules, token rate/bitrate, and MUSAN noise on listening paths \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.

Social/affective capabilities. BoSS evaluates audio QA, dialect following, multi-turn memory, age adaptation, and emotion comprehension/generation; report zero-shot vs prompted styles \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.

Unified multi-task benchmarking. Combine IndicSUPERB (12 languages; ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, KWS), STAB for tokenizer assessment, and VoxEval-style SpeechQA; disclose tokenizer I/O budgets, end-to-end latency, and robustness to acoustic variation \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.




\subsection{Benchmark suites and leaderboards} \label{subsec:Benchmark suites and leaderboards}

\input{benchmark_table}

We synthesize recent benchmark suites and leaderboards for spoken language modeling into three complementary branches, as visualized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_8}. This organization proceeds from broad, instruction-style capability suites, through dialogue-, style-, and diversity-oriented evaluations, to specialized fronts that stress robustness and system design. The layout in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_8} highlights both the complementary coverage across these branches and the logical progression from general competence to interaction quality and, finally, to engineering concerns. Table \ref{tab:benchmark_table} summarizes the principal benchmarks referenced in this subsection, situating their scale, domains, task formats, and evaluation metrics to support comparison across instruction-style suites, speech recognition and synthesis, spoken language modeling, and robustness-oriented evaluations. To anchor the discussion of benchmark suites and leaderboards, Table \ref{tab:benchmark_table} consolidates the principal benchmarks referenced in this section, summarizing their domains, task formats, sizes, and evaluation metrics for direct comparison. Table \ref{tab:benchmark_table} consolidates the principal benchmarks surveyed in this subsection, contextualizing their scale, domains, task formats, and evaluation metrics to support comparisons across instruction-style suites, speech recognition and synthesis tasks, and robustness studies.

Instruction-style, multi-task suites. Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2 aggregates 180 instruction-following tasks across speech/music/environmental audio with open pipelines, domain-level aggregation, and a compact core; streamable SLMs (e.g., BESTOW) show strong content/speaker skills but headroom in paralinguistics/robustness, and instruction-tuned systems (e.g., DeSTA2) adopt its scoring \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem,lu2025desta2developinginstructionfollowingspee}. In parallel with these global suites, IndicSUPERB provides regional multi-task standardization, extending the first branch to diverse linguistic settings \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.

Dialogue-, style-, and diversity-focused evaluations. TELEVAL targets Chinese interactive scenarios across knowledge QA, dialect following, memory, paralinguistics, and spoken response quality, surfacing failure modes under dialect shifts, low SNR, and packet loss \cite{li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan}. SALMon offers fast, model-based ranking for acoustic awareness and text--audio alignment, while MAD Speech audits acoustic diversity and mode collapse \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity}. Audio-aware LLM judges for style control are also emerging within this branch \cite{chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe}.

Specialized robustness and design fronts. VoxEval delivers speech-in/speech-out knowledge QA across 56 MMLU subjects with 153k audio variants over 26 conditions and spoken math; cascades remain competitive while end-to-end SLMs exhibit large spread, underscoring robustness and spoken reasoning gaps \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}. Discrete-token benchmarks further probe representation choices: DASB standardizes recognition/generation under bitrate control, finding semantic tokens outperform compression tokens yet trail continuous features; STAB measures invariance, robustness, compressibility, and vocabulary dynamics without transcripts, correlating with downstream ASR/AST/ER/SID/LID, and runs quickly on CPU \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. These studies complement codec/vocoder leaderboards and rate-aware SLM evaluations \cite{guo2025recentadvancesdiscretespeechtokensreview}. LongSpeech-Eval standardizes minute-scale QA and perceptual drift assessments \cite{park2025longformspeechgenerationspokenlanguagemo}, and an SLM-TTS benchmark codifies six axes---speaking style, intelligibility, speaker consistency, prosody, spontaneous behaviors, and human judgments---with public artifacts \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.

Reporting conventions. To ensure comparability across the above branches, use official splits; provide per-task/per-language breakdowns; report multiple seeds with variance; fix scripts and clarify licensing; and prohibit test-time tuning. Disclose instruction modality/provenance, abstention rates, token rate/bitrate, segmentation strategy, and vocabulary size for token interfaces \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,noroozi2024instructiondatagenerationunsupervisedada}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_8}



\subsection{Multilinguality, robustness, and streaming} \label{subsec:Multilinguality, robustness, and streaming}

Multilingual breadth. IndicSUPERB (Kathbath: 1,684 h, 12 languages, 1,218 speakers across 203 districts) provides clean/noisy and known/unknown-speaker splits with gender balance, exposing ASR robustness gaps to noise/unknown speakers, heterogeneous SV/SID robustness across encoders, and strong LID robustness \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. J-CHAT offers 68,892 h of spontaneous Japanese dialogue via Whisper LID, pyannote diarization, and Demucs enhancement, with a reusable in-the-wild recipe \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.

Cross-lingual generalization and alignment. OTReg improves S2TT robustness to domain/language mismatch on CoVoST2/FLEURS while preserving LibriSpeech/MLS performance \cite{xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}. Encoder--Adaptor--LLM stacks trained on ~1,500 h across 11 languages yield larger gains for East/Southeast Asian languages under progressive alignment, highlighting language-aware bridging and segmentation effects \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}.

Dialect and beyond-semantic robustness. BoSS introduces five Chinese dialects and non-verbals to evaluate recognition and audio responses; augmenting ASR with dialect TTS dramatically reduces WER (e.g., Cantonese 89.54%$\rightarrow$26.48%$\rightarrow$23.83%) \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Preference-aligned autoregressive tokenizations sustain quality under out-of-domain speaker transfer \cite{zhang2024speechalignaligningspeechgenerationhuman}.

Paralinguistics. Adaptor-based speech$\rightarrow$LLM bridges preserve multilingual affect without discretization; English-only training achieves Chinese CER 33.3 vs 26.4 ground truth, revealing partial transfer and remaining gaps \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,zhu2024generativepretrainedspeechlanguagemodele}.

Streaming and long-form. StreamUni operationalizes streaming chain-of-thought with truncation-only inference on long talks, matching human segmentation COMET across directions/backbones and demonstrating schedule portability \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Textless streaming S2ST with an acoustic LM + codec attains state-of-the-art BLEU/latency on CVSS-C \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. FastLongSpeech improves long-form processing via iterative fusion and dynamic compression \cite{guo2025fastlongspeechenhancinglargespeechlangua}.

Recommended evaluation matrix. Combine IndicSUPERB for multi-task SLU, SALMon for acoustic awareness and text--audio alignment, STAB for tokenizer profiling, and VoxEval for end-to-end SpeechQA/spoken reasoning; report per-language/dialect deltas (clean/noisy, known/unknown speakers, augmentation), long-form segmentation policies, token rates/bitrates and streaming schedules (chunk size, stride, look-ahead), and pair quality with latency (AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL, time-to-first-audio) \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}.

\subsection{Data quality and reproducibility} \label{subsec:Data quality and reproducibility}

Design principles. Standardized, auditable splits and scoring, contamination control, and open evaluation code enable apples-to-apples comparisons across linguistic and acoustic factors \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.

Splits, stratification, leakage prevention. LLaSO-Eval constructs held-out, stratified splits from LLaSO-Instruct with explicit scope limits; BabySLM enforces voice-disjoint dev/test and balanced phone statistics; IndicSUPERB publishes clean/noisy and known/unknown-speaker splits at 16 kHz with rich metadata; TurnGuide guards against dialog leakage with explicit Fisher/Candor partitions; Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2 is test-only and vettable; instruction corpora should filter sources unsuited for vocalization and low-value outputs \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,lavechin2023babyslmlanguageacquisitionfriendlybenchm,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand,shu2023llasmlargelanguagespeechmodel}.

Contamination checks and statistics. Pretraining leakage in LibriSpeech/Common Voice can depress perplexity or inflate scores; adopt strict decontamination, non-overlapping holds, bootstrap CIs, and multi-seed aggregation \cite{tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}. VoxEval standardizes hyperparameters and applies Friedman/Wilcoxon with Bonferroni \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}. Listening tests require controlled confounds, sufficient raters, and CIs; diversity metrics benefit from multi-seed averaging given label noise \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity}.

Provenance, licensing, privacy. LLaSO releases alignment/instruction pairs and a reference model; open S2S/VoiceTextBlender efforts release code/data/weights for augmentation and alignment \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem}. When full release is infeasible, document restrictions and evaluator calibration (e.g., SpeechGuard), watermark synthetic audio, and enforce ASR-based quality gates post-TTS with reported objective scores \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,zhao2024advancingspeechlanguagemodelsscalingsupe}. Record maker--checker verification, denoising, and transcript correction; curated dubbing/audiobook sets denoise with DEMUCS and include human filtering \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Tokenizer pipelines should specify encoders, codebooks, languages, and retention vs k-means baselines (e.g., RepCodec) \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}.

Reproducible scoring and protocol detail. VoxEval automates speech-in/speech-out scoring via Whisper-large-v3 with deterministic extraction and documented augmentation/math-to-speech procedures \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}. Contextual-biasing ASR should publish prompts, few-shot templates, loss balances, and scoring surfaces \cite{sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu}. Alignment/RL for speech--LMs should disclose preference-pair counts, segmentation policies, steps, and compute \cite{lin2025alignslmtextlessspokenlanguagemodelsrein}. Distribute full evaluation stacks with licensing; Dynamic-SUPERB accepts vetted community contributions \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}.

Recommended checklist. Use IndicSUPERB, SALMon, and VoxEval as standard suites; report dataset scale and language coverage; include FBANK and SSL finetunes; evaluate ASR accuracy, acoustic consistency, speaker/language ID, KWS, robustness, and reasoning. Publish stratified, voice-/speaker-disjoint splits and construction scripts; standardize sample rates/metadata; disclose real/synthetic ratios and scope limits; quantify/mitigate test overlap with decontaminated results and standardized tokenizer assessments (STAB) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. For streaming/rate-aware models, report token frame rate (25/12.5/6.25 Hz), bitrate, synthesis backend and steps, latency metrics alongside quality, and read--write schedules \cite{zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}. When assets cannot be released, provide risk rationales, watermarking, and evaluator calibration. These practices support fair, reproducible comparisons as SLMs adopt LM-aware/aligned tokenizers (LAST, LM-SPT), phonetic--acoustic designs (PAST), and higher-fidelity codecs (RepCodec) \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}.

















\section{Evaluation Metrics and Methodology} \label{sec:Evaluation Metrics and Methodology}

\subsection{Metric taxonomy and selection by goal} \label{subsec:Metric taxonomy and selection by goal}
Metric choice should reflect task goals, interfaces, and deployment constraints: recognition/translation/retrieval prioritize semantic fidelity and intelligibility; generation/dialogue emphasize naturalness, identity/style, prosody, controllability, and latency; efficiency is cross-cutting. Interface-aware reports must disclose token/frame rate, bitrate, chunk cadence, and decoding policy, because decoder quality largely shapes perceived naturalness while quantization design and token rate govern intelligibility and LM fit \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.

Recognition and semantic fidelity
\begin{itemize}
  \item Benchmarks and representation effects. Use SUPERB/IndicSUPERB (ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, KWS) with standardized toolchains; language-specific SSL often beats generic baselines, large-scale SSL excels on speaker ID, and continuous features typically surpass discrete tokens for fine-grained meaning. LM-aligned tokenizers (LM-SPT) preserve semantics at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz, shorten sequences, and improve S2T/TTS efficiency; compute-efficient HuBERT lowers the barrier to SSL training \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,wang2024comparativestudydiscretespeechtokenssema,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
  \item Intelligibility proxies and diagnostics. Report WER/CER (with Sub/Del/Ins) using a fixed recognizer/decoding policy; Whisper-large-v3 WER on generated audio is common in duplex settings, but proxy bias rises at high WER where ASR-free retrieval (SpeechRAG) is preferable \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Pair with substitution-sensitive probes---minimal-pair accuracy, phone confusion, and STAB metrics---since discrete-token TTS can sound natural yet be less intelligible; phonetic--acoustic tokenizers (PAST) improve reconstruction/SLM outcomes while shifting phonetic fidelity \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.
\end{itemize}

Translation fidelity
\begin{itemize}
  \item Text and speech metrics. For S2TT, use SacreBLEU (with signature) and BERTScore; for S2ST, compute ASR-BLEU with a fixed, published ASR recipe, and co-report intelligibility (WER) plus style/identity to expose semantic--paralinguistic trade-offs \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Probe representations with ABX and cross-lingual SLU on IndicSUPERB to test phonetic/segmental encoding and robustness \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
\end{itemize}

Modeling fit for token LMs
\begin{itemize}
  \item LM-centric selection and utility. Favor LM-aware tokens (LAST, LM-SPT) that are compact, semantically faithful, and prosody-aware; robust training-free discretizations (dMel) support unified ASR/TTS; word-level prosody tokens capture content--prosody dependencies \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces}. Report LM perplexity/NLL/next-token accuracy on held-out speech-token corpora and contextualize with STAB and downstream outcomes (WER/CER, MOS) to avoid over-optimizing surrogates; analyze tokenizer vocabulary and LM size trade-offs as in LAST \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}.
\end{itemize}

Perceptual quality and distributional realism
\begin{itemize}
  \item Human and proxy measures. Use ITU-T P.800 MOS (mean$\pm$CI) with native raters; prefer MUSHRA for controlled comparisons across token rates/decoders; UTMOS/MOSNet/NISQA scale reliably \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.
  \item Signal- and corpus-level metrics. Report ViSQOL, PESQ/STOI, SDR, DTW-MCD, F0/energy errors, and DNSMOS; use FAD for distribution shift and diversity metrics (MAD Speech) plus embedding ABX to diagnose shifts/confounds across discrete vs continuous tokenization and codec DRI \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete}. SALMon ranks acoustic-factor consistency and text--audio alignment; augmentation-invariant discrete representations aid robustness \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025augmentationinvariantdiscreterepresentat}.
\end{itemize}

Controllability: identity, style, prosody, affect
\begin{itemize}
  \item Identity/style and prosody. Measure speaker/style cosine, verification EER, and co-report ASR-BLEU/WER to reveal content--style trade-offs \cite{wang2024whydospeechlanguagemodelsfailgeneratesem,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Assess F0 RMSE/Voicing/FFE, duration and rate statistics, filler/pause patterns; word-level prosody tokens enable emergent processing, continuous tokenizers (Cont-SPT) better preserve frequency cues, and PAST strengthens prosody under real-time constraints \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}.
  \item Affect and social cues. Report SER Accuracy/F1, dialect/age-style accuracy, and audio-aware LLM rubrics for empathetic dialogue; combine explicit emotion metadata and heterogeneous adapters to disentangle linguistic/paralinguistic factors \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,wang2025dualinformationspeechlanguagemodelsemoti,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}.
\end{itemize}

Tokenizer/codec diagnostics and efficiency
\begin{itemize}
  \item Interface budgets. Disclose tokens/s, bitrate, frame/chunk cadence, codebooks/channels, dedup/compression ratios, and decoder steps; interpret metrics at matched operating points---token-rate-aware MUSHRA shows 50 tokens/s can rival or exceed 100 tokens/s under identical settings \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.
  \item Codebook health and information retention. Track usage entropy, token perplexity, dead/rare codes, and collapse; correlate with downstream metrics (STAB) and stress-test with SALMon; provide rate-aware panels spanning resynthesis WER/dWER, ViSQOL/PESQ/STOI, MOS/MUSHRA/UTMOS, and speaker similarity to quantify reconstruction--generation gaps \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.
\end{itemize}

Ecosystem anchors include IndicSUPERB (Kathbath; 1,684 h, 12 languages, 6 SLU tasks), fastabx for ABX discriminability, and tokenizers---PAST (supervised phonetic--acoustic, streamable), LM-SPT (LM-aligned semantics at low rates), and dMel (training-free, robust)---that collectively improve reconstruction, semantic alignment, and SLM performance; hybrid discrete--continuous or variationally enriched semantic tokens can further raise naturalness and preference, while SpeechRAG enables ASR-free QA when WER is high \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,chen2025variationalframeworkimprovingnaturalness,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}.

\subsection{End-to-end protocols} \label{subsec:End-to-end protocols}
Protocols prioritize matched rates/bitrates, fixed recognizers/judges, and explicit decoding schedules for reproducible comparisons across speech generation, streaming, and duplex dialogue.

Core hygiene and datasets
\begin{itemize}
  \item Use open suites with fixed configs: IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU and compute-accessible SSL (HuBERT on $\le$8 GPUs) \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
\end{itemize}

Decoding policies, transcription, and judges
\begin{itemize}
  \item Fix defaults and sweep a compact grid (e.g., top-p $\in$ {1.0, 0.99, 0.95, 0.9}); with hierarchical RVQ, decode the first residual autoregressively and others non-autoregressively; report WER, NISQA, SIM-O, and human CMOS/SMOS under a fixed budget \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}. For unified speech--text decoders, keep identical sampling across modalities and disclose beam/temperature/top-p \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}.
  \item Transcribe generated speech with a fixed ASR (e.g., Whisper-large-v3), versioned and recipe-published; use dual LLM judges with released prompts/aggregation rules (e.g., GPT-4o settings) for content/paralinguistics \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}.
\end{itemize}

Dataset handling and scoring
\begin{itemize}
  \item Segment long audio (e.g., 30 s windows, 15 s overlap); use WER for space-delimited languages and CER for logographic/syllabic scripts with consistent toolchains \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}.
\end{itemize}

Instruction-driven ASR/AST and contextual biasing
\begin{itemize}
  \item Apply LLM second-pass rescoring (bias lists, few-shot prompts, entity-class prediction, dynamic prompting) for $\approx$9.6--20% relative WER gains; complement with SpeechRAG when WER is high; assess tokenizer robustness with STAB/NAST and multilingual performance with IndicSUPERB \cite{sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Use fixed prompt templates and identical normalization; respect Dynamic-SUPERB inference rules \cite{wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}.
\end{itemize}

TTS/VC and textless S2ST
\begin{itemize}
  \item Zero-shot TTS: 3 s prompt+transcript; evaluate WER (fixed ASR), NISQA/CMOS/SMOS, and speaker/style similarity; normalize timbre (e.g., FreeVC) and synthesize multiple samples per condition \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}. Unified SLMs with LM-aligned tokens (LM-SPT/LAST) via small adapters over frozen speech/text backbones support interleaved text--speech with a fixed vocoder and disclosed decoding policy \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}. For textless S2ST, report ASR-BLEU, speaker/style similarity, dWER/WER, and NISQA/CMOS; include bilingual human evaluations with calibration and per-item medians \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.
\end{itemize}

Prosody stress tests and perturbations
\begin{itemize}
  \item Use WORLD-based emphasis/range edits (e.g., $\alpha$$\approx$1.15, $\beta$$\approx$2.2) and compute Token Edit Rate with SSL+k-means tokenizers (k$\in${100,500,2000}) under segment/full-utterance constraints; co-report NISQA/WER/SIM-O at fixed decoding \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
\end{itemize}

Streaming, chunking, and duplex dialogue
\begin{itemize}
  \item Long-form streaming ST: SimulEval with chunk sizes (320/640 ms), mWERSegmenter, LAAL/StreamLAAL; sweep delay k and report latency--quality frontiers/schedules \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. Duplex agents: report interruption P/R/F1, WER on generated speech, first-response latency under declared interleave/chunk schedules \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia,hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl}. Speech continuation: prompt 5 s, generate 25--120 s with fixed beam and unit/neural vocoder; score with GPT-based semantics and WER \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}.
\end{itemize}

Diagnostics and disclosure
\begin{itemize}
  \item Include fastabx ABX and STAB profiling; disclose prompts, recognizer/judge versions, decoding settings, interleave/chunk sizes, and interface descriptors; run sampling ablations (WER/NISQA/SIM-O/CMOS vs top-p) and justify operating points \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Bitrate- and token-rate--aware evaluation} \label{subsec:Bitrate- and token-rate--aware evaluation}
Treat frame/token cadence r (Hz) and bitrate b (kbps/bps) as independent levers; hold decoders, sampling, judges, and hardware fixed while sweeping r and b.

Axes and controls
\begin{itemize}
  \item Factors: model/tokenizer family and size (FBANK vs wav2vec2/HuBERT/SLM), tokenizer choice (STAB), input granularity (snippet/utterance), language (e.g., 12 Indic), task (ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, KWS), and acoustic condition with curated ABX metadata \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo}.
  \item Token/frame rate r: sweep 75/50/25/17.7/12.5/8.33/6.25/5.0 Hz; syllabic pacing at 5.0--8.33 Hz ($\approx$60--91 bps) probes ultra-low-rate semantics; large interleaved pretraining suggests $\approx$12.5 Hz as a semantic knee \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.
  \item Bitrate b: at fixed r, vary RVQ channels/bitwidths or K; at 50 Hz, tiers (3/1.5/0.75 kbps) expose WER/WIL vs ViSQOL/STOI trade-offs \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}. Sequence compression via dMel/PAST, dedup/audio-BPE, or attention-based thinning should report reduction ratios and layer placements \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}.
\end{itemize}

Dependent variables and panels
\begin{itemize}
  \item Semantics: WER/CER, WIL, unit-resynthesis dWER, zero-resource probes (sWUGGY/sBLIMP/StoryCloze) under a fixed ASR \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
  \item Acoustics: ViSQOL, PESQ/STOI, DNSMOS/NISQA, MOS/MUSHRA, DTW-MCD, speaker/style cosine; ABX and SALMon for phonetic/extra-linguistic and acoustic-factor consistency \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.
  \item Prosody/diversity: duration/F0 preservation, NDB/JS over pitch/voicing/energy, response to controlled prosody perturbations, and word-level prosody tokens \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}.
  \item Efficiency/latency: LM perplexity/NLL at matched sequence lengths, prefill time, decoder RTF, time-to-first-audio, token-reduction vs accuracy; interleaved speech--text pretraining scales compute more efficiently than textless SLMs \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,ma2025efficientspeechlanguagemodelingenergydis,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
\end{itemize}

Fair ablations and stability
\begin{itemize}
  \item Align datasets/splits/preprocessing/languages so STAB and fastabx correlate with IndicSUPERB downstream performance; fix decoder/vocoder, sampling, and ASR across r/b sweeps \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Bitrate ablations: fix r (e.g., 50 Hz), vary b (3/1.5/0.75 kbps). Token-rate ablations: fix b where possible, vary r only; avoid mismatches (50 vs 75 Hz) at fixed b \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
  \item Plot semantic and perceptual metrics vs r/b at matched operating points; semantically informed codecs can preserve intelligibility better at the same rate \cite{ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom}. At fixed r, increase RVQ channels/bits to raise b and reconstruction quality; check for WER/CER plateaus \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}. Mitigate DRI to stabilize sequence lengths; dMel provides streamable, OOD-resilient tokens; quantify behavior via STAB \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
  \item Enforce fixed-length budgets (e.g., 30--50 tokens) for textless LMs to avoid confounds \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Compare learned codecs (RepCodec/Language-Codec) to frame-rate or mel-channel quantization (dMel); include SALMon tests and report dedup/aBPE or merging ratios \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Mixed semantic--acoustic streams at base 12.5 Hz with depth 12.5--112.5 tokens/s show diffusion decoding can keep DNSMOS stable as depth decreases and that 50 tokens/s can surpass 100 tokens/s in MUSHRA \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. At ultra-low rates (e.g., 6.25 Hz, $\approx$0.0875 kbps), report intelligibility/identity and reconstruction--generation gaps (e.g., TaDiCodec) \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.
\end{itemize}

Minimum reporting
\begin{itemize}
  \item Specify tasks/benchmarks (IndicSUPERB, VoxEval), languages, audio conditions, modalities, metrics, pretraining/fine-tuning, and interface descriptors (r, b, codebooks/channels, dedup/aBPE, encoder layers) \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}. Provide ablations: frame-rate grid (50/25/12.5/8.33/6.25/5.0 Hz), bitrate tiers at 50 Hz (3/1.5/0.75 kbps), and 12.5 Hz acoustic-depth sweeps with stability diagnostics (ultra-low-rate WER gap), length-control effects, and caveats about rate mismatches \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Latency metrics and methodology} \label{subsec:Latency metrics and methodology}
We adopt a structured view of latency evaluation that is summarized by the hierarchical map in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_9}. At the top level, the map organizes (i) core latency definitions and protocols---RTF, TTFT, streaming lag, and duplex IRQ---anchored to shared long-audio workloads; (ii) compute accounting and path-wise latency decomposition spanning encoder buffering, tokenization/adapters, LLM prefill/decoding, and token-to-waveform synthesis, including policy and token-rate effects; and (iii) reporting fields and normalization guidance, covering required fields and duplex sheets, acoustic standardization, dataset/split and tokenization-alignment controls, quality--latency curves, and full compute/time disclosure. Citations throughout indicate exemplar methods and benchmarks supporting each element. Against this backdrop, latency must reflect interface (discrete vs continuous; r/b), streaming policy (wait-k, read--write, chunking), and duplex behavior under fixed hardware and decoding; shared definitions and long-audio workloads enable apples-to-apples comparisons \cite{li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan}.

Definitions and measures (tier i in \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_9})
\begin{itemize}
  \item Real-time factor (RTF): wall-clock/output duration; report global and length-stratified values with FLOPs/memory and batch size. A standard recipe reports throughput (tokens/s), prefill/decoding latency, RTF, and max batch on 120/240 s audio under fixed beams/hardware \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.
  \item Time-to-first-token (TTFT): separate text and speech. Decoder-only ASR with prompt compression improves CPU RTF ($\approx$0.24--0.29) vs encoder--decoder ($\approx$0.53--0.58) \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Publish first audio-chunk time and steady-state throughput under interleaving \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.
  \item Streaming lag: use AL, LAAL, StreamLAAL for SimulST/StreamST; report BLEU/COMET vs lag at fixed chunk sizes/delay k \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Duplex interruption/overlap: pre-register a success window, compute success/latency/false-alarms, and train/report IRQ timing with a common delay criterion \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.
\end{itemize}

Compute accounting and path-wise latency (tier ii in \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_9})
\begin{itemize}
  \item Break down encoder/right-context buffering, tokenizer/adaptor, LLM prefill/decoding, and token-to-waveform decoding (AR/NAR, diffusion/flow); publish audio-only and LLM-inclusive first-packet variants with fixed device/precision and no batching \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. For unified LSLMs (e.g., StreamUni), report policy/segmentation handling and LAAL/StreamLAAL vs quality across wait-k/prefix and chunked read--write schedules \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Include token-rate effects (e.g., 12.5 vs 6.25 Hz) with identical decoders and segmentation overhead when using syllabic pacing \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}.
\end{itemize}

Reporting fields and duplex sheets (tier iii in \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_9})
\begin{itemize}
  \item Minimum fields: TTFT (text/speech), first audio-chunk time, length-stratified RTF, prefill vs decoding latency, tokens/s per modality, memory-bound batch, peak VRAM, and AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL with chunk size and k; compare CPU/GPU RTFs for decoder-only vs encoder--decoder ASR under matched params \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Duplex sheet: interruption window, actuation delay, success/false-alarms, latency distributions with standardized VAD/onset detection under matched SALMon-like acoustic conditions; include tokenizer-aware controls (NAST, STAB) \cite{messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
\end{itemize}

Interpretation and normalization (normalization guidance within tier iii in \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_9})
\begin{itemize}
  \item Normalize dataset/splits (IndicSUPERB), streaming window/lookahead, frame hop/sample rate, decoding settings, hardware/batch size, and tokenization (discrete vs continuous, vocab), as r and speech--text alignment (LAST, Cont-SPT, OTReg) directly affect latency/throughput \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}. Co-report quality--latency curves for streaming ST (BLEU/COMET/BLASER 2.0 vs AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL) and interleaved assistants (time-to-first-audio-token and sustained audio-token throughput vs task quality) \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. Provide compute/timing (hardware, training/finetune time, latency/throughput, FLOPs, memory-bound batch) and contrast distilled vs teacher front-ends when relevant \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}.
\end{itemize}
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_9}
\subsection{Human-in-the-loop and statistical rigor} \label{subsec:Human-in-the-loop and statistical rigor}
Human panels and audio-aware LLM (ALLM) judges must be calibrated, auditable, and paired with prespecified statistics and agreement analyses, stratifying language/speaker/task coverage following IndicSUPERB and validating intrinsic metrics (STAB) against downstream tasks.

Judge selection, calibration, and blinding
\begin{itemize}
  \item Fix judge configuration (model/version, prompts, temperature, trials, aggregation). BoSS uses GPT-4o with three trials and power-scaled averaging and recommends auditor diversity; calibrate ALLM to human judgments, report human--ALLM and human--human correlations, sensitivity to judge settings/data shifts, and use dual-judge setups with an open-source counter-judge \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Randomize and blind evaluators; use speaker-stratified multi-seed splits and balanced AB/BA orders \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
\end{itemize}

Panel design, instruments, and reliability
\begin{itemize}
  \item Target $\ge$60 native raters with balanced loads ($\approx$8 items/rater), 1--5 MOS scales across dimensions (naturalness, meaningfulness), and 95% CIs \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Prefer MUSHRA for controlled comparisons and MOS for holistic judgments; prefilter high-WER items for expressivity/style panels; time-stratify long-form ratings \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Report Krippendorff's $\alpha$/ICC(2,k) per dimension/language/task; include human--human ceilings and drift, and correlate LLM judges with standard metrics; use ABX to localize phonetic vs extra-linguistic cues \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}.
\end{itemize}

Statistics, confounds, and replication
\begin{itemize}
  \item Use two-sided Wilcoxon with Holm--Bonferroni and effect sizes (rank-biserial r/Cliff's delta) for nonparametric panels; when MOS is approximately normal (checked via Shapiro--Wilk/QQ-plots), use two-sided t-tests with 95% CIs and homoscedasticity checks (Fligner--Killeen/Levene); apply ANOVA with Tukey HSD for omnibus tests \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Quantify confounds (e.g., duration--agreement) and guard against dataset imbalance/contamination/compute limits with held-out tests \cite{tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Report mean$\pm$SE or bootstrap CIs, run multi-seed/batch replications, and retain per-trial logs \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}.
\end{itemize}

Reporting checklist and defaults
\begin{itemize}
  \item Specify datasets (e.g., Kathbath 1,684 h, 12 languages), tasks (ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, KWS), baselines (FBANK vs SSL), tokenizer diagnostics (STAB) and correlations to downstream, and include SALMon and VoxEval where applicable \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}. Provide judge metadata (model/version, prompts, temperature, trials/aggregation), human--ALLM calibration, per-sample ratings/transcripts/temperatures, code/seeds, and bias disclosures \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Practical defaults: evaluate with IndicSUPERB, select tokenizers via STAB, and prefer adapter-based SLMs that freeze speech/text backbones ($\approx$1% trainable params) for strong ASR/AST and zero-shot instruction following \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
\end{itemize}

















\section{Efficiency Compression and Deployment} \label{sec:Efficiency Compression and Deployment}

\subsection{Parameter and training efficiency} \label{subsec:Parameter and training efficiency}
Freezing strong speech/text backbones and training thin bridges or PEFT modules delivers the best cost--quality frontier. A $\approx$156M, 2-layer adapter aligning a 2B USM encoder to a 13B mT0-MT maintains text skills and stable next-token modeling while avoiding full-finetuning drift \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}; prompt-tuning adapts textless GSLMs for classification with <0.1% parameters \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. LoRA with small ranks/scopes stabilizes single-stage joint SFT across Whisper-large-v3, Qwen2-Audio, and compact LLMs, especially under iterative Focus--Feedback--Fix schedules \cite{peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem,meng2025iltiterativeloratrainingthroughfocusfeed,sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere}; targeting K/Q/V/O only within speech spans preserves text behavior while adding affective competence \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}. Memory-aware PEFT with 4-bit quantization scales 7B--12B decoders without accuracy loss \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}, and progressive unfreezing after projector-only alignment further reduces early optimization burden in multilingual bridges \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}.

LM-aware and phonetic--acoustic tokenizers (LAST, LM-SPT, PAST) plus training-free dMel compress the interface into LM-aligned or phoneme-faithful units at low frame rates, enabling a single text LM to process speech and text for zero-shot instruction following, ASR-free SpeechRAG, and multilingual transfer validated on IndicSUPERB; fastabx offers efficient ABX diagnostics guiding unit design \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. Compact cores can replace cascades: MSLM-S2ST uses 306M AR + 306M NAR LMs to jointly translate and generate units \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; a $\approx$24M dialogue LM sustains long-form textless generation \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}. Efficient front ends and inference-time token-merging reduce memory by $\approx$7$\times$ and raise throughput $\approx$1.8$\times$ without hurting ER/SQA; long-speech compression and optimal-transport regularization strengthen long-form efficiency and cross-dataset generalization \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,guo2025fastlongspeechenhancinglargespeechlangua,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}.

Compute-efficient recipes widen access: Slam trains a competitive SLM in ~24 hours on a single academic GPU with careful initialization, synthetic data, and preference optimization \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}; optimized HuBERT reproduces Large-model results with $\approx$8 GPUs, sometimes skipping the first SSL iteration via ASR bootstrapping \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}. In multi-stage curricula (ASR$\rightarrow$TTS$\rightarrow$SQA), experience replay, LoRA-scale discounting, and model merging mitigate forgetting \cite{hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi}. Interleaved speech--text pretraining scales more efficiently than textless SLMs and favors spending on model size over tokens \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang}. IndicSUPERB and SALMon standardize multilingual SLU and acoustic-robustness evaluation \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.

Actionable configurations:
- Frozen USM-2B + mT0-MT-13B with $\approx$156M adapter \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}.
- Partial-LoRA for affect (rank 16, $\alpha$=16 on K/Q/V/O; 4$\times$A100 for $\approx$2.6 days) \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}.
- LoRA + 4-bit quantization for 12B decoders ($\alpha$=32) \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}.
- Staged unfreezing: projector-only, then rank-16, $\alpha$=32 LoRA on Whisper encoder + 9B LLM \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}.
- Compact S2ST: 306M AR + 306M NAR \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.
- Small dialogue SLM: 23.64M parameters \cite{nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.
- Parameter-light SSL front ends ($\approx$95--100M) with token-merging inference \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang}.

\subsection{Model and sequence efficiency} \label{subsec:Model and sequence efficiency}
Three levers compose: rate-aware token interfaces, compressed conditioning paths, and schedule-aware decoding/rendering. Moderate cadences shrink sequences while preserving semantics and control---12.5 Hz supports long-context mixed speech--text pretraining without waveform synthesis \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}; 6.25 Hz with few-step text-aware flow/diffusion maintains intelligibility/identity at much lower sequence cost \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Hybrid semantic--acoustic tokenizers keep cadence fixed but vary per-frame acoustic depth; 50 tps ($N_{s}=1, N_{a}=3$) matches or exceeds 100 tps on WER, DNSMOS, and MUSHRA \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Duplex stacks remain compact by fusing a small streaming encoder with middle fusion into a modest decoder \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Conditioning compression trims prefill and attention: 4$\times$ frame subsampling before a thin adaptor and projector pooling (4:1--5:1) reduce injected states with minimal loss and, with FlashAttention-2/ZeRO-2, fit modest GPUs \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. Decoder-only ASR benefits from label-synchronous compression via CTC blank removal, shrinking inputs to $\approx$14.6% of frames and roughly halving inference cost \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. For streaming, semantic truncation discards fully translated history to bound context \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

On discrete streams, run-length de-dup and acoustic BPE shorten strings without changing interfaces; compressibility diagnostics (Huffman/BPE/de-dup) often favor moderate vocabularies at similar accuracy, informing rate-aware K selection \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. Knowledge-distilled tokens shift the rate--quality frontier: supervising early RVQ levels with text/SSL teachers increases informativeness without extra runtime, and AR on RVQ-1 with NAR on residual levels improves intelligibility and shortens acoustic horizons for TTS/S2ST \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.

Parallelization spends AR capacity on semantics and amortizes high-rate acoustics through NAR refinement or masked infilling; projector pooling and CTC blank removal shrink prefill, while delay-controlled emission and semantic truncation bound latency \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Rendering favors codec-native inversion or lightweight vocoders; few-step text-aware diffusion at ultra-low rates achieves favorable real-time factors \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Tooling such as fastabx and FastAdaSP enables large-scale multi-task experiments with up to 7$\times$ memory reduction and $\approx$1.8$\times$ throughput gains at negligible accuracy cost \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}. Across 12.5/6.25 Hz interleaving \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, conditioning compression \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}, distillation with AR/NAR splits \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio,peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}, acoustic-BPE shortening \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}, and lightweight rendering \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}, prefill and per-step latency drop while intelligibility and style control remain strong.

Practical defaults:
- Token rate: 12.5 Hz for mixed training; hybrid tokens with $N_{s}=1, N_{a}=3$ ($\approx$50 tps). Drop to 6.25 Hz only with text-aware decoders \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.
- Conditioning compression: 4$\times$ subsampling or 4--5$\times$ projector pooling with ZeRO-2/FlashAttention-2; CTC blank removal for decoder-only ASR \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.
- Parallelize long sequences with token reduction (FastAdaSP) and efficient ABX (fastabx); allocate AR to semantics and NAR to residuals; apply semantic truncation in streaming \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.
- Distill tokens for informativeness and keep renderers light \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

\subsection{Fast decoding and streaming} \label{subsec:Fast decoding and streaming}
Latency falls by emitting more tokens per step, cutting per-token compute, and shortening horizons. Grouped multi-token prediction (MTP) amortizes attention and pairs with RVQ residual-level parallelism (AR base, NAR residuals); masked infilling adds parallelism in few iterations \cite{li2024masksrmaskedlanguagemodelfullbandspeechr}, and k-frame decoding (k$\le$4) boosts throughput without quality loss \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}. Mixed stacks profit from parallel speech--text emission and NAR vocoders \cite{tan2025drvoiceparallelspeechtextvoiceconversati}; read--write interleaving with short planning bursts yields low first-audio delay \cite{fang2025llamaomni2llmbasedrealtimespokenchatbota}. Dual-stream TTS initiates speech by the second input token and emits all speech tokens per text token in one step \cite{sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}.

Speculative bursts ($\approx$10 tokens) reduce time-to-first-chunk \cite{long2025vitaaudiofastinterleavedcrossmodaltokeng}; block-wise 2 s decoding matches streamable tokenizers and duplex schedules \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Cache-aware inference thins sequences and stabilizes incremental decoding---FastAdaSP merges adjacent input slices before KV construction for 1.44--1.84$\times$ throughput and $\approx$50% decoding-latency reductions on 240 s inputs \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}; prefix-to-prefix decoding with per-chunk KV updates and incremental beam search in a CTC lattice holds latency steady \cite{deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc}. Distilled encoders reduce front-end FLOPs \cite{gao2024gendistillerdistillingpretrainedlanguage}.

Lower token rates shrink decoder steps and KV growth: syllable-synchronous tokens at 5--8.33 Hz accelerate boundary extraction \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}; 6.25 Hz tokens with few-step text-aware diffusion reach end-to-end RTF $\approx$0.12--0.29 \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Acoustic BPE accelerates AR continuation by $\approx$2.8--5.0$\times$ on 20 s segments \cite{shen2024acousticbpespeechgenerationdiscretetoken}. Streamable tokenizers emit semantic tokens online and expand chunk-wise into acoustic tokens and waveform segments via an acoustic LM and codec/diffusion decoder \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. Renderer choice is decisive: single-pass GAN/vocoders minimize last-mile latency when feasible \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}, while step-distilled diffusion compresses sampling steps with minor quality trade-offs \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

A practical low-latency stack composes grouped MTP or k-frame decoding, residual-level NAR prediction, speculative bursts, FastAdaSP sequence thinning, per-chunk KV updates with fixed beams, a streamable tokenizer with chunk-wise expansion, low-rate tokens ($\approx$6.25--12.5 Hz) with few-step text-aware diffusion or a NAR vocoder, and block-wise 2 s decoding---yielding 1.44--1.84$\times$ throughput gains and $\approx$50% decoding-latency reductions from sequence thinning alone, with favorable real-time factors at low token rates \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

\subsection{Bandwidth and edge--server trade-offs} \label{subsec:Bandwidth and edge--server trade-offs}
Deployments must balance link bitrate, VRAM/KV growth, and server FLOPs while supporting duplex, low-latency interaction.
