Low-rate token streams are viable: semantic-conditioned DiffSoundStream matches SoundStream quality at $\approx$50 tps with four diffusion steps \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}; textless streaming S2ST achieves strong BLEU/latency/BLASER-2.0 by emitting discrete tokens end-to-end \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. XY-Tokenizer improves the semantic--acoustic trade-off with high speaker similarity at comparable rates \cite{gong2025xytokenizermitigatingsemanticacousticcon}, and SALM-Duplex caps downlink near 0.6 kbps with high UTMOS and speaker similarity, establishing a sub-kbps working point; $\approx$0.3 kbps modes remain intelligible for poor connectivity \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,xue2025hhcodechighcompressionfidelitydiscretene}. A practical ladder is 0.3$\rightarrow$0.6$\rightarrow$1.24 kbps, with language-dependent adjustments for prosodic density \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}.

Separating perception from generation decouples encoder compute from codec bitrate and eases heterogeneous client integration; semantic-conditioned tokenization shifts information into the semantic path to lighten the acoustic stream when both are transmitted \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. On constrained VRAM, Slam proves single-GPU training in ~24 h \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}; FastAdaSP enables up to 7$\times$ memory reduction and $\approx$1.8$\times$ throughput gains for higher concurrency \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}. Mixed speech--text decoders fit within $\approx$19 GB VRAM on 8B backbones for on-prem single-GPU serving; quantization (4--8 bit) and expert routing by language/task further reduce VRAM/FLOPs \cite{dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}. Interleaved speech--text pretraining improves scaling efficiency and shifts optimal spend toward larger models; speech-native RAG bypasses ASR cascades, reducing latency and compounding errors \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}.

Operational policies:
- Mid-rate default $\approx$50 tps ($\approx$0.55--0.6 kbps) with capacity for textless streaming S2ST; burst to $\approx$1.24 kbps for prosody-critical spans; fall back to 0.3 kbps under congestion \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,xue2025hhcodechighcompressionfidelitydiscretene}.
- Memory/concurrency: track per-session KV/VRAM and max batch; quantify 10--50% token-reduction/merging gains; report single-GPU training and VoxEval robustness \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}.
- Reporting: justify edge vs server execution using IndicSUPERB, STAB diagnostics, SALMon robustness, and interleaved scaling dynamics \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang}.

Blueprints:
- Ultra-low-bandwidth field agent: on-device ESPnet-SpeechLM with dMel tokens for SLU; server-side mixed SLM with FastAdaSP concurrency; $\approx$0.6 kbps downlink \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.
- Mobile default: 50 tps semantic-conditioned acoustic tokens with short expressive bursts; low memory via token-merging; compatible with discrete streaming \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}.
- On-prem single-GPU: 24-hour Slam training, IndicSUPERB evaluation, int8/4-bit quantization, duplex $\approx$0.6 kbps \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice}.

\subsection{Implementation recipes and benchmarks} \label{subsec:Implementation recipes and benchmarks}
As a visual synopsis, \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_10} presents a hierarchical map of the implementation recipes and benchmarks surveyed in this subsection. The top-level branches group best practices into tokenization/alignment, practical modeling with compute-efficient recipes, and evaluation with open benchmarks; the leaf nodes condense these into concise, cited action items---such as efficient k-means, Optimal Transport Regularization (OTReg), single-GPU Slam, SSR-Connector, and the STAB and VoxEval suites---guiding reproducible, rate- and latency-aware development and fair reporting.

Rate- and latency-aware practice centers on open toolchains, standardized token interfaces, fixed decoding policies, and matched compute budgets. Resources: IndicSUPERB/Kathbath (1,684 h; 12 languages; ASR, SV/SI, LID, QbE, KWS), SALMon (noise, emotion, identity, room), STAB tokenizer diagnostics, VoxEval SpeechQA \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}. Compute-efficient baselines include 24 h single-GPU Slam, $\approx$8-GPU HuBERT SSL pipelines, and SpeechGen-style adapters on GTX-2080-class GPUs \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl}; LLM-based contextual biasing improves second-pass ASR by $\approx$9.6--20% relative WER \cite{sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu}.

We elaborate each branch of \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_10} below.

Tokenization and alignment:
- Efficient unitization via k-means (moderately coarse segmentation; larger codebooks) cuts training data $\approx$50% and runtime $\approx$70%; dMel and LAST let one LM handle speech/text \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz}.
- In-memory labeling and cross-node k-means avoid TB-scale storage, keeping clustering memory sub-GB; ESPnet configs document budgets \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
- Optimal Transport Regularization narrows the speech--text gap and improves multilingual generalization with a lightweight, label-free term; a two-stage schedule (2+3 epochs; AdamW 1e$-$4$\rightarrow$1e$-$6; $\lambda$_spr=0.1) is reproducible \cite{xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}.

Unified modeling:
- SUTLM mixes speech-unit LM, paired speech--text (cross/auto-regressive), and text-only LM over a shared vocabulary; at inference, zero non-target modality logits to prevent leakage \cite{entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste,cuervo2025textspeechlanguagemodelsimprovedcrossmod,wang2024c3llmconditionalmultimodalcontentgenerat}.
- Language-Codec trains a language-aware tokenizer/codec with LM-conditioned and masked-codec objectives for robust, streamable tokens; open runs use 2M steps on 8$\times$A100-40GB (AdamW 2e$-$4) \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat,kim2024enclapcombiningneuralaudiocodectextjoint,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}.
- Omni-modal alignment (EMOVA) and SSR-Connector add controllable style/emotion and compress speech to text granularity to avoid forgetting \cite{chen2025emovaempoweringlanguagemodelsseehearspea,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech}.

Representative blueprints:
- SpeechComposer unifies ASR, TTS, speech/text LM, and composite tasks in a decoder-only model initialized from OPT-125M/350M with HuBERT-Base k-means tokens and HiFi-GAN; released configs/checkpoints aid reproduction \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.
- Low-rate semantic pacing ($\approx$5 Hz) via SyllableLM matches or outperforms SotA with $\approx$30$\times$ less training compute and $\approx$4$\times$ faster inference; report RTF on fixed 32$\times$25 s batches with 16 CPU threads \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}.
- Single-stage joint SFT with LoRA mixing ASR/AST/SQA and conversational data, with continual text-only checks to audit forgetting, is tractable on 4$\times$A6000 over $\approx$5 days \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi}.

Evaluation and reporting:
- Prefer intrinsic first, downstream second: run STAB's Apache Beam suite in $\le$15 CPU-minutes per metric per tokenizer before costly downstream sweeps (e.g., DASB) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark}.
- Standardize decoding (beam, temperature/top-p, averaging windows, padding/splicing) and fix token frame/bit rates and AR/NAR schedules for fair timing/quality across tokenizers and languages \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Report RTF, TTFT, tokens/s, WER/BLEU/MOS proxies, VRAM class, concurrency, training wall-clock/GPU-hours, optimizer/schedule, precision/parallelism, and seeds \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang}.
- Publish tokenizer pipelines and memory footprints (on-the-fly labeling, Kaldiio sampling, k-means node counts) and attach code/models/eval harnesses for RepCodec, SALMon, VoxEval, and DASB \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark}.

These practices---paired with interleaved speech--text scaling that prioritizes model size, LM-aware tokenization (LAST/LM-SPT/PAST/dMel), and bandwidth-aware streaming policies---enable reproducible, compute-light development of duplex, barge-in-capable agents under tight latency and memory budgets \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang}. Read together with the high-level structure in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_10}, the section provides both a compact roadmap and the concrete, cited steps needed for faithful reproduction and fair comparison.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_10}





\section{Safety Robustness and Ethics} \label{sec:Safety Robustness and Ethics}

\subsection{Threat models and vulnerabilities} \label{subsec:Threat models and vulnerabilities}
SLMs introduce attack surfaces at the audio encoder, modality connector, and unified decoder absent in text-only LLMs. White-box, gradient-based waveform perturbations reliably bypass safety with $\approx$79--97% success and reach 100% in some categories while preserving intelligibility (low WER); identical prompts presented as clean text or clean speech routed to the same text LLM family yield 0% success, confirming an audio-pathway exploit \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains}. Black-box transfer achieves nontrivial cross-model rates ($\approx$6--10% on public SLMs and $\approx$19.5% across some families), whereas matched-SNR Gaussian or non-adversarial corruptions remain far less effective ($\le$8--22%) \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes}. Prompt templates that prepend affirmative unsafe continuations further raise reliability; post-hoc activation patching can restore $\approx$99% robustness without retraining \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains}. Beyond adversarial examples, SLMs inherit base-LLM hallucinations, exhibit accent/bias failures, and suffer omissions/repetitions from discrete-codec instability \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,algayres2023generativespokenlanguagemodelbasedcontin,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete}. A credible threat model therefore includes: (i) $\approx$90% white-box audio perturbations, (ii) $\approx$10% transferable black-box attacks across toxic categories, and (iii) fragility under codec inconsistencies and noisy acoustics, motivating defenses that decouple transcription quality from safety under attack \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete}.

\subsection{Defense-in-depth} \label{subsec:Defense-in-depth}
To situate the following safeguards within a unified lifecycle view, \autoref{fig:tiny\_tree\_figure\_11} depicts a conceptual defense-in-depth tree that (i) groups pre-deployment measures---data curation and training-time alignment---to improve coverage, robustness, and safety; (ii) consolidates runtime defenses and provenance tools that harden inputs/outputs and enable post-hoc mitigation and verification; and (iii) summarizes a reference stack with recommended components and reporting standards. Arrows in the figure trace the progression from preparation to inference-time protection and auditing, emphasizing layered, complementary controls across the model lifecycle. We instantiate these branches as follows.

A layered stack spans data, training, decoding, and post-hoc defenses with provenance throughout:
\begin{itemize}
\item Data-time safeguards. Curate multilingual, accent-diverse corpora; audit tokenizers with STAB; use IndicSUPERB's stratified coverage to identify language-specific gaps \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Prefer LM-aligned, robust units (LM-SPT, PAST, NAST, LAST) that support low rates and streamability \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}; use ASR-free SpeechRAG in high-WER domains \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}. Watermark synthetic audio (AudioSeal) and apply misuse-aware filtering (KE-Omni); add negative exemplars that map non-speech/inaudible inputs to safe refusals \cite{zhao2024advancingspeechlanguagemodelsscalingsupe,dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice}.
\item Training-time safeguards. Combine safety filtering and refusal shaping with compute-efficient SSL (e.g., HuBERT on $\approx$8 GPUs) and standardized tokenizer assessment (STAB); mitigate forgetting via replay, model merging, and LoRA scaling; enable watermark-by-default to ease provenance \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi,wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot,zhu2024generativepretrainedspeechlanguagemodele}.
\item Decoding-time safeguards. Harden inputs and constrain outputs: time-domain noise flooding at $\approx$48--60 dB SNR drops jailbreak success from $\approx$90\% to $\approx$0--8\% \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes}; apply deny-lists and dialect-aware refusals \cite{dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice}; insert watermarks at generation (SSR-Speech) for frame-level audits \cite{wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot}.
\item Post-hoc defenses and provenance. Activation patching (SPIRIT) detects noise-sensitive neurons and blocks audio jailbreaks with $\approx$98.5--99.9\% success and negligible utility loss, outperforming denoising \cite{djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains}. Use SSR-Speech for edited-region localization and AudioSeal for corpus-scale verification; disclose residual risks and restrict weight release when misuse remains material \cite{wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot,zhao2024advancingspeechlanguagemodelsscalingsupe,park2025longformspeechgenerationspokenlanguagemo}.
\end{itemize}

Completing the lifecycle emphasized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_11}, the reference stack (rightmost branch in the figure) combines robust discrete units (NAST/PAST/LM-SPT; DASB indicates discrete tokens often beat codecs but can trail continuous features at some rates) \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage}, dual watermarking (AudioSeal, SSR-Speech) \cite{zhao2024advancingspeechlanguagemodelsscalingsupe,wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot}, input noise flooding plus activation patching \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains}, and standardized reporting of jailbreak/defense success with GPT-Score and WER trade-offs \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,djanibekov2025spiritpatchingspeechlanguagemodelsagains,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. For noisy or ASR-free pipelines, hybrid representations and retrieval conditioning (e.g., Whisper-GPT, SpeechRAG) stabilize behavior \cite{verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}. In concert with the pre-deployment and runtime controls outlined above and visualized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_11}, this stack operationalizes defense-in-depth from preparation through inference-time auditing.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_11}
\subsection{Provenance, impersonation, and privacy} \label{subsec:Provenance, impersonation, and privacy}
Rising fidelity in synthesis/conversion heightens deepfake risks as models learn prosody and paralinguistics and remain robust under acoustic shifts \cite{chen2025variationalframeworkimprovingnaturalness,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025augmentationinvariantdiscreterepresentat}. Make provenance first-class: frame-synchronous SSR-Speech watermarks enable edited-region localization at tens-of-Hz token rates, while AudioSeal watermarks preserve corpus-scale traceability; pair watermark-on-by-default synthesis with cryptographic request signing and immutable logs linking prompts, enrollment assets, model/version, and watermark keys \cite{wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot,zhao2024advancingspeechlanguagemodelsscalingsupe,gong2024seamlessexpressivelmspeechlanguagemodele}.

Voice conversion/cloning requires explicit consent and anti-spoofing. Tokenizers such as UniCodec permit conversion by swapping a global token, creating a direct impersonation channel \cite{jiang2025universalspeechtokenlearninglowbitratene}. Enforce liveness-verified enrollment, per-request consent tokens bound to target speakers, identity allow/deny-lists, and mandatory watermarks; integrate anti-spoof detectors at enrollment and inference and quarantine suspicious traffic \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}. When identity is unnecessary, use privacy-preserving virtual voices and restrict model release for high-risk systems; surface impersonation risks, consent status, and takedown hooks in UI/docs \cite{zhao2024advancingspeechlanguagemodelsscalingsupe,park2025longformspeechgenerationspokenlanguagemo,gong2024seamlessexpressivelmspeechlanguagemodele}.

Evaluate watermark detection at frame/segment granularity, anti-spoof robustness (replay/synthesis/cross-channel), consent ablations, and style-transfer errors across demographics \cite{wang2025ssrspeechtowardsstablesaferobustzeroshot,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,gong2024seamlessexpressivelmspeechlanguagemodele}. A deployment blueprint couples LM-aware tokenization (LAST), multilingual SLU validation (IndicSUPERB), and acoustic robustness (SALMon) \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}, while acknowledging adversarial audio jailbreak rates near $\approx$90% white-box and $\approx$10% transfer and guarding against flawed test assumptions \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}.

\subsection{Robust and invariant representations} \label{subsec:Robust and invariant representations}
Representations should encode linguistic content while remaining invariant to speaker, channel/room, device, moderate prosody changes, non-speech acoustics, and adversarial perturbations. SALMon documents consistent degradations from noise, identity, emotion, and room effects; discrete codecs exhibit instability that induces omissions/repetitions, mitigated by augmentation-invariant tokens and continuous targets \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio}.

Diagnostics. Decoder-free Token Edit Rate (TER/MTER) on discrete sequences, with feature smoothing and k-means corpus selection, quantify speaker/perturbation invariance; data2vec-audio-base units perform strongly \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. STAB adds chrF robustness under pitch/speed perturbations and additive noise (10 dB SNR) and shows ASR auxiliary losses (USM-v2) improve speaker invariance; metadata-driven ABX across microphones/rooms exposes extra-linguistic leakage \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.

Mixture robustness and selective invariance. SHuBERT pre-trains HuBERT with target-speaker conditioning and dual-path decorrelation, aligning clean/mixture views, suppressing interference while preserving clean accuracy, transferring to low-resource settings, and strengthening downstream discrete unitizers and continuous prompts \cite{entry2025selectivehubertselfsupervisedpretraining,mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,entry2025augmentationinvariantdiscreterepresentat}.

Tokenization and codec targets. NAST produces local units plus a global residual stable under time-stretch, pitch-shift, reverberation, and noise, disentangling style \cite{messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage}. USM-based tokenizers validated with STAB---and boosted by ASR losses---show content-oriented supervision increases invariance \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. dMel tokens align to perceptual time--frequency structure, improving robustness to music/background speech at comparable bitrates over HuBERT-k-means and SpeechTokenizer; self-segmented syllable-synchronous embeddings provide rate-controlled, cross-lingual stability \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.

Training levers and evaluation. Combine content-grounded supervision at the tokenizer (ASR-style losses), enrollment conditioning and decorrelation (SHuBERT-like), and prosody-aware smoothing/data selection, with augmentation for room/channel diversity \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. Report TER/MTER under spectral-envelope warping and cross-speaker same-text pairs, STAB's chrF robustness and invariance probes, mixture robustness via cross-correlation, codec/token robustness under music/background speech, and matched-rate intelligibility/prosody slices \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.

\subsection{Fairness and evaluator bias} \label{subsec:Fairness and evaluator bias}
Fairness requires disentangling true demographic/language disparities from evaluation artifacts such as contamination, tokenizer mismatch, and judge bias, and adopting multilingual, stratified, end-to-end evaluations. Large gaps persist across accents, L1/L2 status, and conditions; zero-shot WER can differ by an order of magnitude, and ICL narrows but does not close gaps and can harm some varieties \cite{roll2025contextlearningboostsspeechrecognitionhu}. IndicSUPERB's gender- and region-aware design supports auditable coverage; Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2 and simultaneous ST pipelines show propagation of upstream demographic/domain biases and teacher errors \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand,ouyang2025cmusiwslt2025simultaneousspeechtranslati}.

Evaluator bias. Audio-aware LLM judges (ALLMs) scale evaluation but exhibit self-bias when grading same-family systems and show limited resolution among weaker models; cross-family judging and calibration reduce errors \cite{chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe}. TELEVAL mitigates bias via string matching for factoid QA, separate text/audio scoring, and objective audio metrics, with triad GPT-4o judging for open-ended tasks \cite{li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan}. Align-SLM and BoSS warn that single-ASR reliance and surrogate judges skew outcomes; dataset-centric coverage (e.g., Chinese focus) and binary gender assumptions demand explicit caveats \cite{lin2025alignslmtextlessspokenlanguagemodelsrein,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity}.

A fairness-aware playbook combines standardized multilingual benchmarks (IndicSUPERB), tokenizer audits (STAB), end-to-end speech QA under varied acoustics (VoxEval), and acoustic-robustness probes (SALMon), within reproducible and compute-accessible SSL recipes \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}. Recommended practices:
\begin{itemize}
\item Stratified reporting with confidence intervals and effect sizes across accent/region, language/dialect, L1/L2, inclusive gender labels, and acoustic conditions; add STAB/ABX diagnostics to attribute disparities \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,sicherman2023analysingdiscreteselfsupervisedspeechrep}.
\item Multi-judge, multi-tool scoring: cross-family ALLM ensembles, human calibration with disclosed correlations, objective metrics where possible, and sensitivity to the chosen ASR \cite{li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan,lin2025alignslmtextlessspokenlanguagemodelsrein}.
\item Provenance for synthetic data and domain matching: enumerate sources/teachers, de-duplicate public corpora, release configs, and quantify domain shift via ablations \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}.
\item Group-aware adaptation with guardrails: report per-group deltas and flag negative or negligible gains from ICL/adaptation \cite{roll2025contextlearningboostsspeechrecognitionhu}.
\end{itemize}
Contamination audits, tokenizer benchmarking with STAB, and LM-aware tokenization controlling vocabulary/rate (LAST, LM-SPT) reduce evaluator bias and yield more defensible fairness conclusions \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset}.

\subsection{Reporting standards and red teaming} \label{subsec:Reporting standards and red teaming}
Safety claims should be auditable and comparable. An evaluation card specifies robustness assumptions (acoustic confounds, adversarial budgets), metrics (Safety/Relevance/Helpfulness, JSR, SPR/SNR), benchmarks (IndicSUPERB, STAB, SALMon), judges (human and calibrated ALLMs), and governance (data provenance, contributor coverage, code/data release) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Threat models must state access and budgets (white-/black-box, targeted/untargeted, SPR/SNR constraints, device/channel assumptions) and report per-category breakdowns with confidence intervals and pinned aggregation policies \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes}. Release spoken red-team sets with speaker/accent/language/device metadata and licensing; pin judge models/prompts, calibrate to human labels (F1$\ge$80%), and disclose procedures and limitations; document IRB approvals, consent, demographics/compensation, and operational safety observations for human-in-the-loop work \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,lin2025alignslmtextlessspokenlanguagemodelsrein,fu2025personalizedsociallyassistiverobotsendsp}.

A minimal red-teaming protocol uses ESPnet-SpeechLM for reproducible preprocessing, pretraining, inference, and evaluation; exercises tokenizer choices (Cont-SPT, LAST) and multilingual SLU coverage (ASR, LID, KWS, QbE, SID/SV) via IndicSUPERB; and probes reasoning under latency with chunked think-while-talking \cite{tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}. Construct bilingual/multilingual seed sets (harmful/benign, varied delivery), generate multi-budget adversarial variants with per-sample SPR/SNR, and evaluate Safety/Relevance/Helpfulness/JSR using calibrated ALLMs correlated with standardized speech metrics (IndicSUPERB, STAB, SALMon), with pre-registered thresholds and human adjudication of low-confidence cases; include cross-accent/cross-lingual stress and expand coverage iteratively with IRB approval \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,fu2025personalizedsociallyassistiverobotsendsp}.

Transparency and stewardship require open-sourcing datasets, models, configs, and code; reporting compute budgets and seeds; releasing harmful/benign sets, judge configs, scoring scripts, and per-sample annotations; and standardizing metric names/calculations with per-category/accent/language breakdowns \cite{peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes}. Maintain multilingual, diverse benchmarks (IndicSUPERB), tokenizer assessments (STAB), spoken QA (VoxEval), and prosody-sensitive unit probes, and run iterative red-team rounds that recalibrate judges, expand datasets, stress-test named-entity handling, and publish full compute/language coverage disclosures \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.

















\section{Open Toolkits Frameworks and Reproducibility} \label{sec:Open Toolkits Frameworks and Reproducibility}

\subsection{5C overview: Code, Config, Compute, Corpora, Comparison} \label{subsec:5C overview: Code, Config, Compute, Corpora, Comparison}
The 5C lens---Code, Config, Compute, Corpora, Comparison---specifies the open artifacts, rate-/latency-aware settings, and disclosure norms required for reproducible and cross-lab comparable speech--AI. As visualized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_12}, the overview is organized into three complementary pillars: (i) open stack exemplars (tokenizers/codecs and corpora), (ii) standardized evaluation anchors (low-cost, rate-/robustness-aware diagnostics), and (iii) benchmark-card disclosures (coverage, conditions, comparability, and compute/cost reporting). Together, these pillars link open, inspectable pipelines to comparable cross-lab evaluation via explicit, auditable reporting. It emphasizes end-to-end, inspectable stacks spanning tokenizers/codecs and LM bridges, interleaved SLMs, decoders/vocoders, and a standardized evaluation harness with dataset hygiene and statistical testing, exemplified by streamable dMel discretization with parallel encode/decode, RepCodec's high-fidelity semantic tokens, and LLaSO's public Align/Instruct/Eval sets with a 3.8B reference model \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere}. 

For low-cost, comparable diagnostics (pillar ii), the framework anchors to STAB for tokenizer assessment, SALMon for acoustic-factor sensitivity, IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU, Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2 for community-expanded audio tasks, and VoxEval for speech-in/speech-out QA and spoken reasoning \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}. Finally, in keeping with pillar (iii), a 5C benchmark card accompanies results with Coverage, Conditions, Capabilities, Comparability, and Compute/Cost fields, mandating explicit token/frame rates, bitrates, schedules, hardware/precision, and auditable scorer scripts \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_12}
\subsection{Code: Open ecosystems and stacks} \label{subsec:Code: Open ecosystems and stacks}
Open ecosystems now release code, configs, and checkpoints with pinned evaluators and interfaces. LM-aligned tokenizers/codecs (LAST, RepCodec) retain semantics better than k-means; interleaved SLMs reuse a single LM for speech+text; IndicSUPERB supplies reproducible SLU baselines and leaderboards across 12 languages; toolchains integrate ESPnet/fairseq/NeMo/SpeechBrain/s3prl and log token/frame rates, bitrates, and streaming schedules \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Representative releases include: ESPnet recipes for encoders, tokenizers, decoders/vocoders, and decoder-only ASR with optional shallow-fusion LMs \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}; SpeechLM toolkits that standardize data prep, pre-training, inference, and evaluation, with ProsodyLM for word-level prosody tokens and ESPnet-SpeechLM covering 15+ tasks \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo}; LLaSO's public model, datasets, and evaluation suite \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere}; OpenS2S for streaming, empathetic interleaved decoding \cite{wang2025opens2sadvancingfullyopensourceendempath}; SimulST/duplex SLM repos with fixed interleaving and read--write policies and first-audio latency under SimulEval \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem}; AV tokenizers (VQ-MAE-AV) and streamable phonetic tokenizers (PAST) for controlled fusion under fixed token budgets \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer}; evaluation harnesses integrating IndicSUPERB, STAB, and fastabx for scalable probes \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}; Encoder--Adaptor--LLM bridges, diarization toolkits, and unified scoring pipelines for replicable ASR/SQA ablations \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}; and community suites expanding discrete-token leaderboards (DASB), speech QA (VoxEval), and audio tasks (Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2) \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}. Ready-to-run tokenizer training (memory-aware k-means), interleaved speech--text pretraining in token space, and mixed-vocabulary duplex stacks should ship with fixed configs/checkpoints, and STITCH-style think-while-talking experiments must publish prompts, seeds, and chunking/scheduling scripts \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}.

\subsection{Config: Reusable building blocks} \label{subsec:Config: Reusable building blocks}
Reusable, declarative configs separate datasets/task templates from model hyperparameters; fix tokenization, prompting, compute, decoding, streaming, and scoring defaults; and encode determinism (seeds, kernels, sharding, exemplar selection) to make few-shot and streaming runs exactly reproducible \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}. Recommended structure:
- Project manifest and task templates: a data.json of corpus paths, sampling rates, language tags, splits; reusable YAMLs for ASR/AST/S2ST/TTS/VC/unit LM/interleaved SLM/KWS/SER/LID and streaming variants across 15+ tasks \cite{tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo}.
- Tokenization block (LM-facing contract): LM-aware or ASR-guided semantic tokenizers (LAST, LM-SPT, RepCodec, dMel) with explicit frame\_rate\_hz, bitrate\_kbps, codebook geometry, segmentation, dedup/aBPE, and upstream features; track quality with STAB and frame-rate/language effects \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.
- Prompting and ICL: enumerated prompt templates, deterministic exemplar selection, contextual biasing lists; multilingual support aligned to IndicSUPERB/SLUE-VoxPopuli \cite{roll2025contextlearningboostsspeechrecognitionhu,hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Training block: resource-aware optimizers/schedules (bf16, grad accumulation), HuBERT-style SSL on 8 GPUs with optional ASR bootstrap and pinned LR/numel packers/seeds \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,roll2025contextlearningboostsspeechrecognitionhu}.
- Inference/streaming: unified segmentation and read--write policies (wait-k/ratio), chunk sizes, decoding knobs (beam/temperature/top-p) with standardized latency reporting (TTFT, first-audio, RTF) for cascaded, textless S2ST, and SpeechLLMs \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}.
- Evaluation/scoring: pinned ASR for WER/CER, BLEU/COMET for text, NISQA/MOS and SIM-O for speech quality/semantics, audio-aware LLM judges with fixed seeds/prompts, fastabx ABX settings, and STAB diagnostics, with URIs/commits/prompts for exact scorer stacks \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis,chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
Config inheritance should lock vetted base strategies (e.g., STAB-validated dMel/semantic tokens; AdamW with 32k warmup to 5e-4, bf16; ~45M numel sampler) while overlays swap datasets/prompts/decoding for ASR/AST/dialog/QA or hierarchical generation; each experiment directory includes the resolved config, data.json snapshot, prompts, exemplar IDs, optimizer/schedule, precision, decoding/streaming settings, and SALMon acoustic-condition metadata \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,wu2023speechgenunlockinggenerativepowerspeechl,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.

\subsection{Corpora: Curation and licensing} \label{subsec:Corpora: Curation and licensing}
Ethical provenance, explicit licenses, reproducible splits, and auditable pipelines are prerequisites for fair comparison. Fieldwork collections like Pangloss provide CC licenses, informed consent, DOIs, and rich metadata enabling factor-isolated ABX diagnostics \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio}. Kathbath, gathered via Karya with maker--checker verification, underpins IndicSUPERB (1,684 h; 12 languages; 1,218 contributors; 203 districts) with standardized WAVs and task-ready splits for ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, and KWS \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Curation checklist:
- Licenses and scope: pin dataset/model/tool licenses, adhere to original splits (e.g., DASB), and publish dataset/model cards detailing uses and composition \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark}.
- Provenance and metadata: document text sources (IndicCorp, LDC-IL, Wikipedia), languages/scripts, sampling/normalization, seeds; release per-language keyword/QbE inventories, OOV/coverage stats; log speaker/microphone/room/genre to support robust splits \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Standardization: resample to 16 kHz mono; pin tokenizers/codecs and SSL layers (PAST, RepCodec, Cont-SPT); release preprocessing, and verify acoustic/content fidelity with SALMon \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.
- Splits and audits: provide language-balanced train/valid/test-known/test-unknown/noisy and streaming subsets; publish de-dup logs and overlap checks to prevent contamination; disclose synthetic supervision recipes and hours by condition \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.
- Benchmarks and eval: release task cards with metrics and judge calibration sets; align with IndicSUPERB, STAB, SALMon, and VoxEval; report stratified CIs and effect sizes \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}.

\subsection{Comparison: Evaluation and testing} \label{subsec:Comparison: Evaluation and testing}
Comparability depends on open suites with fixed toolchains, pinned configs, and statistical reporting. Core pillars: IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU, STAB for tokenizer diagnostics, SALMon for acoustic robustness, VoxEval for speech-in/speech-out QA, and DASB for discrete-token benchmarking under bitrate control; LLaSO-Eval unifies schemas and scorers; TELEVAL fixes decoding/backends for spoken dialogue; fastabx standardizes ABX pipelines \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. Required practices:
- Pin interfaces and schedules: record token/frame rate, bitrate, codec depth/vocab, dedup/aBPE, decoder family/steps, beam/temperature/top-p, chunk sizes, read--write/wait-k; fix audio conditions, I/O modalities, and scoring dimensions \cite{zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,li2025televaldynamicbenchmarkdesignedspokenlan}.
- Rate-aware ablations: sweep rate/bitrate with fixed decoders; report WER/CER/BLEU, MOS/MUSHRA/NISQA/FAD, and identity/style metrics while holding prompting/policies constant \cite{mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}.
- Statistical rigor: paired, distribution-free tests with FDR control; bootstrapped CIs/effect sizes; human--ALLM agreement and judge calibration; forbid post-selection \cite{tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo,chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe}.
- Minimum report: benchmark/task names and splits, corruption/conditions, decoding/schedules, judges/metrics, interface descriptors; supply code links, commit hashes, runnable recipes \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.
- Leaderboards: aggregate across discriminative/generative tasks and bitrate tiers; require seeds, tokenizer/decoding configs, judge specs, I/O schemas, compute metadata; adopt SALMon's likelihood protocol as APIs converge \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.

\subsection{Compute: Budgets and resource table} \label{subsec:Compute: Budgets and resource table}
Transparent compute disclosure normalizes comparisons across tokenizers/codecs, interleaved SLMs, streaming/duplex stacks, and judged evaluations. Report per-stage device type/count, precision, optimizer/schedule, steps/epochs, effective batch size, device-hours, throughput/utilization, and FLOPs or ISO-FLOP equivalents, with wall-clock on named hardware; pin software stacks (PyTorch/Transformers, FSDP/ZeRO, FlashAttention/TensorRT-LLM) and memory tactics; token-reduction (e.g., FastAdaSP) should publish reproducible memory/throughput gains and configs \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}. Resource-card blueprint (one line per stage):
- System/stage and roles: tokenizer/codec (LAST, LM-SPT, dMel) at 6.25--25 Hz; SLM pretraining that reuses a text LM; SFT/alignment; decoder/vocoder; streaming/duplex policy; links to IndicSUPERB, STAB, VoxEval, SALMon, and fastabx \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.
- Data footprint: hours/tokens, sequence lengths, per-language/task breakdowns on Kathbath/IndicSUPERB; curriculum phases/data mixes \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Hardware/precision and kernels: accelerator model$\times$count, bf16/fp16/int8/QLoRA, checkpointing/packing, parallelism, kernel accelerators; report MFU/TFLOPs/s and speedups \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}.
- Optimization: AdamW with warmup/decay, weight decay, gradient clipping; optional OTReg to align speech--text embeddings for cross-dataset generalization \cite{xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}.
- Batching and lengths: micro-batch, accumulation, effective batch; audio durations and token/frame counts; note segmentation width and vocab effects on efficiency \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}.
- Time/utilization/FLOPs and latency: wall-clock, device-hours, throughput, MFU, total FLOPs; RTF and streaming latency for interactive use \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.
- Artifacts: repo URLs, tags/commits, pinned configs, checkpoints, tokenizer files, evaluation scripts, dataset IDs and checksums \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}.
Tracks should scale by budget: small uses modest labeled subsets and FBANK baselines on IndicSUPERB; medium adds SSL (e.g., wav2vec2) and tokenizers (dMel, Cont-SPT); large unlocks full multilingual coverage with SALMon acoustic tests and optional contextual biasing. Include single-/few-GPU recipes (e.g., Slam: 24 h on one GPU; HuBERT on 8 GPUs) with pinned schedulers/decoders, fixed evaluation envelopes, and safety-eval compute/attack budgets (PGD steps, SNRs, judge runs) \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,peri2024speechguardexploringadversarialrobustnes}. Worked exemplars should state hardware/epochs per stage for empathetic alignment (BLSP-Emo), AV tokenization (VQ-MAE-AV), multilingual bridges (Encoder--Adaptor--LLM), and textless dialogue LMs on J-CHAT with dataset links and global statistics \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,nakata2024jchatjapaneselargescalespokendialoguecor}.

















\section{Open Challenges and Future Directions} \label{sec:Open Challenges and Future Directions}

This section distills open problems and concrete paths for streamable, duplex speech--language systems that retain text competence, scale multilingually, and operate under realistic rate--compute budgets across five axes: interfaces that disentangle lexical content from residual acoustics; long-context memory for multi-turn interaction; low-resource and multilingual generalization; alignment to text knowledge without catastrophic forgetting; and a rate--capacity perspective linking token cadence, per-token entropy, and decoder scheduling with evaluation guardrails.

\subsection{Unified vs multi-stream tokens and disentanglement} \label{subsec:Unified vs multi-stream tokens and disentanglement}

Interface choice sets the entropy presented to the semantic planner and the controllability of prosody/speaker identity at a given bitrate, thereby constraining streaming and duplex schedules. Factorized designs split a low-entropy semantic stream from acoustic/codec tokens, enabling conditional decoding that reduces redundancy, preserves controllability at low rates, and supports direct streaming S2ST without ASR$\rightarrow$MT$\rightarrow$TTS cascades or unified LSLMs that interleave segmentation, policy, and translation \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Unified designs remain competitive when decoders are text/prompt-aware and aligned: semantics and speaker/style are pushed into conditioning, permitting ultra-low-rate operation with intelligibility and style retention across ASR, TTS, VC, enhancement, and mixed speech--text generation, albeit with potential residual entanglement inside a single stream \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.

A capability-centric decomposition suggests explicit channels for lexical content ($V_{L}$), affect ($V_{AC}$), contextual dynamics ($V_{CD}$), implicit semantics ($V_{IS}$), and non-verbals (e.g., laughter, backchannels) to expose beyond-semantic control \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Mixed tokenizers that condition acoustics on semantics (e.g., FiLM-style conditioning) reduce cross-stream redundancy and enable multi-level, multi-rate hierarchies aligned to linguistic structure and streaming constraints \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. A pragmatic hybrid keeps lexical progression in a predictable, low-entropy semantic stream and adds a thin, low-bandwidth control plane for style/affect and non-verbals; discrete--continuous mixes and global control vectors instantiate this while preserving editability and duplex responsiveness \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

Bitrate-controlled evaluation should probe: lexical predictability and resynthesis WER/CER (semantic path); speaker/style similarity, zero-shot prompt stability, prosody editability (duration/F0) and non-verbal timing (social cues); and latency/schedule compliance for streaming/duplex operation. Unified systems should audit leakage of tempo/affect into content tokens and quantify the conditioning strength needed to match explicit style channels, while mixed-token systems should report cross-stream redundancy and token-budget reductions from semantic$\rightarrow$acoustic conditioning \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Design guidance: when bandwidth and simplicity dominate, use a unified low-bitrate stream decoded with text/prompt-aware models---achieving intelligible, style-consistent speech at tens of tokens/s with a single promptable interface \cite{zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,jiang2025universalspeechtokenlearninglowbitratene}; when expressivity and social competence are primary, separate $V_{L}$ from $V_{AC}$/$V_{CD}$/$V_{IS}$ and condition acoustics on semantics for rate efficiency \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. A forward path combines both: keep semantics unified and predictable, attach an explicit thin control stream for affect/non-verbals, and leverage text/prompt conditioning in the decoder to restore residual style under sub-kbps budgets.

\subsection{Long context and conversational memory} \label{subsec:Long context and conversational memory}

Duplex agents must reason over minutes of audio and preserve state across turns while remaining streamable. Current stacks highlight windowing and segmentation limits: decoder-only speech LMs stabilize in-context learning at $\approx$4 demonstrations and prefer fixed-length formatting \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}; Encoder--Adaptor--LLM pipelines commonly bound context to ~30 s segments with overlap, constraining cross-turn dependencies \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}; lightweight history already helps (e.g., two-utterance augmentation improves mid-training WER/CER) \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}. Streaming translation depends on punctuation cues and heuristic boundaries, motivating trainable semantic boundary detectors, adaptive delay policies, and joint speech-encoder--LSLM training \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Community evaluation is beginning to reflect longer-audio and multi-audio protocols \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}. These constraints, and the modular remedies they invite, are synthesized in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_13}, which organizes (i) current streaming limitations (few-shot ICL saturation, segmentation and boundary heuristics), (ii) a duplex-aware memory blueprint (small local windows plus sparse, turn-synchronous summaries with prosody-aware compression and joint encoder--LSLM policy alignment), and (iii) a phased curriculum with evaluation and reporting (fixed-window warmup, boundary/delay supervision, distant supervision, progressive unfreezing, and memory-centric probes/metrics). Taken together, the chart maps constraints to design choices and training--evaluation practices for robust, low-latency duplex dialogue.

Aligned with the memory blueprint highlighted in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_13}, a streaming-/duplex-aware memory maintains (i) a small, fast local context for token-level decisions and (ii) sparse, turn-synchronous summaries in a persistent, channel-tagged store so overlaps/interruptions do not erase state. Summaries should be rate-aware, combining variable-rate compression with anchors that preserve prosody/non-verbals; write/evict/retrieve policies are trained with latency objectives and informed by learned boundaries and adaptive delay \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Mechanistically: derive turn-synchronous anchors from streaming representations; keep local windows fixed-length for stable in-context behavior \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}; smooth boundaries via overlap--add or masked streaming schedules; retrieve conditioned on speaker and turn under duplex constraints; and align memory control with streaming policy via joint encoder--LSLM training \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Practical recipes: prepend one--two prior utterances for conversational ASR \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}; for assistants, store channel-tagged turn summaries and retrieve each turn while writing on longer segments but inferring on short windows \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}; for simultaneous translation, replace punctuation triggers with learned boundaries and adaptive delay \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Building on the curriculum and evaluation axis depicted in \autoref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_13}, a phased curriculum introduces memory without destabilizing streaming: start with fixed-length local windows \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}; add supervision for boundary detection and delay control \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}; incorporate distant supervision for memory writes from transcript segmentation or multi-audio alignment \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}; and progressively unfreeze the encoder to manage compute and latency \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. Evaluation tracks should include multi-turn instruction tasks with minute-scale memory probes and multi-source reasoning \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}, recall--lag curves and learned boundary/delay policies under noise \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}, with/without-context ablations reflecting two-utterance gains \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}, and speech in-context scaling beyond four demonstrations with variable-length prompts \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Reporting minimums: segment size/overlap; boundary detector and delay policy; memory write/eviction rates, lookup latency, cache hits; duplex channel tagging/conflict resolution; quality--latency deltas with/without memory; SLM initialization/interleaving; compute budget/allocation; synthetic data and TextLM family; continual-learning stages and retention metrics; forgetting mitigation; and reproducibility metadata \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.
\input{figs/tiny_tree_figure_13}
\subsection{Low-resource and multilingual generalization} \label{subsec:Low-resource and multilingual generalization}

Multilingual robustness hinges on joint advances in interfaces, training, data, and evaluation. Interleaved pretraining is still En/Zh-heavy, limiting cross-lingual coverage and prosodic diversity \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}; expressive S2ST studies urge typologically diverse pairs (including tonal languages) and direct S2ST for unwritten languages \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. Low-rate, streamable, linguistically aligned semantic streams stabilize semantics and reduce supervision pressure across languages \cite{tseng2025tastetextalignedspeechtokenizationembedd}; speech-only routes scale without orthography \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; discrete unit streams transfer cross-lingually but show zero-shot gaps on unseen languages, calling for stronger invariances and adaptation \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Multimodal distillation into residual-quantized latents and multi-teacher alignment reduce ASR dependence and strengthen language-robust content latents under noise and style variability \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}. Adapter-based bridges can deliver competitive conversational WER/CER across 11 languages with thin projectors over frozen encoders/LLMs, and modest multilingual additions yield further gains \cite{li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy}.

A practical curriculum couples: broader multilingual interleaved pretraining beyond En/Zh \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}; speech-only S2ST scaling for unwritten languages \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; and codec/token-side semantic distillation with multi-teacher ensembles \cite{ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}. Promptable, in-context adaptation with frozen backbones and thin multilingual adapters, plus LM-aware tokenization, supports label-lean transfer \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}. Data should prioritize human-recorded multilingual dialogue and expressive S2ST with code-switching, disfluencies, and diverse acoustics, complemented by synthetic instruction data to mitigate distribution shifts \cite{shu2023llasmlargelanguagespeechmodel,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu,ahasan2025dmcodecdistillingmultimodalrepresentatio}.

An actionable blueprint integrates IndicSUPERB for multi-task SLU across 12 Indic languages \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}; LM-aware tokenization (LAST, LM-SPT) aligning speech tokens to a single LM at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}; VoxEval for spoken QA and robustness; SALMon for acoustic awareness; and fastabx for ABX discriminability \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. Interfaces should emit low-rate semantic tokens directly in streaming S2ST and synthesize with an acoustic LM and codec to shorten sequences and improve BLEU/BLASER 2.0 on CVSS-C \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}. Training can warm-start from text LMs, scale to O(10^{12}) interleaved tokens via text-to-token synthesis, and extend speech-only S2ST with multi-teacher alignment \cite{hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Streaming/duplex evaluation should stress adaptive interleaving and barge-in under multilingual noise/overlap, including direct textless S2ST \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}, with targets of improved unseen-language zero-shot accuracy over SVC baselines \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}, lower multilingual streaming WER/CER \cite{li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy}, and expressive, style-preserving S2ST on typologically distant pairs \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.

\subsection{Alignment with text knowledge without forgetting} \label{subsec:Alignment with text knowledge without forgetting}

Warm-starting from a pretrained text LLM and localizing updates to small speech-side adapters or discounted-LoRA while freezing the language core preserves instruction-following and world knowledge during speech alignment; forgetting is better mitigated via experience replay, occasional model merging, and regularizers that maintain the base model's geometry/behavior \cite{hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,lu2024destaenhancingspeechlanguagemodelsthroug,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}. Naive cross-modal finetuning degrades text abilities, and warm starts are insufficient without geometric/behavioral alignment \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}.

Practical levers include LM-aware tokenization (LAST) and tokenizer diagnostics (STAB) that predict downstream quality \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}, chunked think-and-talk decoding (STITCH) for silent reasoning at constant latency \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}, and adapter bridges that enable ASR/AST and zero-shot instruction following with frozen backbones \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}. SpeechRAG shows that aligning speech--text embeddings for ASR-free retrieval/generation can outperform cascades when WER is high \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}. A retention-aware curriculum: Stage A aligns geometry with a speech$\rightarrow$text projector into the frozen LLM embedding space plus KL distillation to the base decoder \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels}; Stage B jointly finetunes speech--text tasks (ASR, ST/S2ST, SQA) interleaved with text-only SFT for rehearsal \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}; Stage C densifies textual conditioning before speech (plan-before-speak) \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}; Stage D deepens on multilingual SLU (IndicSUPERB) and refines tokens with LAST or dMel \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}; Stage E preserves MI between LLM latents and speech units to retain prosody/affect \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Additional tactics include prompt bottlenecks for decoder-only ASR with pseudo-prompt curricula \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}, behavior KL to the base policy \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}, and RL safeguards with KL/hypothesis-similarity constraints when process-based rewards are sparse/ineffective \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}. Evaluation should combine IndicSUPERB, STAB, augmentation-invariant tokenizer checks, and retention audits on text-only tasks \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025augmentationinvariantdiscreterepresentat,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}.

\subsection{Toward a theory of rate vs capacity} \label{subsec:Toward a theory of rate vs capacity}

Treat the interface as an information pipe with load R = r·H bits/s, where r is token cadence and H is per-token entropy after abstraction; decoder capacity B($\theta$,C,S) depends on model size/architecture, compute, and schedule (autoregressive vs masked/grouped vs diffusion). Mismatch yields saturation when R>B, while underuse and unstable codes arise when R$\ll$B or codebooks misalign; higher-information tokens mitigate waste \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}. Mixed speech--text pretraining reveals a semantic knee near $\approx$12.5 Hz for moderate decoders, with smaller models showing modality competition \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Diffusion-backed decoders shift detail into the schedule and maintain DNSMOS/WER as tokens/s shrink via variable acoustic depth \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}; text-aware diffusion reaches low WER and high SIM at 6.25 Hz with both large AR and compact MGM decoders, exposing quality--efficiency contours via 10--32 diffusion steps \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}.

Design pillars: cadence shaping and abstraction alignment to fit R within B at target scale, reducing H through structure-preserving abstraction rather than blunt downsampling; dual-channel apportionment of B across lexical and paralinguistic streams with cross-layer alignment to each stream's entropy budget; and scheduler-level control of bits-per-step (diffusion steps, acoustic depth, multi-token prediction) to keep online R compatible with B under streaming \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. A measurement program should run iso-rate sweeps (vary decoder expressivity/schedule at fixed r), iso-capacity sweeps (vary r at fixed $\theta$/S to locate knees near $\approx$12.5 Hz and validate 6.25 Hz feasibility), and cross-modal competition tests that vary interleaving and size to map interference thresholds; reporting must include R = r·H estimates, schedule descriptors, and downstream quality/latency to place systems on a shared rate--capacity plot \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Actionable defaults: 12.5 Hz for mixed streams with entropy reduction via segmental abstraction/quantization; 6.25 Hz for large/hybrid stacks when tokens are generation-aligned and decoders operate at 10--32 diffusion steps; capacity apportioned across lexical/acoustic channels with abstractions aligned accordingly, using schedule-level knobs to maintain streaming compliance.

\subsection{Trade-offs and limitations} \label{subsec:Trade-offs and limitations}

Choices in interfaces, tokenization, decoders, alignment curricula, and evaluation co-determine quality, latency, robustness, and validity. Thin adapters (~1%/156M) over frozen speech/text foundations bridge modalities and enable zero-shot instruction following; LM-aware tokenization narrows tokenizer--LM mismatch so a single LM handles speech and text; interleaved SLMs initialized from TextLMs scale compute-efficiently relative to textless models, though multilingual disparities remain on IndicSUPERB and demand co-design and smarter compute allocation \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Limitations include: decoder-only ASR sensitivity to CTC-prompt design and capacity ceilings \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}; end-to-end S2ST with a single decoder lagging encoder--decoder S2ST and constrained by channel scheduling \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; unified decoders trailing task experts in ASR/enhancement and being data-hungry \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}; duplex LMs degrading under noise/speaker mismatch without echo cancellation/diarization and lacking standardized full-duplex benchmarks \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}; empathy/capability evaluations relying on synthetic or single-turn judgments \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}; SLM-driven TTS improving prosodic diversity but lagging on intelligibility/speaker consistency and susceptible to ASR-derived biases \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.

Tokenization should disentangle content from speaker/paralinguistics while meeting codec--decoder constraints: dMel provides training-free discretization with parallel decoding \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}; LAST aligns tokens to text-LM objectives \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}; PAST jointly models phonetic/acoustic tokens with a streamable variant \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer}; and SSL-based semantic tokens plus universal vocoders with layer attention enhance OOD robustness \cite{mousavi2024howshouldweextractdiscreteaudiotokenssel}. Language-aware codecs remain speech-only at 24 kHz, lose accuracy beyond four channels, and lack streaming/channel-budget evaluations; online inversion and channel balancing remain open \cite{ji2025languagecodecbridgingdiscreterepresentat}. Text-aligned discretization improves editability but inherits ASR-encoder domain limits and needs co-training with streaming, duration-aware vocoders to close rhythm/prosody gaps \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}.

Paralinguistic grounding benefits from explicit metadata (emotion/prosody) and implicit supervision (QA pairs), improving LLM-judged empathetic QA by 38--46% and enabling contextual biasing that cuts ASR WER by up to ~20% \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund,sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu}; yet metadata can conflict with content and cross-corpus transfer remains fragile \cite{wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. Benchmarks provide compute-efficient, reproducible scaffolds---IndicSUPERB for multilingual SLU breadth \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}, VoxEval for end-to-end spoken QA \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin}, STAB for tokenizer diagnostics \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}, SALMon for acoustic awareness \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}, and fastabx for ABX discriminability \cite{poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}---but pitfalls persist: test-set contamination, over-reliance on ASR-derived proxies insensitive to prosody/spectrum, limited multilingual coverage, and variability from LLM input-length constraints \cite{tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}. Human or audio-aware LLM judges should complement ASR metrics; continuous tokenizers such as Cont-SPT capture prosodic/spectral detail aligned with human MOS and should be benchmarked accordingly \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext}.

Actionable guardrails: standardize on IndicSUPERB and STAB, preserve benchmark assets, and track text-only capabilities before/after each alignment stage \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Stress-test duplex stacks with barge-in and multilingual noise/overlap; compare continuous projectors versus discrete-token conditioning at matched rates and, if discrete lags, increase text rehearsal, apply MI-preserving unit alignment, or route semantics through prompt bottlenecks \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. For ASR, strengthen CTC prompts (e.g., blank-frame removal), provision decoder capacity, and report compression--WER trade-offs \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. For S2ST, ablate codec channel budgets and prompt/context lengths; adopt adaptive channel capping; report streaming latency (average lagging, first-packet delay) and online inversion latency; and compare against textless streaming transducers and IWSLT-style simultaneous ST with LM-aligned tokenizers at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,ouyang2025cmusiwslt2025simultaneousspeechtranslati,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}. Standardize cross-codec comparisons at matched rates/domains/bitrates with public AR/NAR/masked-parallel baselines \cite{li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang}. Expand multilingual coverage beyond English/Es$\leftrightarrow$En and disclose style/prompt regimes; augment human evaluation with ensembles of audio-aware LLM judges and ProsodyLM-style content--prosody metrics, plus SALMon-style acoustic consistency and broader SLU tasks \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Pair capability work with open, multilingual datasets and compute-efficient SSL to promote inclusive and equitable speech technology \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem,fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio}.

















\section{Conclusion} \label{sec:Conclusion}

\subsection{Key takeaways and recommendations} \label{subsec:Key takeaways and recommendations}

\begin{itemize}

  \item Token interfaces and rates
  \begin{itemize}
    \item Default to mixed speech--text modeling at roughly 12.5 Hz to balance semantic fidelity, sequence length, and streaming practicality; always report tokens/s and effective bitrate alongside quality and latency.
    \item Under tight budgets, adopt coarse, text- or syllable-aligned content tokens and route prosody/timbre through a complementary acoustic path; at extreme budgets, 6.25 Hz is feasible only with a text-/prompt-aware decoder and explicit tracking of the reconstruction--generation gap.
    \item With RVQ codecs, reduce acoustic depth per frame before lowering cadence, and condition the acoustic stream on semantics to remove redundancy and improve predictability at a fixed bitrate.
  \end{itemize}

  \item Modeling and decoding
  \begin{itemize}
    \item Pair autoregressive decoding on semantics (or the first RVQ channel) with non-autoregressive refinement on residual channels to cut wall-clock latency while preserving intelligibility; use grouped/multi-token prediction or masked infilling to further shrink steps.
    \item Favor few-step flow/diffusion decoders at low token rates; otherwise rely on codec-native inversion or lightweight vocoders to bound rendering latency.
    \item For duplex agents, inject streaming listening features at every block (middle fusion), expose an explicit interruption token/head, and interleave short text ``plan'' bursts with speech chunks to minimize time-to-first-audio without sacrificing coherence.
  \end{itemize}

  \item Alignment and training
  \begin{itemize}
    \item First bridge speech to a frozen text LLM via a thin projector; then apply constrained LoRA on speech spans and rehearse text-only batches to preserve text competence and limit drift.
